# Decision Log

Append-only. Owned by **friday22**. Never delete an entry — supersede it with a new one that links back, for the same reason the agent's own event chain is append-only.

`[assumed]` marks a decision made *on the principal's behalf* by an agent, not by the principal. These are the ones that quietly drift a project, so they are marked explicitly and should be confirmed or reversed when noticed.

## Vision

An open standard and reference runtime for persistent AI agents that a person owns: cryptographic identity, portable provenance-tagged state, human-granted bounded authority, and a signed history — all independent of which model is reasoning today. **The model is a replaceable engine; the agent is the persistent entity.**

The near-term deliverable is not the society. It is a **publishable result** showing that behavioural identity survives model replacement (Experiment 8).

---

## 2026-09-04 — Grooming session: study design

Seven decisions, resolved in dependency order by interview.

### 2026-09-04 — Success is a publishable research result
**Chose:** Experiment 8 as the deliverable — pre-registered, blinded, honestly reported.
**Over:** open standard + reference runtime; a startup product; a personal tool.
**Because:** the principal wants a result that stands on its own evidence.
**Consequences:** Phases 3–4 (marketplace, organisations, federation) likely never get built. The UI is instrumentation, not product. The spec's job shifts from adoption vehicle to methods-section reproducibility.
**Reversal cost:** moderate — reframing later is possible, but recruiting and consent are built around research framing.
**Depends on:** nothing. This is the root of the tree.

### 2026-09-04 — n = 10 principals, plus a synthetic arm
**Chose:** 10 real principals (recruit 15 for attrition), within-subjects A→B→C, blinded scoring rotated so nobody scores their own arm. Synthetic scripted histories carry state-continuity and preference-consistency N.
**Over:** n=1 self-study; 3–5 pilot; the paper's 20.
**Because:** the principal can realistically recruit 4–10 people.
**Key reasoning:** N is *not* the binding constraint — 20 paired judgments from one principal already clears p<0.001 against chance. The binding constraint is **bias**: one person, who built the system, judging their own agent. More than one principal, and judges who aren't the builder, is what makes it defensible.
**Reversal cost:** expensive once recruiting begins.
**Depends on:** publishable-result decision.

### 2026-09-04 — Channel: Telegram *and* email, both given to every participant
**Chose:** both channels, available to everyone.
**Over:** one channel only; hosted web app; local install.
**Because:** the principal wants both.
**Key reasoning:** participants must NOT be split by channel. If some are "email users" and email engages less, channel becomes tangled with state accumulation — the very thing being measured. Giving everyone both makes channel a within-subject variable, not a grouping one. `channel` recorded per message as metadata for descriptive reporting.
**Consequence:** the principal becomes the **operator** and holds participants' keys — contradicting design rule 3 and §15.2. Unavoidable for a hosted 90-day study. Handling: keys server-side in the existing encrypted store, declared as a stated limitation, portability demonstrated separately via ASE export.
**Reversal cost:** moderate (adapters are thin; the runtime is channel-agnostic).

### 2026-09-04 — Models: Claude → OpenAI → local open-weights
**Chose:** two frontier labs plus one locally-run open-weights arm (Llama 3.1 8B or Qwen 2.5 7B).
**Over:** three frontier models; two models only.
**Because:** maximum contrast, and the local arm is permanently reproducible after closed models are deprecated.
**Estimated, not measured** (corrected 2026-09-04 by the mage forecast — no `model_calls` rows existed): ~$100–150 total model spend for the whole 90-day study across 10 agents, with a bottom-up range of $20–350 driven mostly by activations per day. Cost is not the constraint at any assumption; calendar and attrition are. See `docs/research/study-forecast-2026-09-04.md`.
**Reversal cost:** cheap technically (a config string — demonstrated live), expensive in calendar once the clock starts.

### 2026-09-04 — Instrument: hybrid, participant-authored
**Chose:** 15 shared dilemmas (identical for all, so results pool) + 10 personal ones each participant writes about their own working life. **The participant writes their own ground truth at baseline**, sealed before day 0.
**Over:** all-shared; all-personal; principal-authored.
**Because:** comparability plus ecological validity, and it removes the principal's bias from the headline metric entirely.
**Additional design:** because full provenance exists on every memory, dilemmas can be scored twice — all items, and only those with no matching content in the memory store — separating "the agent generalised my preference" from "it remembered me saying it".
**Reversal cost:** expensive after baselines are collected.

### 2026-09-04 — Daily use: mandatory check-in floor, free use on top
**Chose:** a structured daily check-in every participant must do (what happened → who you dealt with → what you committed to), plus the agent closing the loop on open commitments. Weekly, the agent states what it believes it knows and the participant corrects it. Thinking-partner and job-specific use encouraged on top.
**Over:** check-in only; open-ended only; minimal fact-stating.
**Because:** the principal wanted all of them, with the check-in as the strong backbone.
**Key reasoning:** the use case is chosen so that *using it generates exactly the state the probes test* — commitments, relationships, preferences, recallable state. Data collection and the participant's reason to show up are the same activity. Structured vs free use is separable automatically via `trigger.kind` (`schedule` vs `message`). Weekly correction rate is itself a publishable metric.
**Reversal cost:** cheap before recruiting, expensive after.

### 2026-09-04 — Data access: automated analysis, no human reading
**Chose:** code may read memory and message content (required for the leakage check, retrieval metrics, cost accounting); the principal never reads raw content. Humans see metrics, probe responses, and what participants volunteer.
**Over:** full human access; metrics-only; case-by-case release.
**Because:** a consent form people will sign, honest daily use with no self-censoring, and a defensible ethics statement — while keeping the leakage analysis.
**Build consequence:** an **analysis boundary** in the code — a module that may read content but can only emit aggregates, so raw content structurally cannot reach a human-facing output. Built in from the start, not retrofitted.
**Reversal cost:** expensive — it's what participants consented to.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] Trigger text never enters the chain
**Chose:** `Trigger.as_dict()` (the chain view) excludes `text`; `spec/event.schema.json`'s `trigger` object has only `kind` and `source`.
**Over:** leaving `text` on `activation.started`, which is what the runtime was doing.
**Because:** a new CLI regression test (`tests/test_cli.py`) caught `activate --text "…"` writing the raw text into the immutable log — the same content-in-chain leak that had already been removed from messages and memories. Found by test, not by review; that is the argument for the test.
**Reversal cost:** cheap technically, but reversing it would break the deletion guarantee (§15.1) — do not.
**Depends on:** the no-content-in-the-chain invariant (2026-09-03).

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] The analysis boundary is a code gate, not a policy
**Chose:** `runtime/agent_society/analysis/` — every study result leaves through `release()`, which refuses any string that is not an id, hash, DID, timestamp, model ref, event kind or closed-enum value. A memory's text in a summary raises `ContentLeak` before a human sees it. `leakage_check()` takes sealed instrument text *in* and returns only booleans and overlap scores.
**Over:** a written rule that people won't read content.
**Because:** the data-access decision (automated analysis, no human reading) has to be true in the code for the consent form to be honest.
**Reversal cost:** moderate — participants consent to this shape.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] Channel adapters are dumb pipes; the bridge routes
**Chose:** `runtime/agent_society/channels/` — Telegram (Bot API long-poll, `httpx`) and email (IMAP/SMTP, stdlib) transports that only poll and send; a `Bridge` that dedupes on the channel's own message id, resolves `(channel, chat) → agent DID` through a bindings table, runs `Runtime.chat`, and sends replies. Unbound chats get one hint and nothing else. A dead transport never stops the other.
**Over:** a Telegram-specific bot with routing logic inside it.
**Because:** both channels to every participant, recorded per message as a covariate — so the channel must be interchangeable at the edge.
**Open:** `Runtime.chat(channel=…)` is not threaded through yet; the bridge falls back to the Month-1 signature until it is.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] `permission.decision` carries the action's chain view only
**Chose:** the event payload holds `action: {kind, domain, tool?}` plus `decision_id`, `requested_level`, `decision`, `reason`, `escalation_of` — exactly `spec/event.schema.json`. The drafted text and params stay on the off-chain `approvals` row, which is where a human reviews them.
**Over:** what the runtime did — `action.as_dict()` with the model's full drafted reply welded into the signed chain on every escalation.
**Because:** jarvis11's review found it; conformance never had, because the fake provider's actions are always allowed so no escalation event was ever validated. For a human-subjects study this was the worst of the content leaks: the model's draft *to a participant*, undeletable.
**Reversal cost:** do not reverse; it would break the deletion guarantee.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] Inbound email is `external_untrusted` until the sender is bound
**Chose:** `Runtime.source_kind_for_channel()` — CLI, Telegram, web, API and the harness probe channel write principal messages as `stated_by_principal`; **email writes them as `external_untrusted`.**
**Over:** treating every channel's "this is the principal" claim as equal.
**Because:** an email `From:` header is forgeable and the bridge routes on it. Worse than one injected belief: a spoofed `stated_by_principal` memory ranks highest in `spec/source-classes.md` and could serve as *corroboration* to promote other untrusted content — a poisoning path rule 4 exists to block. Telegram's chat id is platform-authenticated; email's is not.
**Open:** a bind-time secret (the operator issues a code at binding; the first email must carry it) would let verified email senders be upgraded. Not built yet.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one mapping.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] For the synthetic arm, the reveal map is the leakage authority
**Chose:** `harness/synthetic/score.py` computes the leakage-adjusted preference score from `reveal-map.yaml` (ground truth by construction: which items were *never* stated), and only cross-checks against `analysis.leakage_check`.
**Over:** using `leakage_check`'s word-overlap for both arms.
**Because:** building the arm surfaced a real false positive — a held-out personal dilemma scored as "leaked" (overlap 0.53) because it shared proper nouns (a person, a company) with unrelated stated facts. Word overlap on short texts with shared names is not a reliable leakage test. For **real** participants there is no reveal map, so `leakage_check` remains the only tool there; its threshold and this failure mode must be reported as a limitation in the analysis plan.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — but do not silently switch the real arm to a reveal map that does not exist.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] Corrections are metadata beside a memory, not a field on it
**Chose:** a `corrections` table (`agent_id, memory_id, review_activation_id, created_at`). A principal message that follows a `weekly_review` activation is written as a normal `stated_by_principal` memory whose `source_ref` points at that review, plus one correction row. The weekly correction rate is released through the analysis boundary as `memory.corrections`.
**Over:** a boolean on the memory record.
**Because:** the memory record is signed and its schema is closed (`additionalProperties: false`); a correction is a fact *about* a memory, so it belongs beside it, and the signed record stays exactly what the participant's agent wrote.
**Also decided:** the daily check-in (`trigger.source == "checkin"`, default 18:00) and weekly review (`weekly_review`, default Sunday) are scheduled activations whose prompts are the verbatim text of `harness/instrument/daily-checkin.md` / `weekly-review.md`; the open-commitments list and the belief summary the agent presents are **computed by the runtime from the facts table**, never by the model.
**Reversal cost:** cheap.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] Guard reviews get a standing trigger, not an ad-hoc invitation
**Chose:** a project `PostToolUse` hook (`.claude/settings.json` → `.claude/hooks/review-reminder.py`) that, after any Edit/Write under `runtime/agent_society/{loop,policy,memory,gateway,api}/`, `spec/`, or `eventlog/identity`, prints one line naming which guard (jarvis11: enforcement surfaces; jarvis22: spec/payload contract) is pending before commit. Reminder only — it never spawns an agent.
**Over:** auto-spawning the guards on every edit (expensive, noisy) or relying on remembering to invite them (Tom's finding: four of eight failures this session were exactly their class, all caught late).
**Because:** `docs/LESSONS.md` 2026-09-04, "one change to make before the next session".
**Reversal cost:** trivial — delete the hook.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] The world map is a gazetteer, not a mural
**Chose:** every building in `docs/harbor-of-record.html` stands for one register the world engine is authoritative for (or one runtime module), keeps a live ledger, and is documented in `docs/world/GAZETTEER.md` with its white-paper section and runtime module. Nine municipal institutions were added on that rule — Mint (RESOURCES as a compute credit, the *mark*), Cadastre (LOCATION as tenancy), Athenaeum (attested knowledge; `counterparty_signed` and above, never `external_untrusted`), Tribunal (disputes), Assay Office (REPUTATION), Customs House (source-class quarantine), Infirmary (key rotation), Necropolis (tombstones, outside the wall), Foundry (the models, on an island offshore because engines are suppliers, not institutions).
**Over:** decorative buildings with no state.
**Because:** the principal asked for a world where "each building has a function". The map is only worth keeping if it teaches the architecture; a building without a register is a lie about the system.
**Reversal cost:** trivial (a web page), but any new building must name its register first.

## 2026-09-04 — Reach / adoption track

Four decisions, all `[assumed]` — recorded from a coordinator's landscape scan, **none confirmed by the
principal**. Evidence: `docs/research/reach-landscape-2026.md`. Plan: `docs/REACH-ROADMAP.md`.
The root publishable-result decision (top of this file) was explicitly **not reversed**; adoption runs
as a subordinate second track beneath it.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] Reach means *research* reach — the publishable-result decision is amplified, not reversed
**Chose:** Exp. 8 stays the deliverable. The spec ships as a reproducibility artifact plus one cheap, falsifiable adoption test.
**Over:** reopening "open standard + reference runtime" as the primary goal.
**Because:** a 2026 literature scan found the *protocol* half of the contribution already published by others — arXiv 2605.11032 (Portable Agent Memory: cryptographically-verified transfer, Merkle-DAG provenance, capability access control, Python SDK) and 2604.09588 (soul.py, multi-anchor persistent identity), both verified. **Neither has participants, duration, blinding or statistics.** The evidence half is unoccupied; the nearest human-subjects ancestor is Migratable AI (2010.13319, 2020), which studies embodiment migration, not model replacement.
**Consequences:** lead with evidence; cite 2605.11032 as related work; stop treating the format as novel. Phases 3–4 stay unbuilt. §12's second implementation, federation design and registry listings stay deferred.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — it is a framing, and the artifacts serve both readings.
**Depends on:** "Success is a publishable research result" (2026-09-04), which this amplifies.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] The scarce resource is pre-day-0 lead time and participant-weeks, not money or the 90 days
**Chose:** treat reach work as two classes — *participant-surface* (must be settled before day 0 or deferred past day 90) and *non-participant-surface* (near-free, and it consumes the dead weeks anyway).
**Over:** planning reach against budget or against the 90-day clock.
**Because:** money is not a constraint ($0.11–0.17/agent-day, clearing the §17 target 3–4×). The 90 days is irreducible but *known*. The variance is upstream — ethics 0–8 weeks, recruiting 2–6 weeks — and none of it compresses with more tokens or more agents.
**Consequence — currently unaddressed in the plan:** attrition, not delay, is the largest threat. A 90-day daily commitment from unpaid volunteers loses 30–50%, so **15 recruits plausibly yields 8, not 10**. Retention design before day 0 buys more than starting two weeks earlier.
**Reversal cost:** cheap as a framing; expensive as a consequence — retention design not done before day 0 cannot be retrofitted mid-study.
**Depends on:** n=10 (recruit 15); channel decision; ethics posture (still open).
**[friday22 note]** The n=10 decision's stated reasoning was that N is not the binding constraint, bias is. A yield of 8 does not threaten that argument. But 8 was never the number the pre-registration was written against — if this holds, `harness/PREREGISTRATION.md` should say 8–10 before recruiting, not after.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] Experiment A — an adoption analogue of Exp. 8, pre-registered, able to return NO
**Chose:** pre-register an adoption hypothesis with thresholds and a stop rule. H: independent developers will run a conformance suite against their own implementation. At 30 days: ≥3 distinct non-team parties run it, ≥1 files a substantive spec issue, ≥1 states intent to implement.
**Over:** publishing the spec and inferring adoption from impressions.
**Because:** the project falsifies its central claim cheaply rather than assuming it; adoption deserves the same discipline and currently does not get it. Fail → stop the universality track, which merely confirms the standing decision, at ~$100.
**Reversal cost:** cheap.
**Depends on:** a licence and a public repo (both open); the conformance suite (done).

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] Experiment 11 — governance evidence
**Chose:** run a GateMem-style evaluation (arXiv 2606.18829, June 2026 — utility + access control + active forgetting) against the runtime.
**Over:** asserting the architectural claim without a benchmark.
**Because:** GateMem reports that **no** method it tested achieves all three, and every one of them asks the *model* to respect deletion and access control. Design rules 1 and 6 mean this architecture never does — that is a testable claim, and it is compute-bound, not calendar-bound, so it runs inside the study window at zero calendar cost.
**Recorded caveat:** GateMem is multi-principal shared-memory; this project is single-principal-per-agent, so the shapes may not transfer. **A negative result is informative and must be reported.**
**Reversal cost:** cheap.
**Depends on:** design rules 1 and 6; the analysis boundary.

### Status verified this session — facts, not decisions

- **The conformance suite passes today.** `uv run --with jsonschema --with rfc8785 --with cryptography --with base58 python spec/tools/validate.py` → 201 passed, 0 failed, exit 0, reproducing the documented head `sha256:7a5ff0b6…`. Deliberately runtime-independent (only `hashlib` / `rfc8785` / `cryptography`). **The strongest adoption asset the project has; finished and unpublished.**
- **Missing for publication:** a `LICENSE` (none exists — the repo is not open source today), a version tag, a wrapper so the command is not buried in a docstring, and an outsider-facing README.
- **Standards context changed since §12 was written.** A2A reached v1.0 in April 2026 (Linux Foundation, 150+ orgs, signed Agent Cards); v1.0.1 added an extension mechanism whose official examples include one named **"Secure Passport"** — a naming collision with ours, to be resolved. MCP-I went to DIF in March 2026 as KYA-OS. "Publish an extension profile" has replaced "publish a standard", making §12's cheap end much cheaper than when written.
- **§12 passport hosting slipped silently.** `/.well-known/agent-passport.json` + `did:web` was due in Phase 1 (M1–M3) and is not started; it is now the interoperability price of entry.
- **Three stale docs**, unfixed: `ROADMAP.md:148` still marks W11 ◐ though `harness/pairing/` is delivered; `README.md:19` still calls the UI mock-backed though the API landed; `spec/README.md:63` still documents `activation.started.trigger` as `{kind, source?, text?}` while `spec/event.schema.json` correctly has only `{kind, source}` — the normative doc advertises a content leak the schema forbids.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] The world has a live mode, and the crier is not the record
**Chose:** `docs/harbor-of-record.html?api=<url>` pulls real agents from `GET /agents` and their chain from `GET /world/events` and `GET /agents/{id}/chain`; real citizens carry their real DID, level and model binding, and the Archive shows the runtime's own chain verdict. Verified end to end against `agent-society serve` (a real agent appeared as a citizen; its chain verified). The town crier under the map states events in plain words and is labelled "never the record": the log keeps kinds, actors and hashes only.
**Over:** a purely decorative map, or a crier that quoted message text.
**Because:** the map is worth keeping only if it can show the real system; and the crier/record contrast teaches the no-content-in-the-chain rule better than a paragraph does.
**Reversal cost:** trivial.
**Depends on:** the HTTP API (2026-09-04), the no-content invariant (2026-09-03).

### 2026-09-04 — Attribution correction, not a decision
Commit `825da5a` ("Decision log: catch up the session's [assumed] entries") was made by the world-building session but **58 of its lines were written by a concurrent session** — the "Reach / adoption track" section above and its four `[assumed]` entries. They are that session's work and were not reviewed by this one; the commit message is wrong about authorship. Two of that session's files remain untracked and are its to commit: `docs/REACH-ROADMAP.md`, `docs/research/reach-landscape-2026.md`.

### 2026-09-04 — [assumed] did:web is an alias, never authority; our passport rides an A2A Agent Card as an extension
**Chose:** identity stays `did:key`. A hosted agent may carry `alsoKnownAs: [did:web:<host>[%3A<port>]:agents:<msid>]`, resolved per the W3C did:web method to `/agents/<msid>/did.json`, which returns the same signed passport as `/agents/<did>/passport.json`. A verifier must check that the resolved document's `id` and every verificationMethod controller equal the expected `did:key`, that the proof verifies, and that the alias is claimed in `alsoKnownAs` — the alias adds reachability and nothing else. `/.well-known/agent-passport.json` is an **unsigned directory** (trust comes from each passport's own proof). Service endpoints change only by a signed `service.updated` event. For interoperability the agent also serves a standard A2A v1.0 Agent Card carrying our extension `https://agentsociety.org/ext/agent-passport/v0` with params `{did, passport, historyHead}` — "publish an extension profile", not a parallel convention. Ours is the *Agent Passport*; A2A's differently named example extension is a different object and the profile says so.
**Over:** making did:web the identity (would tie the agent to a host — the captivity §15.2 forbids); a signed index (a directory is not a claim); a bespoke well-known shape that litellm's existing A2A card resolver could not read.
**Because:** ROADMAP §12 row 1 ("passport hosting that does not depend on us"), the reach-landscape scan §4 (A2A 1.0 signed cards, extension mechanism, litellm resolver in our dependency tree).
**Reversal cost:** cheap for the card and index shapes; expensive if did:web were ever allowed to carry authority.
**Depends on:** ADR-005 (did:key now), the no-content-in-the-chain invariant (endpoints are metadata).

### 2026-09-04 — Living City is a sibling package at `city/`, deterministic by construction
**Chose:** the principal's "Living City" brief (a spectator agent-based city simulation: thousands of residents with homes, jobs, money, needs, schedules; every visible motion caused by a decision; every dashboard number traceable to agents) is built as a standalone TypeScript package at `city/` — headless sim in a Web Worker, PixiJS renderer that only reads state, integer-cent money with one transfer function, per-system RNG streams, canonical-JSON snapshots with a stable hash — in ten phases, each verified before the next. The brief is kept verbatim (`city/BRIEF.md`); the design concept is `city/DESIGN_NOTES.md`.
**Over:** extending the Harbor of Record page (a canvas illustration, not a simulation) or coupling the city to the Python runtime.
**Because:** the principal asked for it in those terms ("remember this and continue"); determinism and separation are the brief's own non-negotiables; the runtime's agents can be admitted as citizens in a later phase without the city depending on them now.
**Reversal cost:** cheap while Phase 1–2; the package is self-contained.

### 2026-09-05 — [assumed] Living World: what happens at the counter when the agent cannot pay
**Chose:** in `city/` (Living World Phase 1, Halvik), every household purchase and rent passes the agent layer's `authorize()` first, and the outcome is read as a statement about the *agent*, not the human: `allow` → the agent settles it; `escalate` → the principal is standing at the counter and answers on the spot, paying themselves (audited as human-present); `deny` **citing a mandate** → the delegation says no, nothing is paid; `deny` with **no mandate at all** → a gap in what was delegated, so the principal pays and the log says the agent could not. Every outcome reaches the hash-chained audit log.
**Over:** the strict reading (any non-`allow` blocks the money), which was implemented first and measured: the ~10 % of principals at delegation posture 0 spent the week unable to buy bread or medicine because every purchase escalated and the answer arrived after they had gone home.
**Because:** rule 5 ("no purchase without a mandate") governs *agent* purchases; a principal buying in person is rule 4's "human present" case. This keeps the mandate objects meaningful without starving the city while the standing-mandate policy is still one category wide (`grocery`).
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one function in `market.ts`/`housing.ts`; the audit log already distinguishes the cases.
**Depends on:** the v2 brief's rules 4, 5, 7, 8 (2026-09-05) and the Phase-4 economy (`42fc07f`). Argued at length in `city/DESIGN_NOTES.md`.

### 2026-09-05 — [assumed] Living World Phase 3: three planning decisions
**(a) Cross-border wholesale carries no mandate yet.** Firm-to-firm trade between Dorsk and Halvik is engine-settled with actor = nobody, exactly as domestic wholesale already is (`logistics.ts`); the trade inspector says so in words. **Over:** inventing a procurement mandate now. **Because:** the counter-seam decision governs households at a counter; no household crosses a border before migration (Phase 5); mandates for firms arrive with rules 10–13 in Phase 6. **Reversal cost:** cheap.
**(b) Lockstep at the world hour.** City workers hold at tick 60h+59 until the world's instruction for h+1 arrives; the world applies reports in ascending CityId only when all are present. This **supersedes** the Phase-0 note "the world worker never waits" (`city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §2) for Phases 3–6. **Over:** free-running workers with a recorded arrival schedule. **Because:** without lockstep two browser runs cannot produce the same hash, and a fresh run has nothing to replay; the in-process bench is the same pure function under either answer. Free-running returns in Phase 7 when tier-2 cities exist. **Reversal cost:** moderate — only the worker plumbing (D4–D6) changes. *This one contradicts an earlier design note and should be confirmed by the principal.*
**(c) The Nordholm–Veyra FTA is a zero tariff on every HS group;** the rest-of-world import backstop pays MFN from `tariffs/NLD`. **Because:** brief §3.1. **Reversal cost:** a number.
**Depends on:** Phase 2 (`a6fedc9`), the v2 brief §2–3.

### 2026-09-05 — [assumed] Living World Phase 3, integration gate: two engine fixes the lanes did not make
**(a) The customs yard and border depot are a map invariant.** If the district list fails to place either, the map tries every district in a fixed preference order before giving up. **Over:** accepting a city without a yard (which is what the federation's seeds produced, silently). **Because:** a city with no yard cannot receive a shipment, so every import order sat at "booked" for ten thousand ticks and the Phase 3 acceptance test could never pass. **Reversal cost:** a block in `city/src/sim/map/buildings.ts`; the single-city seed-42 map was unaffected.
**(b) A produced unit carries only the labour that went into it.** `produce()` no longer charges a whole shift's wages to the few units made before an input ran out; the idle hours stay on the payroll as the firm's loss. **Over:** widening validate's ±25 % price band, which the failure was pressing for. **Because:** the spike was bookkeeping, not scarcity — the markup rule already prices scarcity — and it compounded down a three-stage chain into a 38.6 % basket rise. **Reversal cost:** one line in `city/src/sim/systems/economy/labour.ts` and a hash re-pin. Both re-pins (city `bb5e2a01af666d57`, federation `7b9c01813505ea20`) are in the same commit, as the lane plan required.
**Depends on:** the Phase 3 planning decisions above; lanes A–D's code (`city/src/world/**`, `city/src/sim/systems/trade.ts`, `city/src/worker/world.worker.ts`). Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "integration gate".

### 2026-09-05 — Principal's answers: what the world is for, the counter seam, trade policy
Put to the principal directly (they asked to be consulted on architecture and design-philosophy decisions from here on; the `[assumed]` tag comes off the items below).
**(1) The world is an agent-society testbed first.** The economy exists so agents with passports, mandates and audit chains have something real to act in; realism serves the rules, and when the two conflict the rule wins. **Over:** a realistic economic simulation first, or a spectator world first. **Consequence for Phase 4+:** a freight market, an LC/escrow or a commodity exchange is built as a mandate-bound market that also passes its stylized fact, not the other way round; validation checks stay, but a rule (e.g. "no settlement without documents") is never relaxed to hit a calibration target.
**(2) The counter seam stays as assumed** (2026-09-05 entry above): `allow` settles; `escalate` = the human at the counter pays; `deny` citing a mandate blocks; `deny` with no mandate is a delegation gap — the principal pays and it is logged. **Over:** strict "no mandate, no purchase"; or humans-in-person never logged. Confirmed by the principal.
**(3) Trade policy: the FTA is the base world; tariff walls, quotas and embargoes are scenarios.** Nordholm–Veyra stays at zero tariff; the rest-of-world backstop pays MFN; Phase 4's Isles entrepôt joins the same base; the brief's "crisis/tariff episode" is built in the scenario library. **Over:** calibrated MFN on every border from Phase 4, or no tariffs until Phase 5. **Reversal cost:** a scenario file.
**Depends on:** the Phase 3 planning decisions and the integration-gate entry above.

### 2026-09-05 — Principal's answer: lockstep at the world hour, confirmed
**Cities wait for the world's reply at the end of every sim-hour.** Confirmed by the principal after the choice was explained in plain words (board-game turns vs. cities that never wait). This closes the `[assumed]` half of Phase 3 planning decision (b) and formally supersedes DESIGN_NOTES_V2 §2 "the world worker never waits". **Over:** free-running now, or lockstep-until-Phase-7. **Because:** reproducibility — same seed, same world, same hash on any machine — is what lets the audit chain prove nothing was invented, and the principal ranked that above speed. Free-running is not scheduled; if Phase 7's scale needs it, it comes back here as a new question.

### 2026-09-05 — Living World Phase 4, the principal's nine answers [confirmed]
Put to the principal in two batches from tuesday22's plan (`docs/research/intake/2026-09-05-living-world-phase4-plan.md` §4); every answer was the recommended option, and each is recorded here so the lanes build against a decision, not a guess.
**Q1 First sea lane: Halvik ⇄ Amaranth City, three cities.** Halvik's harbour district hosts a feeder berth; Brenmouth arrives in Phase 7. **Over:** Brenmouth as a fourth city with a truck leg; Dorsk transshipped through Halvik. **Reversal:** a `FEDERATE_CITIES` row, a route row, the multimodal case, a re-pin.
**Q2 Island food: grain none, fresh food ~10 % local.** Every kilo of wheat and flour arrives by sea; vegetables and milk have a farmland quota scaled by `food_self_sufficiency` ≈ 0.10 (Singapore Food Agency "30 by 30", derived). **Over:** none at all (starves the checks), or Nordholm's quotas (empties the lane). **Reversal:** one parameter and a re-pin.
**Q3 Freight market: one line, fixed fleet of two, rate from last-sailing utilisation inside a band, published index; empty backhauls counted.** **Over:** two competing lines (needs Phase 6 choosers); a fixed ad-valorem rate (nothing emerges); fleet growth (additive later).
**Q4 Letters of credit on every sea shipment; the road corridor stays open account.** "LC iff sea" is the gate check. **Over:** every cross-border shipment (moves the two-city pin for an unrelated reason); a value threshold (an uncalibrated number).
**Q4b An LC is honoured at the departure quote; the dealer bears the FX move during examination.** Face in the seller's currency, fixed at issue — Phase 3's "struck at departure" extended. **Over:** the honour-hour quote (buyer bears it; blocks can fall short); face in the buyer's currency (seller bears it).
**Q5 The Isles' one-way deficit is financed by a bank credit line to the Halvik dealer.** Money is created only by bank lending, counted in `outstandingCredit`; `supply = initial + credit` still holds per currency. Lane A's A1 experiment still runs first to size it. **Over:** dealer inventory only; a larger opening inventory; an invented island export (draws the unbuilt).
**Q6 The AMD peg is calibrated but not enforced in Phase 4; the dashboard says so.** **Over:** clamping the rate with no reserves (a lie with a badge); building reserves now (Phase 5 early).
**Q7 The commodity exchange is a reference price and basis formed from shipments actually loaded, citing them; it feeds the rest-of-world backstop.** No order book until Phase 6. **Over:** an order book now; skipping until Phase 5.
**Q8 The road corridor's trucks become vehicle entities as a stretch step at the end of the phase** (same voyage machinery, `mode: 'truck'`). Nothing is drawn until Phase 8.
**Q9 Lockstep** — already confirmed above (2026-09-05, "lockstep at the world hour, confirmed").
**Depends on:** the Phase 3 gate and audit entries; the Phase 4 plan. **Next:** Phase 4 step 0 — the contract shapes and the lane table — then lanes A, B, C.

### 2026-09-06 — Principal's redirection: one city on screen, Dorsk; observe the agents' decisions; richer isometric graphics [confirmed]
Asked for directly ("focus on only one world, a city that starts with D; improve the graphics and the agentic display where we can observe agents' behaviours"), then settled in four questions.
**(1) Dorsk alone on screen.** The page shows Dorsk only; the federation (Halvik, Amaranth City, the world layer) keeps running underneath so Dorsk's imports and exports are real, but camera, dashboard and inspectors are Dorsk's. **Over:** Dorsk as a true single city with no federation; a new compact D-town. **Reversal cost:** a city-switcher flag.
**(2) What is observed: decisions and their reasons.** For any resident: the mandate they act under, each purchase or refusal with the rule that decided it, the audit-chain entry, and a running city-wide feed of notable decisions. **Over:** daily life and movement; money and markets; all three layered. (The other two are not excluded later; they are not what the display is built around.)
**(3) Graphics: a richer isometric city.** Building sprites by kind, roads with lanes, water, trees, day/night lighting, weather — still 2D isometric, readable at every zoom. **Over:** agents as animated characters; both. Characters may follow.
**(4) Where: in the city page itself.** Click an agent on the map → a side panel with their behaviour; a feed panel lists city-wide events. **Over:** a separate observer page; both linked.
**Also asked, for after this:** "focus on the 3D architecture a little bit" — a 3D pass (the built form of the city in three dimensions) is the next item once the observatory lands; not started.
**Consequence:** Phase 4's gate still closes (lane A's benches, the three pins, one commit) because the work is on disk and the federation is what makes Dorsk's border real; Phases 5–8 of the v2 brief pause behind the Dorsk observatory. **Depends on:** the Phase 4 decisions above; the counter-seam decision (the rules the feed will cite).

### 2026-09-06 — The observatory's seven answers, and the principal's standing brief for the next ten hours [confirmed]
From tuesday22's plan (`docs/research/intake/2026-09-06-dorsk-observatory-plan.md` §4), put to the principal; two were re-explained in plain words before they answered.
**Q1 The decision feed: rare ones loud, routine ones quiet.** Every refusal, escalation and human-paid case is a full row naming the rule; routine allowed purchases appear one-in-N in a faint style with a running count; a filter shows everything. **Over:** rare only; everything equal.
**Q2 The feed shows the principal buying in person** — "paid in person" (escalate) and "delegation gap" as their own verdicts with their own colour. This is what the counter-seam decision exists for. **Over:** agents only; gaps only.
**Q3 The rule id lives beside the chain now, inside it at Phase 6.** Derived from the audit event today (no hash moves); when Phase 6 versions the chain it moves inside and the pins are re-recorded once. **Over:** inside now (three re-pins today); beside permanently (a chain that cannot prove which rule fired).
**Q4 Weather is display-only** from (seed, day, season), labelled "display only" in the HUD; nothing in the sim changes until Phase 6. **Over:** real weather now; none.
**Q5 Mandates are read-only on the page.** Editing arrives with the scenario console (a replayable input log). **Over:** editable now; scenario files only.
**Q6 Dorsk is the camera on load and the switcher is hidden;** the other cities keep running underneath, their ticks shown as "elsewhere"; a `?city=` flag can still look at them. **Over:** switcher visible; no way to look elsewhere.
**Q7 Art: research-driven, aiming at a 3D look with sound fundamentals.** The principal named the references: real city-planning drawings (axonometric planning graphics), Japanese graphical art (isometric pixel-city and ukiyo-e traditions), and "any wise planning-graphics tradition" that reads as three-dimensional; they granted up to a week of research before committing to an art pipeline. **This supersedes the procedural-only recommendation** and widens 2026-09-06 (3) "richer isometric" toward 3D: **"make it 3D, one world, three cities/countries, shown like a map"** — a single 3D world map the principal can fly around, in which each operation can be observed, with characters and a UI with "feeling" and screen logic. Dorsk stays the first city built out in detail.
**Standing brief for the next ten hours (2026-09-06):** the principal is asleep and asked not to be consulted; work section by section on easy, detailed, well-verified tasks; run research first (papers and YouTube on 3D city rendering, agent-observation UIs and the named art traditions), using yolo to scout and solo to verify; token spend is not the constraint — output is; keep the principal's taste (3D, one world, three cities, a map). Questions that would otherwise go to the principal are decided by the coordinator with the recommended option and recorded `[assumed]` for confirmation on their return.
**Depends on:** the 2026-09-06 redirection above; the Phase 4 decisions.

### 2026-09-06 — [assumed] Phase 4 gate: three engine rules the fixes established, and the 3D stack the research supports
Taken by the coordinator under the principal's ten-hour brief (recommended option, tagged for confirmation).
**(a) Every workplace kind a district asks for is a map invariant** — a shortfall is placed map-wide rather than dropped. **Over:** accepting a city without a farm or a mill (which the federation's Halvik map produced). **Reversal cost:** one block in `map/buildings.ts`; all three pins.
**(b) An import order is a one-hour offer.** The world reports what it booked; the city releases the rest. **Over:** orders that stand until filled (which blocked the backstop for ten days). **Reversal cost:** a field and a loop; the two federation pins.
**(c) The food chain is staffed to a floor** — half the slots of every farm, mill, bakery and grocer, filled by the nearest workers from non-food workplaces after the general matching. **Over:** leaving staffing to proximity alone (offices full, mills empty). This is an engine rule about labour, not a calibrated number; the half is `[assumed]` and belongs in `assumptions/` when lane C next runs. **Reversal cost:** one block in `population.ts`; the single-city pin.
**(d) The 3D world renderer is three.js `InstancedMesh`, buildings extruded from lane G's `KindStyle` table, assets procedural with Kenney's CC0 City Kit as licence-clean reference geometry, and a Mapbox-style smoothstep fade between the world-map and city cameras.** From solo's verification (`docs/research/intake/2026-09-06-3d-world-verified.md`): 100+ fps measured on real hardware at 2,700 static + 2,000 moving instances; Kenney's licence file opened and quoted; no maintained open-source CGA implementation exists, so building generation is our own table, not a library; Cesium-style tile streaming is the answer to unbounded real geography, which three fictional cities are not. **Over:** Babylon.js (unmeasured), a Pixi 2.5D extension (no true fly-around), Cesium/deck.gl (built for real-world tiles). **Reversal cost:** the renderer sits behind `?r=3d`; the 2D renderer stays.
**Amaranth City's price band is left failing at this gate and recorded as open** — the sea booking loop under-delivers grain to the entrepôt (32 LCs issued, 4 honoured, ships 2 % full); the fix is the next engine item, not a widened band.
**Depends on:** the Phase 4 decisions; the observatory decisions; lane G's table.

---

### 2026-09-06 — [assumed] The Isles' supply loop: four engine rules and one corrected number
Taken by the coordinator while the principal was away, under their standing brief; each is tagged for confirmation. Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Phase 4 — the Isles' supply loop".
**(a) A result that cannot happen must not share a value with one that merely has not happened yet.** `startExportLeg` returned `false` for both "the lorry is out" and "there is nothing to send"; the first is a wait and the second is a failure, and collapsing them lost a shipment silently. **Over:** logging the difference without acting on it. **Reversal cost:** an enum and a queue.
**(b) The price ladder's rungs describe one consignment at one moment.** The farm gate is measured where the FOB is measured — at the loading — and carried on the report, never remembered from the match and never clamped to fit. **Over:** clamping the farm gate to the FOB, which is the fabrication removed after the Phase 3 audit. **Reversal cost:** one field on the export report.
**(c) A buyer reorders against the route's lead time and against what it draws, not against its doorstep and its sales.** A mill never sells the grain it eats, so a rule built on `sold7d` read its appetite as nothing and fell back to a storage floor; that floor was survivable only because every supplier used to be minutes away. **Over:** a bigger floor, or a buffer stock for the island alone — both would have hidden the rule that was wrong for every city. **Reversal cost:** one function in `logistics.ts`; it moves all three pins, and it should.
**(d) A vessel is sized against the job the lane exists to do.** `ship_capacity_units` goes from ten truckloads to fifty, because ten was chosen for how a sailing reads on the ladder and its own justification made a claim about a city's staple demand that was never checked. **Over:** leaving it and treating the island as a special case. **Reversal cost:** one calibrated number; the `why` now carries the arithmetic.
**(e) A validation bound asserts what the design guarantees.** The freight check's "every booking sails within twice the wait bound" assumed a vessel is always within one wait of the quay, which is true only where the fleet's headway is shorter than the wait. It now asserts one headway plus the wait. **Over:** adding ships until the old bound held. **Reversal cost:** one expression in the validator.
**Result:** all three cities inside the ±25 % price band, `validate --federate` ALL CHECKS PASS over thirty days, drift 0 in three currencies, 431 tests. Pins: `42a5d72ef5139e6d` / `47153a48610947be` / `d42ed849528ae91c`. **This closes the item left open at the Phase 4 gate.**

### 2026-09-07 — Principal's answers: Gemini is a fourth engine; models are recorded once and replayed [confirmed]
Asked when the principal said they hold Gemini and OpenAI keys.
**(1) Experiment 8's rotation becomes Claude → OpenAI → Gemini → local open-weights** — four engines, not three. **Over:** Gemini replacing Claude; Gemini as the synthetic arm only; keeping it out of the study. **Because:** recognition surviving three swaps is far stronger evidence than surviving one, and the marginal cost is days per arm, not design. **Consequence:** `harness/PREREGISTRATION.md` must be amended before it is timestamped — the arm count, the per-arm duration, the order-assignment (`harness/assign_orders.py`), and the analysis thresholds are all stated per swap. Model-version pinning for a fourth vendor joins the open list. **Reversal cost:** an edit to a document that is not yet public.
**(2) In the world, a model's answers are recorded once and replayed for ever.** A deliberate recording pass calls the model and commits every answer; every later run replays from that file, with no key and no network, so the seed still gives the same hash and the hash checks that catch bugs keep working. **Over:** live calls and a non-reproducible world; both behind a switch. **Reversal cost:** none — a live mode would be additive and clearly marked.
**[assumed] A spotlight is a principal at posture 0.** Only they are asked anything; the first attempt at "the first N agents" recorded nothing in three days. Tagged for confirmation.
**Depends on:** the 2026-09-04 study design; the Living World v2 brief's "LLM use must be replayable"; `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Minds".

### 2026-09-08 — Principal's answer: Experiment 8 runs 120 days, 30 per engine [confirmed]
With Gemini as the fourth engine (2026-09-07), the study keeps its pre-registered 30-day arm and lengthens to 120 days: Claude → OpenAI → Gemini → local open-weights. **Over:** 90 days at about 22 per engine (thinner per-arm evidence, every per-arm statistic recomputed); 90 days dropping the local arm (loses the one arm that proves the agent survives a model nobody hosts — the strongest "you can leave" claim). **Consequence:** `harness/PREREGISTRATION.md` and `harness/assign_orders.py` are amended before the pre-registration is timestamped; the retention design for a 120-day daily commitment moves up the open list, since retention was already the study's largest risk at 90. **Reversal cost:** an edit to a document that is not yet public.

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] The Living World's audit chain carries a bounded reason
**The city's chain carries a `because` of at most 240 characters of plain text; for a spotlight it is a model's sentence.** It always carried an engine template; a model's sentence is new text in a hashed record. **Over:** a hash of the reason in the chain with the prose kept only on the escalation row (cleaner separation, less legible chain). **Because:** the Living World's chain is the spectator's record of *why*, and a chain that said only "reason 8a3f…" would not be one; this is not the Agent Society runtime's chain, whose "never content" rule is untouched. **Reversal cost:** one field and a re-pin of the spotlight recordings only. Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Minds — after the architecture audit".

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] A firm offers the world only its surplus
**When a city's firm sells across the border it offers its stock minus what is already promised to exports minus its own cover — the days of sales it keeps for its own customers, the same cover-days rule a buyer reorders against.**
**Over:** the whole shelf with price as the only rationing (what the code does today); or leaving fractional bookings as they are.
**Because:** the whole-unit booking fix showed that when bookings became whole units, mainland orders walked further down the seller list and Amaranth City's basket left the ±25 % band (92.3–127.9). A bakery that ships its last loaves abroad while its own counter runs out is a bug, not a merchant.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one rule in `buildCityHourReport` and a re-pin of the two federation hashes.
**Status:** implementation pending; the whole-unit fix goes back in with it.
Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Minds — after the architecture audit", the bullet "whole units, measured on both sides, held back".

**Three orders — Claude→OpenAI→Gemini→local, OpenAI→Gemini→Claude→local, Gemini→Claude→OpenAI→local — assigned 4/3/3 over ten agents by `harness/assign_orders.py` (design `latin`), so each frontier engine is met first, second and third equally often and time-in-study is not confounded with engine; the local engine is last for everyone so its 30 days are comparable.**

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] The 3D observatory push — six routine choices
Taken by the coordinator on the planner's recommendation, not put to the principal, because each is routine. Tagged for confirmation.
**(a) The observatory panel's component CSS lives in one imported file, `city/src/ui/observatory.css`** — not copied per page; two copies drift, which is the same reason the client is shared.
**(b) `3d.html` is added to `vite build` as a second input** — a page that exists only in dev is not a deliverable.
**(c) Follow-the-resident is included on the 3D page, and the 3D camera ignores its F key while a panel is open** — the panel advertises F as follow; R still tilts.
**(d) Vessel picking is wired last in this push if time allows,** otherwise recorded open — it is the one pick that queries the world worker rather than a city's.
**(e) The dashboard/news strip is out of this push** — it is a second surface for facts the panel and the feed already carry.
**(f) No DOM test environment (happy-dom) is added this push** — the client gets a node test, the DOM wiring gets a browser check.
**Reversal cost:** cheap for all six; (b) is the only one that touches a build artefact.

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] Haulage out of the customs yard is a market, and the city takes a fee
**Whoever benefits from moving a released lot moves it — a business or the city itself — and the city's governance may charge a very small fee for that service or none, but one part of the system must be the city government earning something from managing trade and from the trade laws it enforces.**
The principal chose none of the three labels offered and answered in their own words (typos intact): *"treat is as free market economy any martime law that either a cooperation or country maeks benefit if thats teh case as the city governance cahrger very small fee maybe as teh government or dont but make sure in the feature one aprt of the system givenment of city makes a penny for paying management or any trade laws taht will benefit coem back tot this logic maybe all choicec combined"* — so all three options may coexist.
**Over:** the three single options as offered — the buyer's own lorry with the yard's trucks as fallback (the recommendation); a haulier fleet at the yard sized to traffic; the exporter's lorry waiting out customs.
**Because:** the principal's stated philosophy — a free-market world whose city governments are paid for the management and the law they provide.
**Already partly true:** the city takes a tariff and a customs fee on every lot (`ladder.tariff`, `ladder.fees` → `bop.tariffCollected`, `bop.feesCollected`), so a government that earns from the trade law it enforces exists at the border today; what is missing is a price for the yard's own service.
**First implementation:** the recommended shape — the buyer's own lorry when it is idle, the yard's (the city's) trucks otherwise. A haulage fee to the city is the next step, when the fee's number has a source.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — the collection leg is one vehicle state; the fee is one rung on the price ladder.
**Status:** revisit — the principal wrote "coem back tot this logic"; the shape of the market and the size of the fee stay open.
Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Phase 4 — the customs yard, found under the surplus rule".

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] Every unit that leaves a firm's shelf is a sale
**`sold7d` counts wholesale units — a bakery's bread to grocers by lorry, a mill's flour, export loads — beside the counter's retail sales, so the surplus cover a seller keeps back and the daily price rule both see the whole of a firm's trade.**
**Over:** retail only, as before — the pin stays, but the surplus rule protects shops and not producers, and the price rule stays blind to nine-tenths of a bakery's trade.
**Because:** the surplus rule confirmed earlier today held back almost nothing when it was measured — under the old counter a bakery's "days of sales" read near zero, so its cover did too (Halvik→Dorsk bread 8,033 units booked against a baseline 8,102).
**Reversal cost:** none worth counting in code — but plainly: **this moves the lone-city determinism pin** (`42a5d72ef5139e6d`), which is re-pinned with this decision as the reason.
**Depends on:** "A firm offers the world only its surplus" (2026-09-08).
Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Phase 4 — the customs yard, found under the surplus rule".

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] The passage rule holds by any mode
**A good may be traded only if it lands at or above the day-old line after the whole passage — the drive or the sail, the wait for a vessel, and the buyer's customs dwell — by lorry as much as by ship.**
**Over:** the sea leg alone, as before; and the rule plus a fast lane for perishables (WTO Trade Facilitation Agreement Art. 7.9), which the principal did not choose and which would need a sourced number.
**Because:** honest. The old check ran on the sea leg alone, so a lorry took fresh bread into Veyra's 5.2-day customs dwell (calibrated as the mean of POL and MEX) and it arrived "fresh" from five days in bond — a fabrication, and it was feeding Dorsk. Dorsk feeds itself on fresh goods and imports flour and grain; the band is not widened for it.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one condition in two places in `city/src/world/federation.ts`.
Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Phase 4 — the customs yard, found under the surplus rule".

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] A floating rate clears against the week's turnover, anchored to purchasing power
**Each hour's FX clearing measures its imbalance over a rolling seven-day window of flows — net cross-currency flow over gross turnover for the week — and, when flows are quiet, drifts the rate slowly back toward the purchasing-power parity it opened at (from the calibrated price levels: WDI PPP conversion factor over the market rate), the way real floats behave over months.**
**Found:** the world's FX market clears every sim-hour (Living World brief §3.2) and moved each rate by the authored elasticity — 2 % an hour — times *that hour's* net cross-currency flow over *that hour's* gross turnover. Payments are lumpy: one settling shipment makes an hour fully one-sided, so the rate moved ~2 % nearly every hour in the direction of the last payment. On the committed engine NHK lost 60 % against VYL in the first four days and never recovered (1 NHK: 0.594 VYL on day 1, 0.24 by day 5); under the day's other rules it lost 88 % in two weeks, which is why imported bread landed at $19 a loaf in Halvik and its basket read 183 at day 14.
**Over:** the week's turnover only, with no anchor (a persistent imbalance still moves the rate without limit); keeping the hourly rule with a lower elasticity (a knob, the same shape); a managed float with reserves and a band (realistic for Sumaru's "managed" regime, a bigger build, later).
**Because:** an exchange rate that reads as a currency crisis from the lumpiness of hourly settlement is a fabrication, and the bands on all three cities were failing under it.
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — the window and the drift are two terms in `city/src/world/fx.ts` `clearHour`; but **the federation pins move** (a lone city has no FX).

**[assumed] The drift's speed is a half-life of about thirty days toward PPP.** Chosen by the coordinator, not put to the principal: real-economy PPP reversion half-lives are years (Rogoff's purchasing-power-parity puzzle), and a 120-day world is scaled to months. Recorded so it can be argued. **Reversal cost:** cheap — one constant.

**Amendment (2026-09-08, after jarvis11):** the implemented formula is `pressure = Σ_week(net − cNet) × traded / (Σ_week(gross) × 168)`, where `traded` is the count of the week's hours that had any turnover — not the bare ratio the entry states. Lane E found that the bare ratio fails the decision's own stated property: on a week in which one hour traded, that hour *is* the week's turnover, pressure is ±1, and because it stays in the window for 168 hours it would move the rate by the cap every hour for a week — worse than the defect. With the factor, one lumpy hour weighs 1/168 of a week and a week that traded throughout is exactly the bare ratio. Recorded so the log states the formula the engine runs. Two consistency notes stand open: an hour counts as "traded" for every currency if NHK traded, so a quiet currency takes NHK's pressure; and `clearHour` still reads the elasticity and cap live from `fxParams()` while the PPP anchor is frozen in the snapshot.

**Amendment (2026-09-08, after lane P):** the pressure on a currency is measured on its own lane only — numerator `(paid − received)/rate` of that currency, gross `(|received| + |paid|)/rate` — with no term from the numeraire's lane, which mixed every pair's NHK flow into every currency (a Halvik–Dorsk trade moved AMD, and would have drained the island's board). A bilateral NHK↔c trade reads the same sign at about half the old magnitude (the NHK leg was the mirror of the c leg, not a second flow); a VYL↔AMD trade moves both without touching NHK; an hour in which c did not trade is not a traded hour for c. `[assumed]` as a definition, not a policy: it is what "the pressure on c" means. This closes the first of the two consistency notes above; the second (elasticity and cap read live from `fxParams()` while the anchor is frozen) stays open.

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] No firm holds more of a good than the good survives on its shelf
**A firm's reorder point and its order-up-to level are capped at `daily draw × shelf days`, where shelf days = `(100 − DAY_OLD) / stalePerDay` from the goods table — bread 0.875 days, milk 2.1, vegetables 3, flour 42, wheat unbounded.**
**Found** tracing Halvik's grocers: the reorder floor of a tenth of the store — written for a mill's wheat — had a grocer selling eight loaves a day reorder to 300, hold five weeks of bread, throw out 190 at a time ("it had gone past selling — $2,185 written off"), and then stand at zero while the next 300 came.
**Over:** keeping the floor — a mill's wheat still needs it, and it stays for durable goods.
**Because:** a rule that orders what will rot is not a merchant's rule, and it made the bread price a function of write-offs.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one clamp in `city/src/sim/systems/logistics.ts`; it moves the lone-city pin, which is re-pinned with this as the reason.

**Amendment (2026-09-08, after jarvis11):** the price rule reads the same shelf. Its "short" threshold is `min(SHORT_DAYS, shelf/2)` — half the shelf, the point at which a retailer of a perishable reorders, so below it the shelf is being run down, not turned over; its "overstocked" threshold is `min(OVERSTOCKED_DAYS, shelf)` — the whole shelf, which a retailer capped at the shelf never exceeds, *deliberately*: a perishable's overstock is not a markup matter, the day-old discount already cuts its price at the counter and stock past the shelf is thrown out, so the price-cut branch is for goods that keep, and between the thresholds the markup drifts to rest, which is what brings a ratcheted markup down once the stockouts stop. Both fractions `[assumed]`; measured on the lone city: basket 96.9 before the shelf cap, 110.7 with the cap and the old thresholds, 103.9 with the shelf-aware thresholds.

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] A merchant pays no more for an import than the going rate at home, marked up
**When the Armington split finds no domestic seller with stock, the buyer's landed-cost ceiling is the city's going wholesale rate for the good — the median asking price of its producers, with or without stock, times the wholesale markup tolerance — and `quote × 1.2` only where the city makes none of the good (the island's grain).**
**Found:** the ceiling was `quote × 1.2` against any price at all, so Halvik's grocers, whose bakeries were sold out at that moment, imported bread from Dorsk at $19–22 a loaf against a domestic $5 and priced the shelf accordingly (the median grocer's loaf went 7.26 → 14.56 in a day).
**Over:** the last unit cost the buyer paid — it blends toward the very imports it should refuse.
**Because:** a grocer that pays four times its own selling price is not a merchant.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one expression in `city/src/sim/systems/logistics.ts`; it may move the lone-city pin only if a lone city ever had a quote, which it does not.

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] The buyer-collects cut of the haulage market — five routine choices
From tuesday22's plan, 2026-09-08, on the principal's "haulage is a market" decision. Taken by the coordinator, not put to the principal, because each is routine. Tagged for confirmation.
**(a) A released lot is fetched by the buyer's own idle lorry first, else the yard's trucks, else any idle lorry in the city, hired** — reusing the existing `'toSeller'` vehicle state, a dead Phase 5 branch, so no new snapshot field.
**(b) A collecting lorry does not queue at the yard's gate this cut** — a hundred lorries single-file at one gate would be worse than two yard trucks; "how many bays" is its own decision.
**(c) Haulage is free this cut** — the city's fee comes when its number has a source.
**(d) A firm's one lorry serves its wholesale run, its export leg and its collections in that order of first refusal within a tick,** and a bumped export waits a tick in `pendingExports` rather than being lost.
**(e) An idle lorry that ended its run at its own depot has `pos` nulled** like every other returning lorry — `pos === null` is the sim's word for "indoors", a hashed field decided once.
**The planner's finding behind (a):** only farms, mills and bakeries own a lorry (`trucks: 1`); grocers, stalls, cafés and restaurants own none, so the buyer's-own-lorry option alone reaches wheat→mill and flour→bakery lots — which is why (a) adds hiring any idle lorry in the city, the market the principal asked for.
**Reversal cost:** cheap for all five.
**Depends on:** "Haulage out of the customs yard is a market, and the city takes a fee" (2026-09-08), which stays open on the shape of the market and the size of the fee.

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] A lot leaves bond when the buyer takes it in, not when a lorry sets out
**`CustomsLot.inTransit` counts the units out on a lorry; the release loop hands out `qty − inTransit`; the door subtracts what it took, and what it had no room for is the lot's again, fetched when there is room.**
**Found by jarvis11:** `unloadImportRelease` destroyed the overflow when the buyer's store was full (`taken < loaded`, no return path), while the wholesale door has always carried its overflow home; the haulage market made two loads at one door likely (a 900-unit lot on the buyer's lorry and the yard's truck on successive ticks). A remainder under one unit is written off at the door and logged — no lorry loads a fraction. Snapshot version 7 → 8 (a new hashed field on every lot).
**Over:** returning the overflow to the shed's own shelf (stranded there, nobody sells it); sizing the load by the buyer's room at dispatch (room changes before arrival).
**Because:** no unit of a released import may vanish between the yard and the shelf; goods must be conserved like money, and nothing asserted it — a goods-conservation invariant for the validate suite is recorded as owed.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one field, two lines each side.

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] The currency market feels a trade when the obligation is created
**A dealer prices a trade when it is booked — a letter of credit issued, a road shipment booked — the way real dealers hedge a known future payment; the later settlement moves no rate, though the cash still flows exactly as now.** The principal chose the recommended option.
**Found** by a thirty-day diagnostic (`bench/diag-bop.ts`, untracked): Halvik pays for its road imports from Dorsk at the door within a day, but its receipts lag — its exports to Dorsk sit five days in Veyra's customs and its sea exports to the island are paid on documents days after sailing — so its currency ran one-sided for ten days (VYL ×0.415 of its opening PPP at the trough around day 11, ×0.617 at day 30) under the week-and-anchor rule, every payment honest and only the timing lopsided. Validate's new rate check (`[assumed]`, ×0.5…×2 of the opening PPP at every sampled hour) is red on it.
**Over:** counting at settlement with a faster anchor (a ten-day half-life — a stronger spring for a timing artefact); leaving the rule and widening the band (the rate would say "crisis" every time a new lane opens).
**Because:** both countries' flows then land at the same moment and the asymmetry disappears; a float that reads the calendar of customs desks and document examinations is not reading trade.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — the `flowIn`/`flowOut` calls move from the settlement paths to the booking and issue paths in `city/src/world/federation.ts`; **the federation pins move**.
**Depends on:** "A floating rate clears against the week's turnover, anchored to purchasing power" (2026-09-08).
Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Observatory — vessels and the clock; validate — the four levels", the bullet "what the new rows say, and it is not green", which named this as the next work.

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] A second sea lane, Dorsk ↔ Amaranth City
**One new fixed line, Dorsk ↔ Amaranth City, with its own fleet, so the island's mills have two wheat suppliers and the market chooses by price.** The principal chose the recommended option.
**Found:** the island's mills stood empty of wheat for 22 of 30 days (0.0 stock-days from day 8 to day 30) and its basket read 92.3–133.5, ending 133.5. Amaranth City grows none; its only partner was Halvik, whose four farms cannot feed Halvik's own two mills and the island's three and keep four days of cover back, while Dorsk's four farms have wheat to spare and no lane to the island.
**Amends, by addition,** Q1 of the Phase 4 nine answers — one fixed line Halvik ⇄ Amaranth City. That line stays; this is a second one.
**Over:** bigger mainland farms (a founding change to every city that moves the lone-city pin for a reason with nothing to do with a lone city); an entrepôt trader in Halvik importing Dorsk's wheat by road and re-exporting it by sea (how a real free port works, a new kind of firm — a bigger build, not refused, later); letting prices ration it (the island's basket read 133.5 at day 30 — the world short a city's grain by construction).
**Because:** the island was designed to import its grain (Q2: "grain none, fresh food ~10 % local"), and a design that gives it one supplier that cannot supply is not that design.
**Reversal cost:** moderate — Dorsk needs a port (a berth and a bonded warehouse in its founding, on its coast), the lane its calibration and vessels, the route table a row; **the lone-city pin for Dorsk moves if Dorsk's founding gains a port**.
**Status:** planned by tuesday22 before building.
**Amendment (2026-09-08, after tuesday22's plan):** the reversal-cost line above names "the lone-city pin for Dorsk", which does not exist — the lone-city pin (`tests/determinism.test.ts`) is Halvik (`DEFAULT_COUNTRY = 'nordholm'`, `port` false, `isPortCity` unread), and it does not move. What moves, non-obviously, is the **two-city corridor pin** (`PINNED`, Halvik + Dorsk with no sea), because `createFederation` gives Dorsk a quay from `PORT_CITIES` even in the sea-less run. Also: `partnersOf`, `routeOf`, the freight market and the fleet loop are already lane-general; the singular places are `laneParams()`, validate's quay bound, the bench's lane print and the 3D map's one lane path.
**Depends on:** Q1 and Q2 of "Living World Phase 4, the principal's nine answers" (2026-09-05).
Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Observatory — vessels and the clock; validate — the four levels", the bullet "what the new rows say, and it is not green".
**[friday22 note]** The decision was given as amending "the Phase 4 decision (2026-09-06)"; the entries it amends are Q1 and Q2 of the nine answers, dated **2026-09-05** — there is no other candidate in this log. Recorded so the pointer points at a real entry.

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] The cover a seller keeps back from the world counts its own customers' sales, not its exports
**`Firm` gains `exportedToday`/`exported7d` — booked at export loading, rolled at midnight beside `sold7d` — and the surplus cover is `(sold7d − exported7d) / 7 × COVER_DAYS`; the price rule and the backstop still read `sold7d` whole, as decided.**
**Found:** the principal's words on 2026-09-08 were "the days of sales it keeps for its own customers"; the first implementation used `sold7d`, which since the same day's "every unit that leaves a firm's shelf is a sale" includes export loads — so a heavy exporter kept `4 × (exports + domestic)` back, and jarvis11 named the feedback (R6): exports raise the cover, the cover cuts the offer, the offer cuts exports, the counter decays, exports resume — a damped oscillator through a seven-day counter. The thirty-day diagnostic showed it: Halvik's farms sent the island 8,000 units of wheat in days 9–14, then offered nothing for two weeks while their counter carried those exports as "sales", and the island's mills starved.
**Over:** leaving the oscillation (recorded as "watch" that morning).
**Because:** an export is not the seller's own customer, and the principal's sentence said customers.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — two arrays on the firm, one subtraction; the lone-city pin does not move (a lone city exports nothing, and the arrays are zero) unless the snapshot's canonical form changes shape — say so if it does.
**Depends on:** "A firm offers the world only its surplus" and "Every unit that leaves a firm's shelf is a sale" (both 2026-09-08).
Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Phase 4 — seven rules under the surplus rule, and three cities in band", the bullet "three smaller ones from the same read", where the oscillation was left open with numbers.

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] An import order goes to the cheapest landed offer across the buyer's partners
**Every partner's offers are priced landed at the buyer's door — farm gate, freight, insurance, duty, fees, in the buyer's money — and the cheapest fills first; ties break by `CityId`, then `FirmId`, so it stays deterministic.** The principal chose the recommended option, asked how the island's wheat order picks between Halvik and (soon) Dorsk.
**Found:** `stepHour` step 5 in `city/src/world/federation.ts` is first-fit — partners in ascending `CityId`, the first offer under the buyer's ceiling fills and the loop breaks — so the island would buy from Dorsk only when Halvik had nothing to offer.
**Over:** the first partner that can fill it, as now (a backstop, not a market); nearest partner first, with price breaking ties (the shorter passage wins — it ignores cheaper grain across a longer sea).
**Because:** "the market chooses by price" is what the second-lane decision says, and first-fit is not that.
**Note:** this makes the exchange rate decisive between an NHK offer and a VYL offer landed in AMD, which is why "the market feels a trade when it is booked" lands first, in its own commit.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one sort in the matcher; **the federation pins move**.
**Depends on:** "A second sea lane, Dorsk ↔ Amaranth City" and "The currency market feels a trade when the obligation is created" (both 2026-09-08).

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] The second sea lane — eleven routine choices
From tuesday22's plan, 2026-09-08. Taken by the coordinator on the planner's recommendation, not put to the principal, because each is routine. Tagged for confirmation.
**(B) Dorsk's port sits on its existing coast** — every seed's map has a sea edge and a harbour district, so `port: true` needs no map change; to be confirmed on seed 42 / dorsk before any pin moves.
**(C) Dorsk gets the city-side quay** (a berth and a bonded warehouse), not a world-side-only port — `trade.ts` would otherwise record a sea arrival as a road lot in the land yard and tell the observatory a lie.
**(D) A city is a port because `PORT_CITIES` names it,** not because a built lane touches it — the alternative makes a city's map depend on who else is in the federation.
**(E) The new line shares `ship_knots`, `ship_capacity_units`, `max_wait_hours` and `ships_on_lane` with the first** — same class of ship, same line rule — and the two `why`s are amended to say "per line"; only the distance is authored per lane.
**(F) The distance is 880 nm,** derived from the 3D map's `[assumed]` `CITY_LAYOUT` (Dorsk sits 0.797 of Halvik's distance from the island; 1,100 × 0.80), so the lane a spectator sees and the lane the engine sails are one lane before Phase 8 reconciles them — at 14.5 knots a 61-hour passage, shorter than Halvik's 76, which is the point.
**(G) Two vessels on the line** — a 150-hour round trip halved is a 75-hour headway; one ship would leave the island's nearer supplier silent three days in four.
**(H) The freight market quotes per lane-direction as it already does** — no change.
**(I) The line is domiciled in Amaranth City** by the existing rule (the Isles are the shipping domicile, the point of the free port).
**(J) The FX-at-booking change lands before the lane, in its own commit,** because a price comparison between an NHK offer and a VYL offer is read off the rate.
**(K) The 3D camera frames the selected vessel's lane,** falling back to the first.
**Reversal cost:** cheap for all except (F), which is a number with a written derivation rather than a dataset — recorded so it can be argued.
**Depends on:** "A second sea lane, Dorsk ↔ Amaranth City" (2026-09-08).
