### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] The island's currency is pegged, with reserves that can run out
**A currency board holds AMD at its opening parity — the PPP rate it opened at — by selling the island's reserves against the deficit; the reserves start at a sourced size and drain while the deficit lasts, and when they are gone the peg breaks, the currency floats, and the world says so — in the readout, the validate rows and the chain.** The principal chose the recommended option.
**Found:** with trades priced when booked ("The currency market feels a trade when the obligation is created", same day), VYL holds within ×0.55–×1.04 of its opening purchasing power for a whole month, but AMD falls to ×2.5 — one NHK buys two and a half times the AMD it did — and stays there: Amaranth City imports its grain (14,038 in its own money over the month) and exports little (5,619), and a float prices a persistent deficit every hour, honestly. Its calibration says its regime is a peg (`countries/amaranth/fx_regime = peg`, the Singapore-like archetype), which the Phase 4 answer left floating for now.
**Over:** floating and giving the island what a free port earns — transshipment, goods landed in bond re-exported without duty, by an entrepôt trader or by the matcher offering bonded stock (the bigger build, not refused, later); floating and accepting it (the rate check stays red for AMD, recorded as expected for a deficit economy — honest and harsh).
**Because:** it is realistic for a free port; it is a bounded build (a reserve stock in the numeraire, one intervention rule in `clearHour`, a snapshot field); and it turns the deficit into a visible countdown rather than a price.
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — the intervention is one branch per pegged currency in `city/src/world/fx.ts`; **the federation pins move**.
**Depends on:** "The currency market feels a trade when the obligation is created" and "A floating rate clears against the week's turnover, anchored to purchasing power" (both 2026-09-08); the numbers were read off the world with "A second sea lane, Dorsk ↔ Amaranth City" (2026-09-08) in it.
**[assumed]** Two things beneath the decision, taken on the recommendation and not put to the principal:
- **The reserves' opening size is three months of the island's imports at the opening rate** — the IMF's traditional reserve-adequacy metric, three months of import cover, applied to the world's first month of imports as measured. A sourced rule of thumb carrying its `why`, not a dataset value.
- **A broken peg does not re-peg on its own.** Once the reserves are gone the currency floats and stays floating; a re-peg is a policy decision the world does not take by itself.
**[friday22 note]** The decision names "the Phase 4 answer (2026-09-06, 'the peg floats, say so')"; the entry it means is **Q6 of "Living World Phase 4, the principal's nine answers" (2026-09-05)** — "The AMD peg is calibrated but not enforced in Phase 4; the dashboard says so" — whose rejected option, "building reserves now (Phase 5 early)", is exactly what this decision now does. This entry supersedes Q6. It also takes up what the float rule set aside on 2026-09-08 — "a managed float with reserves and a band … a bigger build, later" — for the pegged currency only; Sumaru's managed regime is still not built.

**Amendment (2026-09-08, after lane P):** the reserves are sized at creation from the world's own grain need — not from a measurement, and not from the calibrated nation's macro. `population (assumptions/trade_fx/reserve_city_population, a declared fallback of 2,000 naming the city roster as its dataset) × bread per person per week (assumptions/diet_amaranth/bread_loaves_per_person_per_week, the same key the cupboard rules draw on) ÷ 7 × kilograms of wheat per loaf (walked through the recipes: 0.72 kg flour a loaf × 1.32 kg wheat a kilo of flour) × the world price of wheat (the goods table's 30 cents a kilo) × 90 days (assumptions/trade_fx/reserve_months_of_import_cover = 3 × 30) ÷ the opening PPP` = **1,662,343 NHK cents**. Lane P's first cut sized it from the calibrated national macro (GDP per capita PPP × imports share of GDP) and got 15.8 billion cents — faithful to the IMF rule and to the nation's numbers, and four orders larger than the trade this world actually does, so the peg could never break; the base is now the world's grain, which is what the island imports by design. **Known approximation, stated in the code:** the world price is the catalogue's at price level 1 and each city trades at its own level (`wageScale`), which the world layer cannot read before the cities exist — so against the island's measured monthly bill (~19,900 NHK) the board holds about five weeks of cover, not three months; the fix is a world price published per country in `calibration/`, recorded as owed. This amends the first `[assumed]` above.

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] The mainland's farmland is sized to the world's mouths — and land policy is not fixed
**The founding gives Halvik and Dorsk wheat acreage for their own people *plus the island's share* — the island's grain need is already known from the diet chain the currency board was sized with — written as a calibrated rule (farms per thousand mouths fed) carrying its `why`, not as a number.** The principal chose the recommended option, asked how the world should grow enough grain, **and added a condition, in their own words:** "go with recomnend answer but do not forget each land policy and useage coudl change beavsue we dont know yet how a scoity of agents cna perform".
**The condition, as a rule:** land policy and land use are **not fixed facts of the founding**. How much a city farms, and for whom, is a *policy* — a per-city land-use record the founding reads and a later phase can rewrite — never a constant baked into the map. Nobody yet knows how a society of agents performs, so the sizing must be legible and changeable by that society when it arrives.
**Found:** the world grows less than three cities eat. Halvik's four wheat farms and Dorsk's four feed two mainland cities; the island grows none and imports by design. With two lanes and the cheapest-landed market the shortage moved rather than ended — the island's mills still empty, wheat 0.0 stock-days from day 11, and by day 28 Dorsk's bakeries short, bread 0.0, its basket ending 125.6.
**Over:** the island growing some of its own (the ~10 % fresh-local share — the island's map gains farms it was designed not to have); raising every farm's yield (a calibrated yield would be the honest source, but it changes every city's food economy at once); leaving it to prices (two cities at the edge of the band at month's end, the island's mills empty).
**Because:** the world was short a city's grain by construction, and honest rules only moved the shortage.
**Reversal cost:** moderate — the founding record changes and each mainland city founds more farms, so **the lone-city pin moves**; the policy's changeability is the point, so a later society rewriting the land use is the design working, not a reversal.
**Depends on:** "A second sea lane, Dorsk ↔ Amaranth City", "An import order goes to the cheapest landed offer across the buyer's partners", and "The island's currency is pegged, with reserves that can run out" — whose amendment walked the same diet chain the sizing reads (all 2026-09-08).
**Status:** planned by tuesday22 before building.
Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Phase 4 — the second sea lane, the cheapest landed offer, and the map that shows it", the bullets "What it reads now" and "Open — the island's wheat cover is still 0.0 from day 11, and the shortage has moved to Dorsk".

**Amendment (2026-09-08, after tuesday22's measurement):** the premise the question was asked on was wrong — the world is not short of grain. A wheat farm feeds ~9,150 mouths (recipe 60 kg per worker-hour, 8 slots, measured 1,740 kg a day staffed as the city staffs it), and the mainland's eight farms grow roughly 11× what three cities eat. The grain was going to a fictional buyer — the rest-of-the-world account of the Phase 1 surplus dump ("The federation is the world — there is no rest of the world beyond it", same day). Sized to mouths, Halvik and Dorsk would found **fewer** farms — four each down to about two each, with a cover multiplier and a floor — not more. The decision stands as a design: a per-city land-use record the founding reads and a later society can rewrite, per the principal's condition. But its first application is a reduction, and it is deferred until the closed world has been measured for a month, so the policy is sized against real demand and not against the loop. The principal's condition is unchanged and is the reason the record exists at all.

**Amendment (2026-09-09, the principal's answer):** the deferral above ends. The first application — a reduction, about four farms a city down to about two, farms per thousand mouths fed including the island's share, a cover multiplier and a floor — lands now, folded into the founding-to-customers lane ("The farmland reduction folds into this lane", 2026-09-09, below): the same record, the same shape of rule as the rest of the sizing, one pin move instead of two. **Status now:** building in that lane; the record stays rewritable by a later society, per the principal's condition, which is unchanged.

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] A line sails to demand
**A vessel with nothing booked at this end and bookings waiting at the other sails at once, empty, instead of waiting out `max_wait_hours`; the wait bound applies only where there is cargo to wait for.** The principal chose the recommended option.
**Found:** a vessel waited up to two days at either end for cargo before sailing; at the island there is little to carry home, so a ship sat two days for a backhaul that would not come while grain queued on the far quay. Bookings waited a mean 29 hours for the Dorsk line, and each line sailed only three times in twenty days.
**No new number:** the headway roughly halves and the empty-backhaul count is the honest cost, already reported.
**Over:** a fixed timetable (a liner schedule — N sailings is a knob unless it is derived from the round trip); as now (the freight index already halved on every direction, the market saying the fleet is large for the trade).
**Because:** a ship that waits for cargo that does not exist while cargo waits for it is not a line.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one condition in the loader (`city/src/world/shipping/vessels.ts` / the vessel loop in `federation.ts`); **the federation pins move**.
**Depends on:** "A second sea lane, Dorsk ↔ Amaranth City" and "[assumed] The second sea lane — eleven routine choices" (E), which shares `max_wait_hours` per line (both 2026-09-08).
Argued in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 "Phase 4 — the second sea lane, the cheapest landed offer, and the map that shows it", the bullet "Open — the island's wheat cover is still 0.0 from day 11, and the shortage has moved to Dorsk".

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] The federation is the world — there is no rest of the world beyond it
**In a federation the surplus dump is gone: a producer's surplus is offered to partners under the surplus rule, and a producer whose store is full stops making until it sells — production demand-bound, as in any closed economy. The import backstop — buying from "abroad" when a shelf is empty — goes the same way for federated cities. A lone city keeps both devices as its Phase 1 testbed.** The principal chose the recommended option.
**Found** (tuesday22's measurement while planning the land policy, then the coordinator's thirty-day trace of a lone Halvik)**:** under the supposed grain shortage was a Phase 1 device — every producer with more than 40 % of its store sells the surplus to a rest-of-the-world account at world price (`exportSurplus`, 05:00), and bakes or mills at full capacity regardless of what the city eats. The city eats ~380 kg of wheat a day; the mills sell ~1,800 kg of flour a day at home to bakeries baking at capacity and dump ~3,000 abroad, drawing ~6,800 kg of wheat a day from farms that run to zero from day 6 — while one wheat farm feeds about 9,150 mouths and the mainland's eight grow roughly 11× what three cities eat. In the federation the fictional buyer outbid the island.
**Over:** a rest of the world that pays less than any partner and only after partners were offered first (keeps the small-open-economy device, but the discount is a new number); as now — a bottomless buyer at world price in every city, which is why the island starved next to a mainland growing eleven times what it eats.
**Because:** a world whose surplus vanishes into a buyer that is not in it cannot show where its grain goes, and the whole day's chain of rules sat on that.
**Reversal cost:** moderate — the two devices gated on a `federated` founding flag (a snapshot field), and production paused at a full store; **the federation pins move**, the lone-city pin does not (its devices stay).
**Depends on:** "A firm offers the world only its surplus" (2026-09-08), whose surplus rule now carries the whole of a federated producer's outward trade; and the entry below, which made the export visible as an export.

**Amendment (2026-09-08, after lane R and jarvis11):** (a) the lone-city pin *does* move — by the snapshot's bytes alone (`"federated":false` and the version, 19 bytes; basket 98.2 before and after; the reversal-cost line above said it would not) — because a snapshot field is hashed and not snapshotting the flag would restore a federated city as a small open economy; (b) there is a **third** rest-of-world device the decision did not name and that is NOT gated: `settleDay` in `city/src/sim/systems/economy/labour.ts` bills the `external` account every morning for a service firm's hours at its `exportMargin` (offices, workshops, sawmills, warehouses, the customs infrastructure itself) — about a week's private wage bill a week, into an account opened with a year of it and allowed to go negative for ever; it sits inside the ledger's conservation identity so no check can see it, and it is those firms' entire income, so gating it alone would stop every wage in them and then every shop. It stays until service firms have a domestic or partner customer; recorded as **open**, with the ordering constraint (a customer first, then the foreign one goes), and it is the next question for the principal; (c) `dailyDraw` nets out a lone city's *dump* only (`dumped7d`, snapshot v11), not every export — a federated mill does mill wheat for the flour it ships; the `[assumed]` loop entry below is amended accordingly.

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] A surplus dumped abroad is an export, not a customer, and a firm's draw of an input is sized to what its own customers buy
**`exportSurplus` books `exportedToday` beside `soldToday`, and `dailyDraw` reads `sold7d − exported7d` for both the good itself and the output it makes — the same subtraction the seller's cover already makes.** Taken by the coordinator on 2026-09-08 from tuesday22's finding, before the decision above and independent of it.
**Found:** without it a mill's wheat appetite read its own dumped flour as demand, and its reorder point outran its store — 11,700 kg against a 6,000 kg store by day 21, drawing 2,938 kg a day and climbing.
**Moves the lone-city pin** (`923adf3bb75b62f1` → `bb54cd9b4e4ba1d4`; basket 98.2 before and after). Validate's stock-days now read the engine's own `dailyDraw`, exported for the reading, instead of a copy that had already drifted.
**Reversal cost:** cheap.
**Depends on:** "[assumed] The cover a seller keeps back from the world counts its own customers' sales, not its exports" (2026-09-08) — the same subtraction, applied to the draw.

**Amendment (2026-09-08, after jarvis11):** the first cut netted `exported7d` — every export — out of the draw, which was right for a lone city (its only export is the dump) and wrong for a federated mill whose exports are real partner orders bounded by a partner's want. `Firm` now carries `dumpedToday`/`dumped7d`, written only by `exportSurplus`; the draw nets the dump; the seller's cover still nets every export, as decided. Snapshot 10 → 11; the lone-city pin `745f34cacccdb869` → `06173ff0eeec4458` for the shape alone.

### 2026-09-08 — [assumed] A repositioning leg is a sailing and an empty one, and moves no freight rate
**Under "A line sails to demand", a vessel that leaves empty to meet demand across is tagged `Voyage.toDemand`; it counts in the sailings and in the empty count (the decision's stated cost, kept honest) but does not push the freight index's load-factor rule.** Taken by the coordinator on 2026-09-08 from jarvis11's lane S.
**Found:** the light direction was reaching the rate floor twice as fast for a policy reason, not a demand fact, and the same imbalance was already priced once as the loaded direction's low load factor.
**Also:** `nextSailing` publishes the hour an empty hull with demand across will actually leave, not its bound, so the "next departure" tile is not a promise the world breaks.
**Over:** recording every departure as a reading (as before).
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one boolean on a voyage, one argument to `recordSailing`.
**Depends on:** "A line sails to demand" (2026-09-08).

### 2026-09-08 — [confirmed] A service firm's customers are real — firms and households in the world — and the foreign bill goes when they exist
**Build a service market: firms pay other firms for hours and space (an office does a bakery's books, a warehouse stores a mill's flour, a workshop repairs a lorry), households keep buying the retail services they already buy, and a service crosses a border by the same ladder as a good. When a service firm has real customers, the foreign bill is gated off in a federated city like the other two devices. Until the market lands, the foreign bill stays and its size is printed on the dashboard as the last fiction in the world.** The principal chose the recommended option, after asking for the device to be explained in plain words.
**The device, in plain words:** about a third of every city's firms — offices, workshops, sawmills, warehouses, the pharmacy, the customs yard — sell nothing inside the city and earn their entire income by billing the "rest of the world" account every morning for their hours at an export margin. The account started with a year of the city's wages and may go below zero for ever; it is inside the conservation identity, so no check sees it. It finances the wages that buy the bread, so gating it alone would stop those wages and then every shop.
**Over:** keeping it and making it symmetric — the city earns from beyond the map with an equal outflow (foreign spending, or a levy the city government keeps), a bounded two-way stream, still an invisible buyer; cutting it now and letting those firms close (honest at once, a collapse at once).
**Because:** a closed world with a third of its wages paid by a customer nobody can see, buy from or exhaust is not closed.
**Reversal cost:** this is a build of its own — a service market: demand for services by firms and households, a price rule, a ladder for cross-border services; the gate is one branch once it exists.
**Depends on:** the amendment (b) to "The federation is the world — there is no rest of the world beyond it" (2026-09-08), which named this third device, left it ungated, and recorded the ordering constraint — a customer first, then the foreign one goes. This entry closes that open question.
**Status:** planned by tuesday22 before building; the dashboard row first.

**Amendment (2026-09-09, after tuesday22's plan and the coordinator's measurement):** the service market this entry ordered is **superseded**. tuesday22 planned it (2026-09-09) with a threshold of its own — the plan stands if the wages of the bill-fed kinds are no more than 0.25 of the wages of every private firm with a real customer put together. The coordinator then measured (context below) and read 3.5× to 6.5×: the plan's own test failed by 14×–26×, and the domestic customers that exist could pay for roughly a tenth of the hours. The principal chose founding-to-customers over a market for hours nobody can buy ("Firms are founded to their customers", 2026-09-09, below). The first step was done as ordered: lane B2's boundary inventory — `economy.boundary` (snapshot 12), a row per city in validate with an `unaccounted` residual, and a `foreign bill` tile. **Status now:** the sizing rule is the next lane, to be planned by tuesday22; the bill goes at founding in federated cities when the sizing lands ("The foreign bill stops at founding, no transition", 2026-09-09); the row stays as the standing inventory, reading zero. The principle of this entry — a customer first, then the foreign one goes — stands; only the way of getting a customer changed.

### 2026-09-09 — The foreign bill, measured — context, not a decision
The coordinator ran `city/bench/diag-foreign-bill.ts` (untracked) over thirty federated days, seed 42. **The 06:00 foreign service bill is 84 % of all private revenue in Halvik, 77 % in Dorsk, 88 % in Amaranth City.** Eighteen offices per city — 70 slots each, 2,650 cents an hour, placed by the district quota in `city/src/sim/map/buildings.ts` — carry most of it; workshops, warehouses, forestry camps and depots the rest. Four shops also carry it — pharmacy, clothing shop, hardware store, fish market — and are paid twice for the same hours, once at the shelf and once by the world; without the world they would earn about a third of their wages. The wages of the bill-fed kinds are 3.5× to 6.5× the wages of every private firm with a real customer put together; tuesday22's own threshold for "the plan stands" was 0.25. Lane B2's boundary row reads the same in wage terms: 58.1 % / 44.5 % / 64.7 % of the private wage bill, `unaccounted` 0.00 a day. The calibration has no employment-by-sector or input–output table; the worldkit scaffolds for them exist and are unimplemented by design (`city/worldkit/worldkit/loaders/{icio,ilostat,firms,consumption}.py`); yolo's lead sheet for the datasets is `docs/research/intake/2026-09-09-sector-employment-datasets.md`. The four decisions below were put to the principal on this reading, recommendation first in every case.

---

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] Firms are founded to their customers
**At founding a firm exists only if the calibration can name who pays it — households by budget share, other firms by what they buy, the treasury by its budget. Premises nobody can support stand vacant, ready for agents to found firms in later. Workers go where the calibrated employment structure puts them; the rest are unemployed as the real rate says. A sizing rule a society can rewrite — the same shape as the land policy.** The principal chose the recommended option.
**Found:** the context above — a service sector whose hours nobody in the world buys, when the domestic customers that exist could pay for roughly a tenth of them.
**Over:** keeping the 18 offices and giving them a customer by budget (closes about a tenth of the gap); reopening the world for services only (reverses "The federation is the world" for one flow).
**Because:** a firm the world cannot name a payer for is not a firm; the count of firms is a sizing to be derived, as acreage is, not a quota to be filled.
**Reversal cost:** moderate — the founding reads an employment structure the calibration does not yet have (the worldkit loaders above, or a declared fallback carrying its `why`), and the district quota in `buildings.ts` becomes a count of premises, not of firms; **every federation pin moves**. A later society rewriting the sizing is the design working, not a reversal — the principal's land condition, applied here.
**Depends on:** "The mainland's farmland is sized to the world's mouths — and land policy is not fixed" (2026-09-08), the same shape of rule, whose amendment also found that the first application was a reduction; "The federation is the world" (2026-09-08); the service-bill entry above, whose market this supersedes and whose ordering it keeps.
**Status (updated 2026-09-09, a fourth time; was "landed in the working tree, being measured and committed", before that "planned by tuesday22; building in three lanes", before that "the sizing rule is the next lane, to be planned by tuesday22 before building"):** **landed and committed 2026-09-09; the three pins moved (lone city `4873830dae251aa9`, two-city `f65c078efbf46f4f`, three-city `f101351e1f8a05bf`); 598 tests.** The three lanes that landed — **the sizing engine** (the record, `sizing.ts`, vacant premises, the gate), **the payers** (household weekly bills, the firms' services bill to an office, the customs infrastructure paid by customs — superseded the same day, civic now; the amendment to (A) of "four routine choices", below), and **the worldkit assumption groups** (employment structure, consumption shares, value-added shares, with badged fallbacks citing solo's verified sources at `docs/research/2026-09-09-sector-employment-datasets-verified.md`). The four `[confirmed]` entries and the three `[assumed]` entries after "The town founded to its customers, measured" (2026-09-09, below) are this lane's decisions; the second and third `[assumed]` blocks record what landing and jarvis11's read changed, and the "Open after the sizing landed" note after them what is still undecided. **Status, the same evening:** every governance question the founding raised is now answered — the fiscal anchor, the deficit (a hard budget, no borrowing), the rents (reprice to vacancy, slowly), the restore rule, the border sheds: the five `[confirmed]` entries after "Open after the sizing landed", below. What remains open is engine work — (i), (v), (vii)–(xii) — and the idle-hours question, (ii), the principal's.
**Not decided (as written this morning):** whether the lone city is also founded to its customers, or keeps the quota founding so the Phase 1 device has something to bill. The entry below keeps the device in the lone city; it does not say what the lone city founds. **Answered the same day, below:** "The lone city is founded to its customers too" (2026-09-09) — one rule, one code path.

---

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The foreign bill stops at founding, no transition
**With firms founded to their customers, the closed world is founded closed: no service firm in a federated city ever bills the world. No gate, no flag day, nothing to unwind. The lone city keeps the device as Phase 1's testbed.** The principal chose the recommended option.
**Over:** tuesday22's per-firm gate (a firm stops billing when its own 30-day revenue covers its 30-day wages); a flag day after N days. Both are transitions off a bill that, founded to customers, never starts.
**Because:** a world founded without the bill has nothing to be weaned from.
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — `settleDay` in `city/src/sim/systems/economy/labour.ts` gated on `federated` like the other two devices; it lands *with* the sizing and not before, because until then the bill is those firms' whole income (amendment (b) to "The federation is the world").
**Depends on:** "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09); "The federation is the world" and its amendment (b) (2026-09-08), whose ordering constraint — a customer first, then the foreign one goes — this meets by construction.

---

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The twice-paid shops keep both payments until the sizing lands
**Pharmacy, clothing shop, hardware store and fish market stay on both payments — the shelf and the world — until firms are founded to their customers; then the world's payment goes with the rest.** The principal did **not** take the recommendation.
**Recommended:** paid once, by their customers, now — and cut in number if the shelf cannot cover their wages.
**Chose:** keep both until the sizing lands.
**Because:** the principal's reason — it avoids two pin moves and one more step of the fiction built only to be torn down.
**Over:** the recommendation, which would have made four honest shops sooner at the price of a pin move the sizing would move again.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — nothing is built; the shops are left as they are.
**Depends on:** "Firms are founded to their customers" and "The foreign bill stops at founding, no transition" (both 2026-09-09).
**[friday22 note]** Until the sizing lands, these four shops' shelf sales cover about a third of their wages and the world pays the rest; any reading of retail in the three cities should say so. The boundary row carries the amount.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] Kinds with no buyer are not founded until one exists
**The textile mill comes when cloth is a good the clothing shop buys; the sawmill, brickworks and forestry camp when construction exists; the workshop when things wear out. Their premises stand vacant until then.** The principal chose the recommended option.
**Over:** founding them now with new goods (cloth first — a second pin move); keeping them selling hours abroad as a named exception (a hole in "The federation is the world", with a name on it).
**Because:** the founding rule, applied to kinds — a firm exists when someone pays it, a good when someone buys it.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — vacant premises are the map's, and founding a kind later is the design.
**Depends on:** "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09); "The federation is the world" (2026-09-08).

### 2026-09-09 — [assumed] The boundary inventory's share is read against the founding private weekly payroll
**Lane B2's `economy.boundary` row reports the foreign bill as a share of the founding private weekly payroll — a fixed yardstick set when the city is founded — not of the live wage bill.** Taken by the coordinator on 2026-09-09.
**Because:** a fixed denominator reads the same across days and seeds; a live one moves with the thing it measures.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one denominator in validate.
**Revisit if a city grows:** a fixed yardstick understates the share of a payroll that has outgrown its founding.
**Depends on:** the service-bill entry (2026-09-08), whose first step this row was.

### 2026-09-09 — The town founded to its customers, measured — context, not a decision
tuesday22 planned the lane "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09, above) and measured two things. **At founding, 534–771 jobs per city — offices alone 452–690 of them — exist only because the foreign bill pays for them; and offices outbid the schools in the job-choice score, so the civic buildings the treasury already funds stand about 60 % empty.** A thirty-day trial with the world's money removed and the nine no-customer kinds vacated: the treasury survived on rent and paid every dole in full, and no household fell into arrears — but the shops that took in the displaced workers were out of wage money by week five while household balances rose. The money exists and has nothing to buy. Founded honestly the town has about 460–590 jobs for 1,225 workers, because the catalogue has five goods and no buyer for cloth, timber, bricks, repairs or storage. The four decisions below were put to the principal on this reading, recommendation first in every case.

---

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The engine pays what the calibration names
**Two small payments. Every household pays weekly bills by budget share to the nearest shop of each kind — medicines, clothes, repairs, and "the office", standing in for insurance, the bank and every service the map does not draw. Every firm pays a small share of its wages to an office for its books. Both are real payers with real income; the shelves stay empty because those goods are not drawn yet, and the tile says so.** The principal chose the recommended option ("Yes, two small payments").
**Found:** today households pay only for food, a bar or café cover and rent; a shop founded "to households by budget share" was never handed the money the share names — a payer on paper, not in the ledger.
**Over:** founding only what is drawn — the catalogue's five goods would size the town, and the budget shares the calibration carries for everything else would name payers the engine never lets pay.
**Because:** a payer the calibration names must pay, or the founding rule reads a share the world does not honour.
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — two settlement steps; moderate in readings — the household budget and every shop's revenue change, and they land inside the sizing, so **every pin moves** with it.
**Depends on:** "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09), whose payer test these payments make real; "Kinds with no buyer are not founded until one exists" (2026-09-09) — the shelf is empty, the shop is not.

---

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The idle share is not the concern; the laws are
**Founded honestly, 40–60 % of the town has no job against a real rate near 5 %. The recommendation — print the reading honestly and pay the dole meanwhile — stands, not contradicted. The principal's answer, verbatim: "dont woryy about this now server will become a ecnomy itlsef ocnce we allow other agents to join just make sure of the laws , principles laws like roman empire where economy can run and govern itlsef".**
**Read as direction:** the idle share is a temporary reading; the town fills in when other agents are allowed to join and found firms. What the engine must get right is the **laws and principles**: the founding rule is written as a law any agent can invoke to found a firm in a vacant premise, with the same payer test the founder applies — not a table only the founder reads.
**Design principle for the agent layer, recorded from this answer:** the Roman-law framing — an economy that can run and govern itself under laws. It is the Agentic Singapore thesis (D-1, 2026-09-06, the Architect Mode table below) applied to the economy: there, the laws make domicile valuable and leavable; here, the laws are what let a society of agents run the town without the founder.
**Over:** sizing the town to its jobs (fewer households — hides the reading); a treasury public-works customer (the foreign bill renamed, with the treasury's name on it).
**Because:** the principal's — the server becomes an economy itself once other agents join; the laws are what must be right now.
**Reversal cost:** cheap for the reading (a printed rate; the dole is already paid). The law is the sizing engine written as an invocable rule rather than a founder's table — the same work in a different shape, and the shape is the decision.
**Depends on:** "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09); "The mainland's farmland is sized to the world's mouths — and land policy is not fixed" (2026-09-08), the principal's condition that a society of agents may rewrite the record; D-1.
**[friday22 note]** The trial's other finding — shops out of wage money by week five while household balances rose — is the money-with-nothing-to-buy problem; the two payments above answer the part the calibration names, and the rest waits on goods being drawn ("Kinds with no buyer", 2026-09-09). The idle reading stays printed, not sized away, until agents fill the premises.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The lone city is founded to its customers too
**One rule, one code path: the lone city founds firms by the same payer test as a federated city. Its three Phase 1 devices — the surplus dump, the import backstop, the foreign service bill — stay gated on `federated` exactly as decided; the bill then bills about a hundred desks instead of a thousand, and its size sits on the boundary row as before.** The principal chose the recommended option.
**Over:** keeping the old quota founding in the lone city so the Phase 1 device has a thousand desks to bill — two founding paths, one of them a fiction kept for a testbed.
**Because:** one rule; the testbed's device still has something to bill, at a size the row prints.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — the alternative is one branch on `federated` in the founding; **the lone-city pin moves** with the sizing, as it would have anyway (the district quota in `buildings.ts` becomes a count of premises).
**Depends on:** "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09) — this closes its "Not decided"; "The foreign bill stops at founding, no transition" (2026-09-09), which kept the device in the lone city; "The federation is the world" and its amendment (a) (2026-09-08), which gated the other two devices on the snapshot flag.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The farmland reduction folds into this lane
**The land-policy record's first application — farms per thousand mouths fed, including the island's share, a cover multiplier and a floor, about four farms a city down to about two — lands in the founding-to-customers lane: the same record, the same shape of rule as the rest of the sizing, one pin move instead of two. The record can be rewritten by a society later, as the principal conditioned.** The principal chose the recommended option ("Yes, fold it in").
**Over:** keeping the reduction deferred until the closed world had been measured for a month (the 2026-09-08 amendment's deferral) and landing it as a second pin move after the sizing.
**Because:** the sizing lane moves every pin once; a farm sized to mouths is the same rule as a shop sized to budget shares.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — the record carries its `why`; a society rewriting it is the design.
**Depends on:** "The mainland's farmland is sized to the world's mouths — and land policy is not fixed" (2026-09-08) and its amendments, the second of which records this; "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09).

### 2026-09-09 — [assumed] Founding to customers — four routine choices
From tuesday22's plan, 2026-09-09. Taken by the coordinator on the planner's recommendation, not put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.
**(A) The border depot and the bonded warehouse are paid per lot from the customs account** — a real customer already in the world — rather than founded to nobody.
**(B) The warehouse is not founded** — no storage buyer exists — under "Kinds with no buyer are not founded until one exists" (2026-09-09).
**(C) The office's customer is the firms' services share of their wage bill plus the households' services divisions no other kind draws** — the two payments of "The engine pays what the calibration names" (2026-09-09), routed to the office.
**(D) Number fallbacks land as `param()` values with a `why` naming the loader that will replace them** (`city/worldkit/worldkit/loaders/{icio,ilostat,firms,consumption}.py`).
**Reversal cost:** cheap for all — (A) one payer, (B) one vacant premise, (C) two routings, (D) the badge the worldkit already uses.
**Depends on:** "Firms are founded to their customers" and the four `[confirmed]` entries above (all 2026-09-09).

**Amendment (2026-09-09, after jarvis11's read): (A) is superseded the same day.** As built, the border depot and the bonded warehouse were paid per present hour — not per lot — from an account that holds only fees (the FTA's tariffs are zero on every HS group), with the treasury behind it and bounded only by its balance. jarvis11 read that form for what it is: a treasury public-works customer no law names — the option the principal rejected under "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09). The sheds are **civic** now: the border's own infrastructure, in the treasury's sized budget, paid in the civic branch like the customs yard and the berth — one of each a city, as the founding already had them (Q1 of "Living World Phase 4, the principal's nine answers", 2026-09-05; (C) of "The second sea lane — eleven routine choices", 2026-09-08). Reversal cost: cheap — one branch in the sizing; the pins moved with the lane. Recorded because a payer "already in the world" that turns out to be the treasury under another name is the drift this log exists to catch. **Confirmed by the principal, 2026-09-09** — "[confirmed] The border sheds are civic", below.

### 2026-09-09 — [assumed] Founding to customers, landed — five choices made while measuring
Taken by the coordinator on 2026-09-09 while landing the three lanes, each measured before it was kept; not put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.
**(a) One base for the bills and the sizing.** Lane P found the household bills applied the raw COICOP shares — shares of *total* consumption, in which food and housing already sit — to income after rent and food, a double subtraction that under-billed every shop 1.7× on Nordholm's numbers, while the sizing priced the same shops at gross income. Now the bills renormalise each division's share over `1 − share_food − share_housing`, and the sizing prices the bill-paid kinds at wages × (1 − savings rate − rent-to-income target − food share at the median) ÷ that rest — wages alone, not the dole or a pension: a household on either clears neither rent nor food at these rents and pays no bills (lane P counted 309 households of 974 with anything left on the first Friday).
**(b) The sizing's income is a fixed point of its own founding.** Priced at the calibrated 1,000 employed, the shops were founded to an income a town 36–44 % idle did not have, and earned half their wages. Now round 0 reads the calibrated employment at the mean wage and every round after reads the wage bill the previous round founded, offices and civic included, so the founding is priced at the customers it leaves in work. **Anchored, not fed back:** the treasury's civic budget and the sector caps stay at the calibrated level, because rents are per dwelling and do not follow the founding (tuesday22's trial paid every dole at 70 % idle from rent); a first cut that fed the founding into the civic payroll collapsed the town to 36 jobs. Result, seed 42: lone Halvik 386 of 2,442 slots (145 private, 241 civic), idle 61 %; federated Halvik 426 (167 + 259), 57 %; Dorsk 401 (160 + 241), 55 %; Amaranth City 458 (199 + 259), 63 %. One office per city (44–49 desks), from eighteen. **Confirmed by the principal, 2026-09-09** — the anchor was put as a question the same evening; "[confirmed] The fiscal anchor: the city's payroll is held at the calibrated level", below.
**(c) A farm floor founds premises, not slots.** The floor of two wheat farms had founded all sixteen of their slots when the mouths needed two; two whole farms earned a seventh of their wages. Now the floor is the least premises the payable slots are spread over — two farms of two slots each for 3,000 mouths.
**(d) The market stall is founded to nobody until it has a shelf.** Measured: stalls sold nothing in thirty days in any of the three cities, before and after the sizing. Founded "to households" who never come is the same fiction as the foreign bill.
**(e) The seven-day roll's warm-up is corrected in the view, not the engine.** The observatory's foreign-bill rate divides the roll by `7 × (1 − (6/7)^days)`, exact for a constant series; and two dashboard labels were renamed to fit two lines (`out of work`, `goods exported`) — seen in a browser.
**Measured after the fixed point (thirty federated days, revenue ÷ wages):** the food chain near one (grocer 1.4, bakery 0.8–1.2, mill 1.1–1.8); the office, clothing shop and hardware store about 0.5 on the mainland and about 1.0–1.2 on the island; the pharmacy 2.8–6.4, paid twice — the outing's cover and the health bill; farms 0.4–0.6 — the ×2 cover multiplier founds capacity that stands full half the time and pays wages for it; bars 7–14; the wider world drew nothing anywhere; baskets over twenty days Halvik 92.1–120.9, Dorsk 92.3–101.3, Amaranth City 91.7–106.9, all PASS; money conserved in all three currencies.
**Reversal cost:** cheap for (a), (c), (d), (e) — a formula, a floor, one vacant premise, a divisor in the view; moderate for (b) — the fixed point is the sizing's shape, and what it anchors (the civic budget, the sector caps) is a choice the principal has not seen — seen and confirmed the same evening, below; **every pin moves** with the lane, as its decision said.
**Depends on:** "Firms are founded to their customers", "The engine pays what the calibration names", "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are", "The farmland reduction folds into this lane", and "[assumed] Founding to customers — four routine choices" (all 2026-09-09).
**[friday22 note — resolved 2026-09-09]** (b) is a shape, not a number, and was put to the principal the same evening as the standing instruction requires; confirmed — "[confirmed] The fiscal anchor: the city's payroll is held at the calibrated level", below. Open item (i) still stands, and the two halves of (vi) the anchor did not settle — may the treasury borrow; do rents follow the town's income — are the principal's.

### 2026-09-09 — [assumed] Founding to customers, landed — after jarvis11's read
Taken by the coordinator on 2026-09-09 after jarvis11 read the landed lanes, each measured before it was kept; not put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation. The amendment to (A) of "four routine choices" (above) is from the same read.
**(a) The sizing prices the bill-paid kinds net of income tax at the cap.** The bills are paid from income after the tax the founding solves for, and the same gross cents were already the treasury's on the civic row. Fail-closed: it under-founds slightly.
**(b) A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms, or whose residents hold jobs outside every founded firm's slots.** The record and the firms are two hashed authorities; a society that rewrites the record (the principal's land condition) must not restore against stale firms. **Confirmed by the principal, 2026-09-09** — refuse, not repair; "[confirmed] A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms", below.
**(c) A founding that stops at the round cap is written `converged: false` with its `rounds`,** and the record's sentences are always the table priced at the founding as it stands.
**(d) The bills tally keeps no lifetime totals.** An index is not state; a reading that survives a restore differently is state in disguise.
**(e) Spotlights are appointed among adults only.** Once the founding changed who came first, creation order appointed a child, a working teen and an 84-year-old, and none was asked anything in two days.
**(f) The decisions feed test accounts for rows the ring dropped, and says so.** Founded to its customers, a 300-resident test town has one bar, and 148 evenings there in one 17:30 frame overrun the ring — (vii) below.
**(g) Validate's rent-to-income is read over renting households with an earner** — the calibration's own universe for the target — and the welfare-only households are printed beside it, not judged.
**(h) Two checks the founded town fails on the seven-day window are pinned in the tests with their reason, not widened.** Engel's quintile order — the bottom quintiles are dole households; the correlation stays negative and the check passes over thirty days. Rent-to-income — ~50–55 % over earners: rents were priced for a household with the calibrated number of earners and the founding leaves ~0.6. Likewise 63 % of commutes under an hour where the brief's town read 90 % — the jobs are where the customers are. Printed, not sized away, as the principal directed on the idle share ("dont woryy about this now … just make sure of the laws" — "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are", 2026-09-09).
**Measured (thirty federated days, seed 42, after every fix):** Halvik 399 of 2,382 slots founded (110 private, 289 civic), Dorsk 412 of 2,434, Amaranth City 434 of 2,418; lone Halvik 363 of 2,442 (41 firms in 141 premises, 100 vacant); idle 54–65 %; one office a city, from eighteen. The wider world moved 0.00 a day in every federated city and the boundary row reads `gated off`. Every check PASS except rent-to-income (49.5 % over 232 earning households in the lone city); Engel PASS over thirty days. Lone basket 95.5–115.3, ending 97.6; VYL ×0.747–×1.000, ending ×0.805; AMD pegged the whole month with 1.21 of 3 months of cover left; 73 shipments delivered, 0 short, 24 sailings; money conserved in all three currencies; the fake-minds recording re-recorded (97 answers); 598 tests.
**Reversal cost:** cheap for (a), (c), (d), (e), (f), (g) — a term, a flag, a tally, a filter, an account, a filter; moderate for (b) — a restore that refuses is a rule about what a snapshot is, and a society rewriting the record meets it first; (h) is a debt, not a cost — the two checks pass when the laws in (v) and (vi) below exist, and the pins say so.
**Depends on:** "[assumed] Founding to customers, landed — five choices made while measuring" (2026-09-09), whose (a) the tax term here refines and whose anchor (b) stands, now named as a law in (vi) below; "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" and "Firms are founded to their customers" (both 2026-09-09).
**[friday22 note]** (h) pins two failing checks as expected failures. That is honest only while the log names the law that would make them pass — (v) and (vi) below, the principal's. If a third check joins them without a law named, the pin has become a way of not looking.

(xv), (xvi)–(xviii), (xx), (xxiii)–(xxix), (xxxi)–(xxxiii) stand. **Updated 2026-09-14, with the rents-against-net answer:** two new items — **(xxxix)** the under-founding (363 of 1,000 slots, tax at cap), the principal's; **(xl)** the scale lane under D-4, the coordinator's to plan, targets to the principal.
- **(i) A law of exit and entry, not a better estimate.** The mainland's mean-field base still overstates what households actually have left after rent per dwelling — the remaining half of the shortfall (b) above closed the other half of. The honest closer is not a finer estimate but a law: a firm that cannot pay its wages for some weeks closes and its premise stands vacant; an agent may found in a vacant premise by the same `canFound` law — the principal's "laws under which an economy can run and govern itself" ("The idle share is not the concern; the laws are", 2026-09-09). **Decided, late the same evening:** the weeks are four short paydays, and closing frees the workers and the premise — "[confirmed] A firm closes after four short paydays"; the entry is any resident's agent by `canFound`, with four weeks of wages and a very small fee — "[confirmed] Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages"; both below. **Found again, and answered at the door, 2026-09-11:** the exit and entry laws did not close the overstatement this item names; the ownership landing moved it from the treasury's purse to a founder's — every resident founding closed within a month ("[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read", 2026-09-11). Answered as this item said it should be, by a law and not a better estimate: before a premise is offered, the entry law reads the books of the firms of that kind and offers only where the customer has actually paid the wages — "[confirmed] The law must name a customer who pays enough" (2026-09-11), building now.
- **(ii) Who pays for standing capacity.** The land policy's cover multiplier founds farm slots that produce nothing when the store is full but are paid for the hours — a labour-law question for the principal: are idle hours paid? **Still the principal's, late the same evening:** not put with the exit-and-entry questions; no recommendation stands; not to be tagged `[assumed]` — a labour law is the principal's to write. **Still unanswered after the exit-and-entry lane landed (2026-09-09):** the closing law now reads the farms the cover multiplier oversized — Dorsk lost two wheat farms, a mill, a dairy and a vegetable farm in ninety days — so the question of who pays for standing capacity is no longer only a sizing reading; it decides whether those farms were failing or were always paying for hours nobody bought. Still the principal's; still not to be tagged `[assumed]`. **DECIDED, late the same evening (2026-09-09) — "[confirmed] Idle hours are bought", below.** Yes: a worker who turns up is paid whether or not there is anything to make; that is what an employment contract is, it keeps the payroll simple, and it is why a firm with too much capacity runs out of money and closes. It is also the answer to Dorsk's oversized farms: the cover multiplier founds capacity the diet does not draw and the wages are real, so the closing law read them correctly. (ii) is closed.
- **(iii) The pharmacy is paid twice** — by the outing's cover and by the health bill — for the same need.
- **(iv) The 30-day window ends a week before a firm's four weeks of opening capital does**, so a thirty-day reading cannot see a firm run out; validate's "weeks of wages left" column is the tell to read.
- **(v) A law of exit and of entry is what closes the remaining gap.** The founding's customer is a mean-field one; the one who pays is a household with what it has left after rent. A firm that cannot pay its wages for some weeks closes and its premise stands vacant; an agent founds in a vacant premise by `canFound`. The gap, thirty days in: mainland offices, clothing and hardware at ~0.5 of their wages, the island at ~1.0. This is (i) with the number on it — the principal's "principles laws like roman empire where economy can run and govern itlsef" ("The idle share is not the concern; the laws are", 2026-09-09). **Decided, late the same evening, with (i):** the four entries below "The city earns from what it makes and serves" — the closing, the founding, the rents' rate, and where a closing firm's money goes. **2026-09-11:** the gap's other half — the customer who pays about half — is closed at the entry law's door, not by a finer estimate: "[confirmed] The law must name a customer who pays enough" (2026-09-11). The mean-field customer stays the day-zero founder's table and the fallback for a kind no firm has ever traded in; everywhere else the door reads what was actually paid.
- **(vi) The fiscal anchor is decided; the deficit and the rents are not — decided in full the same evening.** The anchor — the civic payroll and the trade-structure caps held at the calibrated level, because rents are per dwelling — was put to the principal the same evening and confirmed ("[confirmed] The fiscal anchor: the city's payroll is held at the calibrated level", below; (b) of "five choices made while measuring", above). What remained, the principal's: whether the treasury may run a deficit — may it borrow — and whether rents follow the town's income. Those are the governance laws that would let the two pinned checks in (h) pass; the 36-job collapse is what the anchor holds off until one exists. **Decided in full, 2026-09-09, the same evening, both on the recommendation:** the deficit — the treasury keeps a hard budget, no borrowing, no money created; when short it pays part wages and part dole and says so ("[confirmed] The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created", below); the rents — a landlord lowers the rent of an empty dwelling and raises rents when none stands empty, a few percent a month, so rents follow the town's income through the housing market and the hard budget absorbs what the city as landlord loses ("[confirmed] Rents reprice to vacancy, slowly", below). Nothing of (vi) is open. Neither law is built: the next lane after exit and entry, or with it, tuesday22 to plan; the pins in (h) flip when it lands.
- **(vii) Nothing bounds a venue's room.** A bar with five slots serves 148 covers in one frame ((f) above).
- **(viii) A coarse path edge on seed 42 passes through tile 23398, which is not a road tile** — a routing-layer defect, pinned in the schedule test with its cause.
- **(ix) `KindFounding` carries the whole sentence as `why` and no numeric `payable`,** so the founding table cannot print "the customer pays for N".
- **(x) Grocers hold 0.0–0.5 weeks of wages at day 30 while earning 1.4× their wages** — a cash-flow reading, not judged; and federated Halvik's bonded warehouse is staffed 0 of 14 — nobody takes the jobs.
- **(xi) The pharmacy is paid twice for one need** — the cover and the health bill. This is (iii), still standing after the read.
- **(xii) The `ledger30d` fields are cumulative from founding, despite the name.**
- **(xiii) Should a Phase 6 founding policy come next? — the principal's.** Added 2026-09-09, after the exit-and-entry lane landed. The exit law works and the entry law exists as a path an agent can take, but no agent holds a policy that decides to take it, and the city founds nothing after day zero. Over ninety days on seed 42 eleven firms close across three cities — lone Halvik ends with no grocer — and nothing re-founds one; ten of a hundred and four vacant premises have a customer `canFound` can name. The measured reading under "[assumed] Exit and entry, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-09, below) is the argument for it: without a founding policy the towns hollow out over three months. Whether that lane comes next, or whether something else does, is the principal's to say; no recommendation is recorded here and this is not to be tagged `[assumed]`. What such a policy must do is already shaped by the founding amendment: only an `allow` founds, so a principal must write its agent a founding mandate and widen its per-transaction limit before it can act ("Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages", amendment, 2026-09-09). **ANSWERED, late the same evening (2026-09-09) — "[confirmed] The server is the government of the city, and it intervenes under law", below.** The principal took none of the three options and gave a direction instead: the server itself is the government of the city and the entire ecosystem, and it intervenes; make sure of the laws. Read concretely by the coordinator as **the city is the founder of last resort** — a vacant premise whose `canFound` names a real customer, unfounded by any agent after a badged number of days, is founded by the city from the hard budget, as an ordinary firm under every law including the closing one. That is **built and measured (2026-09-10)** — "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built", below: fourteen days of first refusal and one founding a city a week, both badged; the three pinned hashes unmoved; lone Halvik keeps two grocers over a hundred and twenty days where it ended with none. What remains of (xiii) is **the Phase 6 policy's first refusal**: the delay must be long enough that an agent policy, when one exists, gets the premise before the city does — and the reading that shows whether it ever does (city-founded against agent-founded, now printed on validate's treasury row, and reading 10–11 to 0 in every city at day 120) is the tell. The built law also has a shape worth re-opening then: the window counts a premise's vacancy, not the age of the opportunity, so the first city founding lands on the same midnight as the closing that freed the customer. Whether that Phase 6 lane comes next is still the principal's. **Updated 2026-09-10, with the ownership lane's answers:** the first-refusal half is settled — **"[confirmed] The agent looks first"** (2026-09-10, below): the founding policy runs in the same midnight, immediately before the city's walk, so first refusal is an order and not a delay. What remains of (xiii) is only the policy itself — until one exists, the agent's step is empty and the treasury row still reads city N, agents 0. **CLOSED 2026-09-11 — the policy landed late on 2026-09-10:** "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11, below). Its shape — a single-use payment mandate for exactly capital plus fee, eligibility nearest-first, a fitted rule for every resident who is not a spotlight — is the coordinator's and tagged there. At day 120 the treasury row reads **city 0–1, agents 10–16** in every city. Nothing of (xiii) is open.
- **(xiv) The dividend's share and its cadence — the coordinator's to settle and badge, unless the principal says otherwise.** Added 2026-09-10 with "Who owns the day-zero firms" (below), whose reading pays a badged share of the city's owner-draw straight out to resident households, equally, month by month. Neither the share nor the cadence is chosen. Both land as `param()` with a `why` in the calibration bundle, rewritable by a society, and both are tagged `[assumed]` when they land. **The line at which this stops being the coordinator's:** a share large enough that the owner-draw is mostly a transfer to households is a fiscal law, not a settlement, and goes back to the principal. **DECIDED, later the same day (2026-09-10) — "[confirmed] The residents' dividend is fifteen percent of the city's draw", below.** The coordinator did not settle it: the share was put to the principal with the measured arithmetic (the city draws about NHK 1.2m a month in Halvik across 974 households), and he chose **fifteen percent** over fifty — the transfer-programme line this item drew, at which a Dorsk household would receive 0.74× its median balance every month — and over five, which changes nothing anyone can do. The cadence is the month's turn the draw already has. The number lands as `assumptions/founding_<country>/owner_dividend_share` = 0.15, badged `assumed` in the calibration sense (not from a dataset; a society may rewrite it), but the choice is the principal's and the entry is `[confirmed]`. (xiv) is closed.
- **(xv) Whether being an owner becomes a decision rather than a settlement — the principal's.** Added 2026-09-10, from the option not taken under "The owner draws everything above four weeks of wages, monthly" (below): the owner deciding each month what to draw was rejected *for now*, on the ground that one fitted rule answering for every owner is a rule pretending to be a choice. Once agents own enough firms for their choices to differ from one another, the question is live — does an owner draw by law, or decide? Not to be tagged `[assumed]`: it is the same class of question as the labour law was, and it is the principal's. **Still the principal's, 2026-09-11:** the landing did not touch it. The reading that would make it live — an agent-owned firm holding enough above four weeks to draw — has not yet happened in a hundred and twenty days; every founder's firm closes before it does. **Made concrete for one decision, 2026-09-11:** whether a firm sells day-old stock below what it paid is the owner's — "[confirmed] The owner decides whether to sell below cost" (2026-09-11): an owner's standing order to its firm's agent, the engine's rule as the default for firms nobody owns and for residents who are not spotlights. The draw itself stays a settlement; the rest of (xv) stays the principal's.
- **(xvi) No cooling period — `[assumed]` by omission.** Added 2026-09-11 with "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (below). A founder may found again the midnight after their firm closes, and a premise the exit law empties is re-founded the same midnight — the lone city's day-48 foundings are in the two grocer premises the very midnight they close. Neither wait was chosen; both are absent because no law names one. With the fitted rule (d) saying yes whenever a household holds capital, fee, a month's rent and a month's food, the shape to watch is a household founding, losing the stake, and founding again as soon as its balance allows.
- **(xvii) The `seeking` count climbs while the new firms are fully staffed — an engine reading.** Added 2026-09-11. The seeking law hires whoever is nearest ("Anyone without a job looks for one once a day", 2026-09-09), so a firm founded at midnight is staffed the next morning by the nearest seekers, and the workers a closing displaced stay seeking. The rising count is the law working as written, not a founding that failed to hire; whether nearest-first is the hiring rule the principal wants when there are more seekers than slots is not decided. **2026-09-11:** still not decided, and the Phase 6 lane does not touch it — see (xx).
- **(xviii) The last turn's dividend lives on a tally, blank after a restore until the next turn — an engine reading.** Added 2026-09-11. What the feed and the inspector show as "the last dividend" is a per-world tally that is never snapshotted, in the same class as the payday tally `weekOwed` was kept off ("[assumed] The founder of last resort, built", (b), 2026-09-10): no law reads it, so nothing answers differently after a restore, but a restored world shows no dividend until day 31, 61 or 91 next comes round. Not put in the hash on purpose; recorded so nobody reads the blank as a dividend that was not paid.
- **(xix) The dispatch order of lots — oldest-first or freshest-first — is to be measured, not assumed.** Added 2026-09-11 with "Phase 6 under D-2, planned, and the grocer's shelf measured" (2026-09-11). The shelf correction puts lots at every firm, binned at source; which lot a firm ships or sells first decides how much arrives day-old and how much is binned, and the two-bin measurement already showed that a fix at one point in the chain can sell less on the same draw. Neither order is chosen; the lane measures both and records the one kept as `[assumed]` with the reading beside it. **CLOSED 2026-09-11 — by measurement, not by choice:** a lorry loads the producer's freshest lot and a shopper takes the shelf's oldest (`DISPATCH_ORDER = 'freshest'`, `SELL_ORDER = 'oldest'`); milk arriving day-old 63.3 % → 0.0 %, grocers' binning 12,656 → 1,518 units, grocers' revenue after goods NHK −6,142 → +14,361 over 45 lone days; freshest/freshest measured and rejected. (a) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11), with the reading beside it. Nothing of (xix) is open. **REVERSED 2026-09-12 — the sell order is the blend:** those numbers were read through a till that priced the bin's mean while handing over the oldest lot, so the "16.3 %" and "+14,361" were old stock struck at list. With the till pricing what it hands over, oldest reads 47.3 % at the day-old price and +1,258 after goods; freshest 16.0 % and +3,452; blend 13.6 % and +10,392; federated grocer #17 closes under oldest and stays open under the blend, keeping more than its wages. `SELL_ORDER = 'blend'`, `DISPATCH_ORDER` stays `'freshest'` — (a) of "[assumed] jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes" (2026-09-12). Closed again, on an honest till; the orders themselves pass to (xxiv).
- **(xx) Hiring nearest-first and the wage stay the engine's until (xvii) is settled.** Added 2026-09-11. Under D-2 the firm's agent and its operating mandate are being built, and the question of whether an owner's agent hires — and at what wage — is the natural next clause. It is **not** in this lane: the seeking law hires the nearest seeker ("Anyone without a job looks for one once a day", 2026-09-09) and the wage is the calibration's ("The engine pays what the calibration names", 2026-09-09), and both stay the engine's until (xvii) — nearest-first when seekers outnumber slots — is put to the principal. Recorded so that an operating mandate landing without a hiring clause is read as deferred, not as decided.
- **(xxi) A passport principal's mandates do not verify — an engine reading.** Added 2026-09-11 with "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" ((g)). A passport principal holds no secret in the engine (`secret: ''`), so the standing grocery intent and any founding mandate issued for it carry the engine's stand-in signature over the empty string and `verifyMandate` answers false, honestly. The fix is a signed-request path at `issueMandate` so the passport holder signs its own mandates; until it lands, an admitted agent acts under mandates nobody outside can verify, which is the opposite of what D-3's gate promises. **Extended 2026-09-12:** the passport principal's **delegation credential** is as unverifiable as its mandates — same empty secret, same stand-in signature. The signed-request path must cover both, or the door admits an agent whose delegation nobody outside can check either. **CLOSED FOR MANDATES 2026-09-12 — open for the delegation credential only.** The principal chose that the passport carries the first mandate ("[confirmed] The passport carries the first mandate, and the door makes it easy to sign", 2026-09-12); the holder now signs every mandate in its name — the opening intent at the door, foundings, operating orders, escalation answers — with its passport key, verified against its public key, `signedBy: 'holder'` on the chain, and `issueMandate` throws for a holder ("[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3", 2026-09-12). **What stays open:** the delegation credential `createAgent` writes for a holder's personal agent still carries the stand-in over the empty secret and verifies false; a countersigning door is the fix, and until it lands D-3's gate is met for what the agent does, not for that it is the holder's. **CLOSED WHOLE 2026-09-12 — the countersigning door landed:** `countersignDelegation(host, agentId, signature)` — the holder signs `delegationPayload(body)` with its passport key and the credential carries that signature; `delegationVerifies` reads the key by scheme; one chain row `agent:<role>:countersigned`, `signedBy: 'holder'` ("[assumed] P5 — the holder countersigns its agent's delegation", 2026-09-12). A holder's mandates and its delegation now both verify against its public key. Nothing of (xxi) is open.
- **(xxii) What a second admission of the same identity means — DECIDED 2026-09-12.** Added 2026-09-11 with (c) of the same entry. Admission is once per DID per city, so a DID that leaves cannot re-enter that city today. Once the exit law reaches households — a household of one two months in arrears moves, and one that cannot pay leaves — an identity that was put out and returns is a case the admission law has no sentence for: refused for good, re-admitted without the credit, or re-admitted as new. The principal's kind of question once it is live; not to be tagged `[assumed]` then. **DECIDED — "[confirmed] A returning identity is a return: same identity, no second credit" (2026-09-12):** a return is the same principal, history, reputation and any firm still owned attached; the credit is paid once per identity, ever. Nothing acts on it yet — no law lets a household leave, so a DID on the table is a resident still here and is refused as such; the branch is written the day the exit law reaches households. Nothing of (xxii) is open. **2026-09-12, late:** the exit is now a law — "[confirmed] Leaving: a signed act, debts settled, balance taken, free return" (2026-09-12) — so the returning branch has something to act on; it is second in the `[assumed]` build order. **BUILT 2026-09-12, later:** `returningOf`, `admissionAnswer` → `returning`, `returnHolder` — no credit, the escrow returned whole, agents halted under `holder-departed` resumed; a DID still here is refused "already admitted and still here" ((d) of "[assumed] Leaving and the return, built", 2026-09-12). - **(xxiii) Three engine readings from the landing.** Added 2026-09-11. **`AUDIT_RING_CAP` 4,096 is smaller than one payday hour of a lone city (4,111 rows)**, so validate reads the treasury off a cursor inside the hour; the fix on the roadmap is exporting older events (v2 P6), not a wider ring. **The collusion row fires on the engine's own reprice rule** — all firms of a kind move together by construction — so pricing must be the owner's before the row means anything; until F3 lands ((h) of the same entry), the row reads the engine, not the firms. **F3 landed 2026-09-12** ("The owner's order in force — lanes F3, F4, F5"); with every ask declined the row still reads the engine until an owner orders. **Kinds locked out on tombstone evidence are never re-offered** — the third point of the `[friday22 note]` under "The law must name a customer who pays enough" (2026-09-11), listed here so it is counted: a tombstone's books never change, so a kind whose every firm died is not offered again in that city until something re-reads it.

The first real order will be a spotlight's under the prompt fingerprint ((f) of "jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes", 2026-09-12), or a passport principal's once (xxi) lands; until then every ask is declined and the tell reads zero. **2026-09-12, later:** (xxi) landed for mandates — a holder can now sign an operating order, so the first differing order may be a holder's.

- **(xxix) What lifts a resident's `R18/tampered-register` halt — not decided.** Added 2026-09-12 with "[assumed] The kill switch, built — and what the engine cannot hold". A resident's agent whose mandate no longer verifies is halted under a law, and nothing lifts it: the holder's `resumeByHolder` lifts only a `'holder'` halt, and no engine writer un-halts a law halt. A resident (no passport key) cannot re-sign anything either.   **The forged-signature clause is DECIDED 2026-09-13 — "[confirmed] Forged signatures and flooding are the server layer's, on the connection — not the engine's", above the Architect Mode table.** The runtime rate-limits by request origin; the engine judges bytes and keeps no per-DID count — a lockout law would be a denial-of-service law. - **(xxxi) Whether a federation-wide present goes through the city port or each city's worker — deferred.** Added 2026-09-13 with "[assumed] Lane C — the worker's present seam". The worker's seam is one city's door on one worker's port; a holder whose city is another worker's is routed by `client.ts`, and `city/src/worker/protocol.ts` says in so many words that "a federation-wide present — the same passport at every door — comes later through the city port, not through these." Deferred, not decided; the coordinator's line in `protocol.ts` is a placeholder, not an `[assumed]`.

The mechanics (`Policy` record, `setPolicy`, `policy:changed`) are tuesday22's lane plan, `[assumed]` there. **Still open:** which numbers are a governor's to set — moved to (xxxvi).

- **(xxxiv) A child's seat that leaves can only return by presenting a working age — and the return overwrites the seat's age and stage from the passport. The principal's.** Added 2026-09-13 with "[assumed] Adoption, built — and what jarvis11 corrected". A child's seat is claimable ("Any seat, including a child's"), and an adopted seat leaves through `leave` and returns through `admit` like any holder; but the return path is the newcomer's — it takes the passport's age and stage on the holder's word ((d) of "[assumed] Leaving and the return, built", 2026-09-12) and admission asks a working age — so a child who left comes back only as an adult of the passport's choosing, and a returning adult's seat is re-aged to whatever the passport says. - **(xxxv) Two holders in one family who both leave re-form the escrowed purse with whichever returns first — an engine reading, the principal's kind.** Added 2026-09-13 with the same entry. A holder leaving a household of several takes nothing and the household stands; when the last present member is a holder's and it leaves too, the household leaves whole and its balance goes to `ACC_ABROAD` under one household claim. Whichever of the two returns first gets the household back — dwelling and purse — and the second returns into a standing household, lifting the marks only, with no claim of its own on the money the first has already had returned. "Takes nothing; the purse stays with the family" was said about one leaver from a standing family, not about a family that has emptied; whether the escrow is one claim (first-back takes all) or is held until every departed holder is back is the principal's. Not to be tagged `[assumed]`.

- **(xxxix) The under-founding — 363 of 1,000 calibrated slots, 64 % idle, income tax at `TAX_RATE_CAP` 0.40 — fix the founding or accept it; the principal's.** Added 2026-09-14 with "[confirmed] Rents priced against net at founding" (above the Architect Mode table). The town founded to its customers ("Firms are founded to their customers", 2026-09-09) fills about a third of the calibrated jobs, so the civic payroll and the dole for the idle 637 fall on a third of the wage base and the founding's `shortfall / weeklyGrossWages` runs past the cap. The rents fix (option 1) leaves this alone on purpose: the cap at 0.40 is an honest symptom, not a bug. Whether the founding should be made to fill more of the calibration (a sizing change, moving every hash again), or whether a lightly-populated town at the cap is what "founded to its customers" means until agents found the rest under D-2, is the principal's. Not to be tagged `[assumed]`.
- **(xl) The scale lane — what "ready for 10,000 agents" means in engine terms; the coordinator to plan with mage and tuesday22, targets to the principal.** Added 2026-09-14 with D-4. The principal's direction names 10,000 as the readiness target and 3 million as a future minimum, little by little. Not yet named in engine terms: throughput at population 10,000 (ticks per second, the day's midnight laws), memory per agent, the audit ring (`AUDIT_RING_CAP` 4,096 is already smaller than one payday hour of a lone city — (xxiii)), and the worker frame (one worker per city, one message port per door). The coordinator plans the lane with mage and tuesday22 and puts the targets — the numbers, not the direction — to the principal before building. Which laws already fail D-4's "no per-agent scheduled work" (the seeking law's daily walk, the rent index's monthly walk over dwellings) is measured in that lane, not judged here. Not to be tagged `[assumed]`.
  **Measured 2026-09-14 (mage, read-only) — the walls in order.** (1) Housing: 724 dwellings, 70 empty at seed 42; the 71st holder is refused — decided, "[confirmed] Housing for the port" (2026-09-14). (2) Tick throughput is O(residents): the 1,000 t/s pin fails cold at 2,000 residents on this machine (901 t/s) and 10,000 runs at 220–388 t/s. (3) Append-only arrays scanned per poll or act: `inbox.ts` walks every escalation ever written; `audit.ts` ring uses `shift()`; DID scans in `admission.ts`. (4) The snapshot as one JSON string caps at about 70,000 residents. The sim itself is cheap — a 10,000-resident year is about 30 CPU-minutes; the `agents` slice is 2 % of the tick.
  **`[assumed]` — the coordinator's checklist for "light laws" in code terms (mage's five), the reading D-4 flags as the coordinator's, not the principal's:** (1) a door touches only the actor's own rows — no loop over principals, agents or escalations per act; the indexes this needs are DID→principal, account→principal, pending-per-principal; (2) one act = one verify + bounded writes, ≤ 1 ms; (3) no new per-tick sweep — a law runs on an event or a cadence, and the agents slice stays ≤ 5 % of the tick; (4) state a law adds is capped per agent, never an unbounded array read linearly; (5) a law nobody invokes leaves the hash unmoved, and N holders cost N × one holder. The numbers (1 ms, 5 %) are the coordinator's; if a law the principal wants cannot meet them, the number goes back to him, not the law.
- **(xli) The building law's shape — the principal's before it is built; the coordinator will bring a proposal.** Added 2026-09-14 with "[confirmed] Housing for the port". The principal chose that "a law lets agents build dwellings for money" and chose nothing further. Open: who may build (a holder, a firm, the treasury); on what (vacant premises, new land); at what cost, and who is paid; what a built dwelling's rent is (the ladder's price at its rung, the builder's own ask, or the index). The coordinator brings one proposal with the vacancy target's number beside it; nothing is built on the coordinator's word. Not to be tagged `[assumed]`.
