### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The fiscal anchor: the city's payroll is held at the calibrated level
**The founding's fixed point reads the wage bill it leaves, but the treasury's civic payroll and the trade-structure caps stay at the calibrated level — anchored, not fed back.** Confirms (b) of "[assumed] Founding to customers, landed — five choices made while measuring" (2026-09-09, above), put to the principal the same evening, recommendation first. The principal chose the recommended option: "Hold the city's payroll at the calibrated level".
**Found:** a town with no outside money, priced at the wages it leaves, spirals — fewer jobs, less rent and tax, a smaller city payroll, fewer jobs again; the first cut collapsed to 36 jobs.
**Over:** letting the payroll follow the founding and accepting the smaller town — the spiral kept, and the 36-job town with it; letting the treasury run a deficit as a fiscal rule — a governance law, not a sizing choice, and not yet written (Open (vi), above). Neither taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — rents are per dwelling and do not fall with the town's income, and rent is what paid every dole at 70 % idle in tuesday22's trial.
**Reversal cost:** moderate — the anchor is the sizing's shape; letting go of it without a deficit law is the spiral. **Every pin moves.**
**Depends on:** "Firms are founded to their customers" and "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (both 2026-09-09); the `[assumed]` (b) this confirms. Leaves open, the principal's: may the treasury borrow; do rents follow the town's income — both answered the same evening: "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" and "Rents reprice to vacancy, slowly", below.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms
**The founding record and the firms are two hashed authorities for which premises are firms. A restore that finds them disagreeing — or residents holding jobs outside every founded firm's slots — refuses with the reason named. It does not repair.** Confirms (b) of "[assumed] Founding to customers, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-09, above). The principal chose the recommended option: "Refuse, with the reason named".
**Found:** a society that rewrites the record and then restores an old snapshot would get residents pointing at the wrong building's jobs, silently.
**Over:** repair — re-founding the firms from the record inside restore. Not taken; the recommendation's case against it is that a restore which rebuilds state hides the very disagreement a society rewriting the record needs to see.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — a snapshot is either whole or refused, and silence is the failure.
**Reversal cost:** moderate — a restore that refuses is a rule about what a snapshot is; a society rewriting the record (the principal's land condition) meets it first.
**Depends on:** "The mainland's farmland is sized to the world's mouths — and land policy is not fixed" (2026-09-08), the condition that a society may rewrite the record; "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09); the `[assumed]` (b) this confirms.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The border sheds are civic
**The border depot and the bonded warehouse are the border's own infrastructure, paid from the city's sized budget in the civic branch, like the customs yard and the berth. They have no customer.** Confirms the amendment to (A) of "[assumed] Founding to customers — four routine choices" (2026-09-09, above). The principal chose the recommended option: "Civic, in the city's budget".
**Found:** as first built, the sheds were paid per hour from the customs account with the treasury behind it; jarvis11 read that as a treasury public-works customer under another name — the option the principal rejected that morning ("The idle share is not the concern; the laws are").
**Over:** sizing the sheds to the customs account's fee income — the account holds only fees under a zero-tariff FTA, and the treasury would still stand behind it; the importer paying handling per lot as a new ladder rung — a new charge on trade, a law for a society to write, not the founder. Neither taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — the border is infrastructure the city provides, as the yard and the berth are, and a payer that is the treasury under another name is the drift this log exists to catch.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one branch in the sizing; a later society charging importers for handling is a law, not a reversal.
**Depends on:** Q1 of "Living World Phase 4, the principal's nine answers" (2026-09-05); (C) of "The second sea lane — eleven routine choices" (2026-09-08); "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09); the amendment this confirms.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created
**The city treasury spends what rent and tax bring in and nothing more. When they fall short it pays part wages and part dole, and says so; no money is created; the idle share and the pinned checks stay as they read until agents found firms.** Closes the first half of Open (vi) ("Open after the sizing landed", above). Put to the principal the same evening as the anchor, recommendation first, in plain words: "May the city treasury spend more than it takes in? Today it pays the civic payroll and the dole only from rent and tax, and comes up short when those fall." The principal chose the recommended option: "No borrowing, a hard budget".
**Found:** the anchor holds the civic payroll at the calibrated level and says nothing about the shortfall when rent and tax fall beneath it.
**Over:** borrowing against future tax within a limit — a bond, a limit, a repayment rule: a new money-creating act, three rules where the world has none; printing without limit — the old rest-of-world account in another coat, the device "The federation is the world" (2026-09-08) removed. Neither taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — a treasury that pays only what it has creates no money and hides nothing: the shortfall is printed as part wages and part dole, not covered by an account no law names; a hard budget is one law, a bond is three; and the idle share is a reading, not a debt to borrow against, until agents found firms ("The idle share is not the concern; the laws are", 2026-09-09).
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — a shortfall rule in the treasury's settlement is one branch and one printed line; moderate as a law — a bond is a new instrument the money-conservation checks would have to learn, and a society that writes one for itself is the design (the principal's land condition), not a reversal; the founder writing it would be the drift this log exists to catch.
**Depends on:** "The fiscal anchor: the city's payroll is held at the calibrated level" (2026-09-09), whose shortfall this names; "The federation is the world" (2026-09-08) — there is no outside account to print from; "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09); D-1.
**Status:** not built; the next lane after exit and entry, or with it — tuesday22 to plan; the rent-to-income and Engel pins in the tests flip when it lands. **Updated late the same evening:** with it — the shortfall's printed line lands on validate's treasury row in the exit-and-entry lane, and the closing law does not reach the civic payroll it prints: "[confirmed] Civic is a department: the closing law does not reach the city's own firms", below.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] Rents reprice to vacancy, slowly
**A landlord lowers the rent of a dwelling that stands empty and raises it when none stands empty, a few percent a month. Rents then follow the town's income through the housing market, not by decree; the city as landlord earns less when the town is poorer, and the hard budget above absorbs it.** Closes the second half of Open (vi). Put the same evening, recommendation first, in plain words: "Should rents follow the town's income, or stay fixed per dwelling as set at founding?" The principal chose the recommended option: "Rents reprice to vacancy, slowly".
**Found:** rents are fixed per dwelling at founding, priced for a household with the calibrated number of earners where the founding leaves about 0.6; rent-to-income reads ~50 % over earners and is pinned as an expected failure ((h) of "after jarvis11's read", above); the anchor's stated ground is that rents are per dwelling and do not fall with the town's income.
**Over:** fixed per dwelling at founding — today's engine; rent-to-income stays pinned at ~50 % over earners for as long as the founding leaves fewer earners than the rent was priced for; rent as a fixed share of each household's income — a tax with a landlord's name on it, no housing market, nothing for a society to act in. Neither taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — a rent that moves with vacancy is a market, and a market is a law agents can act under (rent, leave, build) without anyone decreeing the number; rents find the town's income through the dwellings that stand empty; and slowly, a few percent a month, so the treasury's rent falls as the town does and not in a day.
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — one repricing step a month per dwelling; moderate in readings — rent is the treasury's main income and the first charge on every household, so **every pin moves** when it lands. The percent and the month are not chosen; they land as a `param()` with a `why`, the badge the sizing already uses, and a society may rewrite them.
**Depends on:** "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09, above), which absorbs the smaller rent; "The fiscal anchor: the city's payroll is held at the calibrated level" (2026-09-09), whose ground this loosens — the note below; (g) and (h) of "[assumed] Founding to customers, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-09), the pin this is the law for; "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09).
**Status:** not built; the next lane after exit and entry, or with it — tuesday22 to plan; the rent-to-income and Engel pins in the tests flip when it lands.
**[friday22 note]** The anchor was confirmed on the ground that rents are per dwelling and do not fall with the town's income. This entry makes them fall, slowly. The anchor stands — the payroll is held at the calibrated level — but its ground is now the hard budget: when repriced rents bring less, the treasury pays part wages and part dole and says so, and the 36-job spiral is held off by the anchor, not by the rents. If the repriced rents fall far enough that the civic payroll is mostly part-paid, that reading is the principal's to see, not to be sized away.

**Amendment (2026-09-09, late the same evening):** the percent and the month are chosen — two percent, at the month's turn — as a badged number in `assumptions/housing_<country>/`: "[confirmed] Rents move two percent a month, a badged number", below.

**Second amendment (2026-09-09, later the same evening):** "when none stands empty" reads the whole city, not a kind; and the household side of the market is named — a household two months in arrears moves to the cheapest empty dwelling it can afford: "[confirmed] The rent rise reads the whole city, and a household two months in arrears moves", below. Status: building now (2026-09-09/10), in the exit-and-entry lane.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward
**The city and the platform ("the company") both earn from what the city makes and serves, so the fees charged to agents for using the city's laws and services are to be very small — and the system may go further and give agents appreciation, a reward or a credit, for using it.** A design principle for the agent layer. From the principal, the same evening, unprompted, verbatim (typos intact): *"also what the city makes sevre and the company itlsf will be makign so fees could be very little or give apprciation fo agents to use it"*. The first sentence is the coordinator's interpretation of those words, not the principal's; recorded as such.
**Read as direction:** the income is the production and the service — the city's economy, and the platform's business on top of it — not the toll at the gate. A fee on an agent for founding, trading or being domiciled here is priced so it never turns an agent away, and the system may pay agents to come. It is D-1 with the price named: the laws are the attraction, and the attraction is not charged for.
**Where it bites:** the city's fee rungs that exist — the customs fee share (`assumptions/trade_costs/customs_fee_share`, 0.005 of CIF, the city's: `bop.feesCollected`); the haulage fee the 2026-09-08 decision left unpriced ("Haulage out of the customs yard is a market, and the city takes a fee", the principal's words there: the city governance "charges a very small fee maybe … or dont but make sure in the future one part of the system, government of city, makes a penny for paying management or any trade laws that will benefit"); and a future founding fee when an agent founds a firm in a vacant premise by `canFound` — nominal or zero. Two more takes exist that the engine names as private, not the city's — the dealer's explicit take on the paying side of an FX trade (rule 22; `city/src/world/fx.ts`) and the issuing bank's LC fee (`assumptions/trade_finance/lc_issuance_fee_share`; `city/src/world/finance/lc.ts`) — and the principle reaches them as the world's price of doing business, which is to stay small for the same reason.
**Over:** nothing was offered; this was volunteered. The shape it rules out is the one the log has been catching all day — a government or a platform that lives on charges to the agents it exists to attract, rather than on the economy they run ("The idle share is not the concern; the laws are", 2026-09-09).
**Because:** the principal's — what the city makes and serves, and what the company makes, is the income; fees can be very little; the system can give appreciation for use.
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — each fee is one `param()` on a ladder; expensive in position — a fee raised on agents later reverses D-1's offer (valuable and leavable), and a society that priced domicile at nearly nothing leaves when it is not.
**Depends on:** D-1 (2026-09-06); "Haulage out of the customs yard is a market, and the city takes a fee" (2026-09-08), whose "come back to this logic" this partly answers — the fee is very small; "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09); "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09, above) — the treasury lives on what the town pays it, not on charges to agents.
**Status:** a standing principle, not built; to be applied when the exit/entry lane and the agent-founding law are planned — tuesday22 names the fee and the appreciation, if any, and the numbers are put to the principal or tagged `[assumed]`. **Applied late the same evening:** the founding fee of "[confirmed] Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages", below, is priced by this principle — very small; its number is not yet named.
**[friday22 note]** "Appreciation" is the principal's word; whether it is a credit, a rebate on a fee, a share of the city's income or something else is not decided. "The company" is the first time the platform's own earning has entered this log — nothing before it says what the platform charges anyone or earns from what. Both are questions for the principal before a number is written.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] A firm closes after four short paydays
**A firm that cannot pay its wages in full on four consecutive Fridays closes at the next midnight. It pays the final wages it can; its stock goes at cost to the nearest firm of the same kind, or is written off and said; its workers become job seekers; its premise stands vacant, with the closing on the founding record — when, why, what was owed.** The exit half of Open (i) and (v) ("Open after the sizing landed", above). Put to the principal late the same evening, from the coordinator's questions for the exit-and-entry lane, recommendation first: when does a firm close, and what happens to its workers and stock? The principal chose the recommended option: "Four short paydays, then it closes".
**Found:** today's engine has no exit — a firm that cannot pay its wages pays what it can, forever; nothing frees a premise or a worker, so the vacant premises the entry law (below) founds in never appear from failure, and the gap (v) measured — mainland offices, clothing and hardware at ~0.5 of their wages — has no closer.
**Over:** never closes, pays what it can — today's engine; closes the first week it is short — one slow week kills a sound firm. Neither taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — four weeks of wages is the horizon the founder gave every firm as opening money, so four short Fridays is a firm that has run through the credit it was founded on; one short week is weather, four is a business without customers; and the closing is written to the record, so a society reading a vacant premise sees when it emptied, why, and what was owed.
**Reversal cost:** cheap for the number — four is one `param()` with a `why`, and a society may rewrite it; moderate for the law — closing is the first thing in the engine that ends a firm, and the stock transfer, the workers' return to seeking and the record's closing line are three seams the tests must pin. The pins move only when a closing falls inside a pinned window, and (iv) above says thirty days ends a week before a firm's opening money does — the first closing is seen in a longer run, or by the bench command.
**Depends on:** "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09) — a law, not a founder's table; "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09), whose founding record carries the closing; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" (2026-09-09, below), the entry half, whose four weeks this four answers to; "A closing firm pays final wages first; the rest goes to the treasury" (2026-09-09, below), which says where the money goes. Bounded and completed late the same evening by three entries below: "Civic is a department: the closing law does not reach the city's own firms" — the law's reach; "Anyone without a job looks for one once a day" — what "become job seekers" means, one law for all; "The rent rise reads the whole city, and a household two months in arrears moves" — what happens to a closed firm's workers' households.
**Status:** planning by tuesday22 now (2026-09-09); building next. **Updated late the same evening:** building now (2026-09-09/10), the exit-and-entry lane, with the three entries named above.
**[friday22 note]** "At cost to the nearest firm of the same kind" is a sale: the taker pays from its own account, and a taker that is itself short cannot. Whether a short taker takes nothing and the stock is written off, or something else, is the planner's to say and tag `[assumed]`. With one office a city, a kind with no second firm is the write-off every time.

---

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages
**Any resident's agent may found a firm of a vacant premise's kind when the law — `canFound`, the same payer test the founder ran at day zero — says a customer exists. The founder pays a very small founding fee to the city and puts up four weeks of that kind's wages from its own account as the firm's opening money, the horizon the founder gave. The city founds nothing after day zero.** The entry half of Open (i) and (v). Put late the same evening, recommendation first: who may found a firm in a vacant premise, and with what money? The principal chose the recommended option: "Any agent, with four weeks of wages".
**Scope:** this lane builds the law and its path — `canFound` invocable by an agent, the fee, the opening money, the record's founding line — exercised by tests and a bench command. A live agent policy that decides to found is Phase 6.
**Found:** the founding rule runs once, at day zero, as the founder's table; vacant premises exist (100 in the lone city) and no law lets anyone found in them, so the town cannot fill in the way the principal said it would — "server will become a ecnomy itlsef ocnce we allow other agents to join".
**Over:** only the city re-founds — the state as sole entrepreneur, the treasury public-works customer under a third name; any agent with no capital — churn without commitment, a firm founded on nothing that closes on nothing. Neither taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — the law is the founder's own `canFound`, now invocable by anyone, which is what "laws under which an economy can run and govern itself" means in the engine; four weeks of the founder's own money is the horizon every day-zero firm got, so an agent's firm is neither favoured nor handicapped against them, and the closing law above reads the same four; and the fee is very small because the city earns from what the firm will make and serve, not from its founding — "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09, above), applied for the first time.
**Reversal cost:** cheap for the money and the fee — two `param()` values with a `why`; moderate for the law — an invocable `canFound` with an account behind it is the first act an agent takes in the engine that creates a firm, and it must write the founding record the restore rule reads ("A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms", 2026-09-09): a founding that does not write the record makes every later restore refuse.
**Depends on:** "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09) — the law that answer asked for; "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09) — the same payer test; "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09) — the fee's size; "A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms" (2026-09-09); "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09, above) — the exit half, whose closings empty the premises this founds in; D-1 (2026-09-06) — founding is the first thing domicile here lets an agent do.
**Status:** planning by tuesday22 now (2026-09-09); building next.
**[friday22 note]** Three things this does not name. The fee's number — "very small" is the principle; tuesday22 proposes it and it is put to the principal or tagged `[assumed]`, as the principle's status said. "Appreciation" — whether founding earns a credit rather than costing a fee — is still the principle's open note. And "any resident's agent": whether a non-resident agent may found — domicile first, then a firm, or the firm as the way in — is D-1's question and is not answered here.

**Amendment (2026-09-09, late the same evening, after jarvis11's read): founding fails closed.** As built, the founding path let the counter's rule carry — an `escalate`, or a `deny` with no mandate at all, meant "the principal paid in person" and the firm was founded anyway, with a row on the chain asserting a human was present that had no fact behind it. jarvis11 read that for what it is: the largest single sum an agent can move — four weeks of a firm's wages out of a household's purse — being the one act the mandate system could not refuse. **Now: only an `allow` founds. An escalation leaves the founding waiting; no mandate at all is a refusal that says so.** The counter's rule stays bounded to what it was written for — a household at a counter, where a person is standing at a till ("[assumed] Living World: what happens at the counter when the agent cannot pay", 2026-09-05, confirmed as (2) of "Principal's answers: what the world is for, the counter seam, trade policy", 2026-09-05). **Consequence worth recording:** a founding is far over an agent's standing per-transaction limit — about 224 NHK against a 4,242 NHK founding — so before an agent can found, its principal must both write it a mandate for the founding category *and* widen what it may spend. That is now what `city/bench/found.ts` does, in the open, and it is the shape a Phase 6 founding policy will have to take. **Reversal cost:** cheap in code — one outcome test on the founding path; expensive as a position — a founding a human's assumed presence could carry puts a claim on the hash chain with no fact behind it, which is the one thing the chain exists to prevent (D-1, 2026-09-06). **Forecast corrected 2026-09-11:** the Phase 6 policy did not take the `found.ts` shape. A widened standing limit would have left a spotlight — posture zero, every non-payment request escalated — unable to found at all; the yes is a single-use payment mandate for exactly capital plus fee, five ticks to live, and the standing limit is not widened — (a) of "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11). Only an `allow` founds, as here; what changed is the mandate the `allow` is read against.

**Second amendment (2026-09-11): the customer must pay enough.** "When the law says a customer exists" was the founder's mean-field arithmetic at the door, and it offered residents premises whose customers pay about half the wages: every resident founding of the ownership landing closed within a month and no founder drew a cent ("[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read", 2026-09-11). Before a premise is offered to anyone, the law now reads the books of the firms of that kind already trading and offers only where what the customer actually paid covers the wages, four weeks in four — "[confirmed] The law must name a customer who pays enough" (2026-09-11), below. Status: building now (2026-09-11).

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] Rents move two percent a month, a badged number
**A dwelling that has stood empty for a month reprices down two percent at the month's turn; when no dwelling of its kind stands empty, up two percent. The number is a calibration assumption with a `why` — `assumptions/housing_<country>/` — replaceable by a measured figure and rewritable by a society.** Prices "Rents reprice to vacancy, slowly" (2026-09-09, above), which left the percent and the month unchosen. Put late the same evening, recommendation first: how fast do rents move with vacancy? The principal chose the recommended option: "Two percent a month, as a badged number".
**Found:** the rents law was confirmed without its rate, and the rate is a number the settlement reads every month — it needed a first value to build against, badged so it is a claim and not a decree.
**Over:** five percent — bigger swings in the treasury's rent income under the hard budget, which has no borrowing to smooth them; half a percent — barely moves within the runs measured. Neither taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — two percent a month is slow enough that the treasury's rent, its main income under the hard budget, falls over seasons and not weeks, and fast enough that a run of the length the experiments use can see rents find the town's income; and badged, so the measured figure replaces it when one exists and a society may rewrite it as it may the land policy.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one `param()` in `assumptions/housing_<country>/`, three countries; the pins move whenever the number does, as for any badged number the settlement reads.
**Depends on:** "Rents reprice to vacancy, slowly" (2026-09-09), the law this prices; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09), which absorbs the smaller rent and is why the swing is kept small; (D) of "[assumed] Founding to customers — four routine choices" (2026-09-09), the `param()`-with-a-`why` badge; "The mainland's farmland is sized to the world's mouths — and land policy is not fixed" (2026-09-08), the same rewrite condition.
**Status:** planning by tuesday22 now (2026-09-09); building next — with the exit-and-entry lane, as the rents entry's status said.
**[friday22 note]** As written, a dwelling that stays empty reprices again at every turn; whether there is a floor is not decided — tuesday22 names one or leaves none, tagged. "Of its kind" is the unit the up-move reads: one empty dwelling of a kind holds every rent of that kind still, which is the law's intent and a thing to watch in the readings.

**Amendment (2026-09-09, late the same evening): "of its kind" is superseded for the rise.** The rise reads the whole city — up only when no dwelling anywhere stands empty; the fall stays per empty dwelling; and a household two months in arrears moves at the month's turn — "[confirmed] The rent rise reads the whole city, and a household two months in arrears moves", below. The note's watch-item is what the principal was shown: per kind would have raised 904 flats' rent while 69 houses stood empty.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] A closing firm pays final wages first; the rest goes to the treasury
**When a firm closes, what is owed to its workers is paid first, as far as its money goes; what is left goes to the city's treasury as unclaimed property, said in the log. No money is created or destroyed. When owners exist (Phase 5), the law changes to pay the owner.** Put late the same evening, recommendation first: where does a closing firm's remaining money go, it having no owner? The principal chose the recommended option: "Final wages first, the rest to the treasury".
**Found:** a firm has an account and no owner — no claim stands behind its balance — so a closing firm's money has nowhere to go: it either vanishes, failing the conservation check every pin carries in three currencies, or sits in a dead account forever.
**Over:** split among its last workers — a claim nobody signed, a windfall the wage contract does not name; written off — breaks money conservation. Neither taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — wages are the one claim on a firm the law already names, so they are paid first; a balance with no claimant is unclaimed property, and the treasury is where unclaimed property goes under laws worth domiciling under (D-1); no money is created or destroyed, as the hard budget requires; and when a claimant exists — an owner, Phase 5 — the law pays the owner, so this is the rule for a firm without one, not a rule about the treasury's take.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — one branch in the closing settlement and one printed line; the owner's branch, when it comes, is an addition, not a reversal.
**Depends on:** "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09, above) — the closing this settles; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — conservation; "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09); D-1 (2026-09-06).
**Status:** planning by tuesday22 now (2026-09-09); building next.
**[friday22 note]** The treasury's receipts from closings should be printed as their own line, apart from rent and tax. A treasury that lives on closings is the shape "The city earns from what it makes and serves" rules out, and the reading is the only way to see whether it is happening.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] Anyone without a job looks for one once a day — the principal's direction, read as one law
**The principal's answer, verbatim (typos intact): *"dont worry about every goods , dont foget goods will genertaed by agenst between each other just make simple economics laws and make system run itlsef that agnets have idnety life structureu and all the poetintioal to build thei rlife"*. A direction, not a pick: none of the three options was chosen by name.**
**Put:** late the same evening, from tuesday22's plan questions for the exit-and-entry lane — when a firm closes its workers lose their jobs; today nobody looks for work after day zero, so a founded firm would have no hands and never close: do people look for work on their own? Three options, recommendation first: the displaced seek daily (recommended — no pin moves; the idle since day zero as a separate step); everyone idle seeks from day one (every pin moves with content); nobody seeks.
**Read as [coordinator's interpretation, not the principal's words]:** one simple law rather than a special class of "displaced" workers — **anyone without a job looks for one once a day**, among the nearest workplaces with a free slot, by the rule everyone used on day zero. Consequences: **every pin moves with content, once**; the free civic slots — the schools the job-choice score left about 60 % empty ("The town founded to its customers, measured", 2026-09-09) — fill from day one. This lands on the mechanics of the second option, not the recommended first; the recommendation lost to the direction, and "once a day" and "nearest with a free slot" are the coordinator's simplest reading, not words the principal used.
**Standing design principle for the agent layer, recorded from this answer:** the engine gives agents an identity, a life structure and the potential to build their lives; goods will be generated by agents between themselves — the founder does not draw every good; the laws stay simple and the system runs itself. Same voice, same evening as "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09): that entry says what must be right (the laws, the Roman-law framing); this one says what the laws are for (agents with a life to build) and what not to build (every good). With "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09) it is the third standing principle of the agent layer from this day.
**Over:** the recommended option — the displaced seek daily, the idle since day zero as a separate step: a class of worker the law would have to name, and two steps where one law serves; nobody seeks — a founded firm with no hands, which never earns and never closes. Neither taken.
**Because:** the principal's — simple economic laws; the system runs itself; agents have identity, a life structure and the potential to build their lives; goods will be generated between agents.
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — one daily step, the day-zero rule re-run; moderate in readings — every pin moves once with content when it lands, and back if reversed. The principle is expensive to reverse: it is what the agent layer is for.
**Depends on:** "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09); "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09) — the closing's workers are what this law picks up; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" (2026-09-09) — a founded firm gets its hands by this law; "The engine pays what the calibration names" (2026-09-09) — a job taken is income the bills read; "The fiscal anchor: the city's payroll is held at the calibrated level" (2026-09-09) — the civic slots that fill are the anchored payroll's; D-1 (2026-09-06).
**Status:** building now (2026-09-09/10), the exit-and-entry lane.
**[friday22 note]** "dont worry about every goods" also reaches "Kinds with no buyer are not founded until one exists" (2026-09-09). It does not reverse it — the city still founds no kind without a buyer — but it says the founder is not to draw the missing goods; agents will. Whether that means the catalogue stays at five goods until an agent adds one, or that an agent may found a kind the catalogue does not carry, is not decided. And "idnety life structureu" is a brief for Phase 6's agent policy, not for this lane; recorded here so the lane's law is written to be invoked by that agent, not by the engine on its behalf.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The rent rise reads the whole city, and a household two months in arrears moves
**The fall is per empty dwelling: a dwelling that has stood empty a month reprices down at the month's turn. The rise is only when the whole city has no empty dwelling — not per kind. A household two months behind on the rent moves at the month's turn to the cheapest empty dwelling it can afford at the target rent-to-income share; if there is none, it stays. The Phase 5 household move, pulled forward.** Put late the same evening, from tuesday22's plan questions, recommendation first: what does the rise read, and what happens to a household that cannot pay? The principal chose the recommended option: "Whole city, and the two-month-arrears household moves".
**Found:** on seed 42, 69 of 173 detached houses stand empty while no flat does, so the rise as written ("when no dwelling of its kind stands empty" — "Rents move two percent a month", 2026-09-09) would raise every flat's rent — 904 households' — while they pay about half their income, and lower houses nobody takes. Today the rise never fires city-wide, and nothing lets a household that cannot pay leave a dwelling for one it can.
**Over:** per kind with no moves — raises 904 households' rent while they pay half their income; lowers houses nobody takes; fall only, no rise and no moves — nothing anyone pays changes for as long as the run is. Neither taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — a rise is the market's signal that the city is full, not that one kind is; the empty houses are the town's slack, and rents find the town's income only if households can move into them; a household that moves when it cannot pay is the housing market acting, not a decree.
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — the rise's test is one count over the city instead of per kind, and the move is one step at the month's turn; moderate in readings — the moves change who pays which rent, and rent-to-income (the pinned check) reads differently once households sit where they can afford. **Every pin moves** with the lane anyway.
**Depends on:** "Rents reprice to vacancy, slowly" and "Rents move two percent a month, a badged number" (both 2026-09-09) — this supersedes the latter's "of its kind" for the rise; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — the city as landlord takes the fall; (g) and (h) of "[assumed] Founding to customers, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-09) — the rent-to-income pin; "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09) — a closing's workers are the households that fall into arrears; "Anyone without a job looks for one once a day" (2026-09-09, above) — the other way out of arrears.
**Status:** building now (2026-09-09/10), the exit-and-entry lane.
**[friday22 note]** "Two months" and "the target share" are the recommendation's numbers, taken with it; both belong as `param()` with a `why` beside the two percent, not as literals. A household that can afford no empty dwelling stays and keeps falling behind — what the arrears become (a debt to the treasury, forgiven, eviction) is not decided, and the treasury row should print the total owed so it is seen. With 69 houses empty on seed 42 the rise does not fire and rents only fall until households move into them — a reading to watch, not a fault.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] Civic is a department: the closing law does not reach the city's own firms
**A school, clinic, berth or customs yard is the city's department, not a firm under the closing law; it cannot close for short paydays. A short civic payroll is the treasury's shortfall — printed on validate's treasury row — and the teachers are paid short and told so.** Put late the same evening, from tuesday22's plan questions: does "four short paydays" close a civic building when the treasury is short? The principal chose the recommended option: "No, civic is a department".
**Found:** under the hard budget the treasury pays part wages when rent and tax fall short, and every civic slot is a wage the treasury pays; read as a firm, a school four Fridays short would close — and nothing could re-found it, because the city founds nothing after day zero and `canFound` names no civic customer (the border sheds, the yard and the berth are civic for the same reason: no payer test names them).
**Over:** the same law for all — a hard budget that can close its own school, which nothing may re-found. Not taken.
**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — the civic branch is the city's sized budget, not a firm founded to a customer; a department short of money is the treasury short, which the hard budget already says to print as part wages and say so; closing what cannot be re-founded is destruction, not exit.
**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — the closing step reads the civic branch out; moderate as a law — a civic building that can close needs an entry law for civic kinds, which does not exist and which "the city founds nothing after day zero" forbids.
**Depends on:** "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — the shortfall this prints; "The fiscal anchor: the city's payroll is held at the calibrated level" (2026-09-09); "The border sheds are civic" (2026-09-09) — the same branch; "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09) — the law's reach, now bounded; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" (2026-09-09) — "the city founds nothing after day zero", why a closed school could not come back.
**Status:** building now (2026-09-09/10), the exit-and-entry lane; the hard budget's shortfall line lands with it, on validate's treasury row.
**[friday22 note]** A teacher paid short is a household that can fall two months into arrears and move under the rents entry above, and by the job-seeking law has a job and does not look for another. Whether a civic worker on part wages may leave for a private slot is not decided; today's rule keeps them. The part-paid share belongs on the treasury row beside the shortfall, so a city running its schools on half wages is seen and not sized away.

### 2026-09-09 — [assumed] Exit and entry, landed — after jarvis11's read
Taken by the coordinator on 2026-09-09 after jarvis11 read the landed exit-and-entry lane; each item measured or verified before it was kept; not put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation. The amendment to "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" (above) is from the same read and is the one item of it that is not routine.
**(a) The closing law refuses a department at its own door.** `closeFirm` throws for a civic kind rather than relying on its one caller's guard — closing a school is the single irreversible act in the engine (nothing may found a civic kind again) and the function is meant to be called by an agent one day, not only by the loop that has the guard today.
**(b) A restore refuses a founding record that founds a premise no open firm stands in.** The firms-to-record check cannot see that shape, and a resident placed in those slots would work for ever unpaid. Asserted at restore only, because the founding law resizes a premise before it makes the firm that will stand in it — mid-founding, the disagreement is the law working.
**(c) The founding does its one throwing step — the resize — before a cent moves,** so a founding that fails charges nobody and leaves the record as it found it.
**(d) The closing threshold is read off the city's calibration bundle, not through `param()` with its own literal.** One law, one authority for its number; a country with no file borrows Nordholm's and says so.
**(e) A load a closed buyer refuses is written off and said when the seller has closed too,** rather than landing in a bin no loop reads.
**(f) The tests that assert what the treasury collects use the engine's own identity** — rent plus what was owed, less what is still owed — rather than a prediction from balances read a tick before the collection. Money moves in the tick the rent is taken, and the prediction was short by 14.87 NHK.
**Measured, ninety days, seed 42, after every fix:** lone Halvik's two grocers close on day 48 and a bakery on day 83, leaving ten workers seeking and the town with no grocer; Dorsk loses a wheat farm on day 69, its mill and a dairy on day 76, a second wheat farm on day 83 and a vegetable farm on day 90; Amaranth City a cafe on day 55, a grocer and a restaurant on day 62, a dairy on day 69 and a bar on day 83. Nothing re-founds any of them: the city founds nothing after day zero and no agent holds a policy yet. The treasury paid every wage and every dole in full throughout, drifting from NHK 7.9m to 4.8m. At sixty days ten of a hundred and four vacant premises have a customer the law can name. Pins: lone `4873830dae251aa9` unmoved; two-city `f65c078efbf46f4f` → `7639d4888ee161c8`, three-city `f101351e1f8a05bf` → `fe373191d9abb9ca`, both because those cities open with free civic slots that idle adults now take from the first morning.
**Reversal cost:** cheap for each — a guard, an assertion, an ordering, a lookup, a branch, a test identity. (a) and (b) are refusals rather than repairs, in the shape "A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms" (2026-09-09) already set; reversing either means choosing to carry on past a state the law says cannot exist.
**Depends on:** "A firm closes after four short paydays", "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages", "A closing firm pays final wages first; the rest goes to the treasury", "Anyone without a job looks for one once a day", "Rents move two percent a month, a badged number", "The rent rise reads the whole city, and a household two months in arrears moves", "Civic is a department: the closing law does not reach the city's own firms" (all 2026-09-09) — the lane these land in; "A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms" (2026-09-09) — (b) extends its check; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — (f) reads the row it prints.
**[friday22 note]** The ninety-day reading is not a fault report, it is the argument for the next lane, and it belongs to the principal: eleven firms close across three cities in three months, nothing re-founds one, and the towns hollow out because the only entity that could found is a live agent policy that does not exist yet. Recorded as (xiii) of the Open note above. The treasury paying every wage in full while the private town empties is the second half of that reading — the hard budget is not what is failing.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] The server is the government of the city, and it intervenes under law

**The principal's answer, verbatim (typos intact): *"i  am tellying you again server itslef shoudl be intewinginas the givenmnet of the city server and enitre ecoysytem just make sure of the laws , system will be designed more bit aganetic design and interaction will design it"*. A direction, not a pick: none of the three options was chosen by name.**

**Put:** the coordinator gave the principal the ninety-day reading — with nothing re-founding, Halvik loses both grocers and a bakery, Dorsk its wheat farms, mill and dairy, Amaranth City a cafe, grocer, restaurant, dairy and bar ("[assumed] Exit and entry, landed — after jarvis11's read", 2026-09-09, above) — and offered three options: build an agent founding policy next (recommended); leave it hollowing and print it; or have the city re-found what it can, labelled in the question as making the state the entrepreneur, "which you rejected once already". The principal answered none of them.

**Read as [coordinator's interpretation, not the principal's words]:** the running world itself — the server — is the government of the city and of the whole ecosystem, and it **intervenes**. What the engine must get right is the **laws it intervenes under**. The agentic design, and the interactions between agents, will shape the rest. Read with the two earlier directions of the same day, this is **the third time the principal has said *make sure of the laws***: "just make sure of the laws , principles laws like roman empire" ("The idle share is not the concern; the laws are", 2026-09-09), "just make simple economics laws and make system run itlsef" ("Anyone without a job looks for one once a day", 2026-09-09), and this one.

**The coordinator's concrete reading — to be built next, and superseded the moment the principal says otherwise: the city is the founder of last resort.** When a premise stands vacant, `canFound` names a real customer who pays, and no agent has founded there within a badged number of days, the city founds it from the treasury's own hard budget. The firm it founds is an **ordinary firm under every law**, including the closing one: four short paydays and it closes like any other, and the treasury gets no exemption it does not already have.

**Why this is not the treasury-as-customer the principal rejected on the same day.** There, the treasury bought hours nobody wanted — a public-works customer no payer test named, a fiction inside the identity, caught twice in one day (the border sheds, "[assumed] Founding to customers — four routine choices" amendment; and the option "a treasury public-works customer" rejected under "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are", both 2026-09-09). Here the law has **already named a customer who pays**; the city is only the founder nobody else was. The demand is real either way; what changes is whose four weeks of wages open the door.

**Over:** the three options as put — an agent founding policy next (the recommendation; not chosen, and not discarded: it is what the delay above gives first refusal to); leave it hollowing and print it (the principal said the server intervenes, so a government that watches its grocers close is not it); the city re-founds what it can, framed as the state as entrepreneur (rejected framing, not rejected act — the principal's own answer is that the server *is* the government and does intervene, which is why the reading above is the state as founder of last resort under a law, not the state as entrepreneur choosing ventures).

**Because:** the principal's — the server itself should be intervening as the government of the city and the entire ecosystem; make sure of the laws; the system will be designed more agentic, and interaction will design it.

**Reversal cost:** moderate. The delay and the badged number of days are `param()` with a `why` — cheap. The law is not: a city that founds is the second founding path in the engine, it must write the founding record the restore rule reads ("A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms", 2026-09-09), and it changes what a vacant premise means — today it is an opportunity waiting for an agent, after this it is an opportunity on a clock. Reversing it after Phase 6 means towns that were kept whole by the city start hollowing again, with every pin moving.

**Depends on:** "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09) — the same voice, the first *make sure of the laws*; "Anyone without a job looks for one once a day" (2026-09-09) — the second; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" and its fail-closed amendment (2026-09-09) — the same `canFound`, the same four weeks, and the reason the city will in practice found nearly everything until Phase 6; "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09) — the law the city's own firms are under; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — the money the city founds with is money it has, or it does not found; "Civic is a department: the closing law does not reach the city's own firms" (2026-09-09) — a founded firm is **not** civic and this does not widen that branch; "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09) — the city pays no founding fee to itself; D-1 (2026-09-06).

**Status:** the founder of last resort is **built and measured (2026-09-10)** — the rule, its two badged numbers, the five routine choices it made and the hundred-and-twenty-day reading are "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built", below; the three pinned hashes did not move. Answers Open (xiii); see the Open note above, updated. What is still open of (xiii) is unchanged: the Phase 6 policy's first refusal, and whether that lane comes next.

**[friday22 note — the tension the coordinator is holding, for the principal]** An agent's founding is fail-closed: only an `allow` founds, and a principal must write its agent a founding mandate *and* widen its per-transaction limit before it can act (amendment to "Any agent may found in a vacant premise", 2026-09-09). No such policy exists until Phase 6. So in practice **the city will found nearly everything** for as long as that is true, and a law written as "last resort" will look like the state as sole entrepreneur in every reading taken before Phase 6 — which is the shape the principal rejected once and framed against again in the question he did not answer. The delay is what keeps it honest: the first agent policy must get **first refusal**, the delay must be long enough for one to act, and the treasury row must print how many firms the city founded against how many agents did, so the day agents outfound the city is visible. If that number never moves after Phase 6 ships, this entry is the thing to re-open.

### 2026-09-09 — [confirmed] Idle hours are bought

**A worker who turns up is paid whether or not there is anything to make. A firm founded with a cover multiplier has slots that stand idle when its store is full, and it pays those hours anyway.** Closes Open (ii), which has stood since the founding lane. Put late the same evening, recommendation first: are idle hours paid? The principal chose the recommended option: "Yes, the hours are bought (Recommended)".

**Found:** the land policy's cover multiplier founds farm capacity the diet does not draw — measured at 0.4–0.6 of wages on thirty federated days ("[assumed] Founding to customers, landed — five choices made while measuring", 2026-09-09) — and the engine already paid those hours, but no law said whether it should.

**Over:** pay only for work done — a job stops being a wage and becomes piecework; the closing law loses most of its bite, because a firm with too much capacity would simply pay less and never run short. Send them home when the store is full — a shift roster, not a law; it is scheduling machinery the engine does not have and the principal has twice asked for fewer, simpler laws.

**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — that is what an employment contract is; it keeps the payroll simple, one rule for every firm; and it is precisely why a firm with too much capacity runs out of money and closes, which the exit law now handles.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — the engine already pays the hours, so nothing changes to land this; expensive in law — reversing it means writing piecework or rostering into the payroll and re-deriving the closing law, whose four short paydays only bite because wages are owed regardless of output.

**Depends on:** "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09) — the law this makes bite; "The farmland reduction folds into this lane" (2026-09-09) and "The mainland's farmland is sized to the world's mouths — and land policy is not fixed" (2026-09-08) — the cover multiplier that founds the idle capacity; "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09); "Anyone without a job looks for one once a day" (2026-09-09) — the other side of the same labour law.

**This is also the answer to why Dorsk's oversized farms closed.** The ninety-day reading — a wheat farm on day 69, the mill and a dairy on day 76, a second wheat farm on day 83, a vegetable farm on day 90 ("[assumed] Exit and entry, landed", 2026-09-09) — is not a failure of the exit law. The cover multiplier founds capacity the diet does not draw, the wages are real, and a firm paying real wages for hours no customer buys runs out of money. The closing law read it correctly.

**Status:** idle hours need no change — the engine already pays the hours, and this entry now records why. Open (ii) is marked decided in place, pointing here.

### 2026-09-10 — [assumed] The founder of last resort, built — five choices, two badged numbers, and what it measured
Taken by the coordinator on 2026-09-10 while building "The server is the government of the city, and it intervenes under law" (2026-09-09) to the coordinator's own reading of it — the city is the founder of last resort. Each item was measured before it was kept; none was put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.

**The rule as built, in one sentence.** At 00:00, after the exit law has run, the city walks its **vacant premises in building order** and founds **the first** one where all four hold: `canFound` names a customer who pays (the same `entryAnswer` an agent's founding is asked, so the city never founds a civic kind or a kind with no buyer); the premise has stood vacant at least **`assumptions/founding_<country>/last_resort_days` = 14**, counted from the tick it last emptied or from tick 0 for a premise the founder never filled; the treasury can put up the opening capital and still hold the week it owes; and the city has founded fewer than **`assumptions/founding_<country>/last_resort_per_week` = 1** firms in the last seven days. It pays **no fee to itself**. The firm is an ordinary firm under every law, the closing one included. Both numbers are badged `assumed` with a band and a `why` in `calibration/assumptions/founding_<country>.yaml`, written by the loader, rewritable by a society — the same badge the other three numbers of this lane wear.

**(a) The law runs in the same 00:00 tick as the exit law, straight after it, and before the daily seek.** The window, not the tick order, is what gives an agent first refusal: a premise that emptied at *this* midnight is on the walk and passed over, its vacancy being zero days old. What the placement buys is the other end — a firm the city founds at midnight has its slots in that morning's seek, so the town is not a day short of a grocer the city has already decided to open. Waiting a day would buy nothing the fourteen-day window does not already buy, and would cost the seek. `economy.lastResort` is a system of its own in `scheduler.ts`, between `economy.exit` and `economy.seek`.

**(b) The guard on the hard budget: the city founds only out of what stands above the week it owes.** `weekOwed` is a full week of the civic payroll at the founded slots' wages, at the upkeep the city pays over them, plus a week of pensions and dole at the standing rates for everyone out of work this minute; the city founds when `balance − capital ≥ weekOwed` and otherwise does not. Deliberately a *forward* commitment and not last week's outturn: the payday tally is a per-world reading that is never snapshotted (`labour.ts`), so a law that read it would answer differently after a restore, and every term of `weekOwed` is in the hash. One week and not four, because the hard budget already pays part wages and part dole when it is short and says so — the reserve exists to stop a founding *causing* that, not to make the treasury solvent for a quarter.

One test moved with (a) and is worth naming, because it is the law's shape showing up in an old assertion: `tests/exit.test.ts` checked that a closing adds exactly the remainder plus the final tax to the treasury in that tick. It no longer does — the city founds in the same tick, out of the same treasury — so the assertion now subtracts the opening money the city put up at that midnight. The exit law's own arithmetic is unchanged; what changed is that midnight has two laws in it.

**(c) The walk stops at the first premise it cannot pay for.** The city does not skip an expensive premise to found a cheap one further down the street; that is the state choosing ventures, the shape the principal rejected. Building order, and it stops — one line a day in the city log when it does. A refusal on the window or on the customer is not said at all: that is the ordinary state of a hundred vacant premises, and a line a day for each is not a log.

**(d) The city is written on the record as `founder: null`, not as an invented principal id.** The city holds no account in `world.agents.principals`, writes no mandate and takes no decision on the chain, so an id would point at nothing. `null` is the engine's own word for "no principal", and on `FoundingRecord.foundings` — where every row *is* a founding after day zero — it can mean only one thing. `foundedByCity()` is the reader; `Firm.founder` stays null too. The inspector's sentence reads **"founded on day 62 by the city with 2 slots"** where an agent's reads a resident's name.

**(e) A city founding is not written to the agent audit chain.** The chain records what agents did under mandates; the city holds none, and a row asserting an agent acted would have no fact behind it — jarvis11's standing reading of the founding gate (2026-09-09), applied. The founding record and the city log carry it.

**Reported, never judged.** `bench/validate.ts`'s treasury row gains three columns — firms the **city** founded, firms an **agent** founded, and the opening money the city put up — so the day agents outfound the city is visible, which is the number friday22 named as the one to watch. `checks.length` stays 6; no check reads any of the three.

**Measured, seed 42, a hundred and twenty days, after every fix.** *Lone Halvik:* both grocers close on day 48; the city founds a grocer the same midnight (8 slots, NHK 324.69 of opening money) and a second on day 55 (2 slots, NHK 80.05); those two close on days 90 and 104 and the city founds again in the premises the first pair left. Day 120: 39 firms open, **two grocers**, four closings, four city foundings, NHK 809.48 of capital put up, treasury NHK 7.91m → 4.10m, every civic wage and every dole paid in full on every sampled day. Before this law the same town ended with no grocer. *Federated:* Halvik 14 closings and 10 city foundings (NHK 3,681.44 of capital), Dorsk 13 and 11 (VYL 1,060.99), Amaranth City 8 and 9 (AMD 2,495.59); Halvik ends with one grocer (its second closed on day 118, inside its window), Dorsk two, Amaranth City three. Treasuries: Halvik NHK 8.35m → 4.28m, Dorsk VYL 3.20m → 1.01m, Amaranth City AMD 9.72m → 1.29m — the city's foundings are 3–9 % of each fall, so **the founder of last resort is not what drains the treasury**; the civic payroll against rent and tax is, as it was before this law. **Agent-founded: 0 in every city, as expected — no agent holds a founding policy until Phase 6.** The three pinned hashes are unmoved (lone `4873830dae251aa9`, two-city `7639d4888ee161c8`, three-city `fe373191d9abb9ca`): the law's first act is on day 48 and the pinned windows end on days 7 and 14.

**The thing this law does not do, and the reason to re-open it at Phase 6.** The window counts a **premise's vacancy**, not the **age of the opportunity**. So the first city founding lands on the *same midnight* as the closing that freed the customer — day 48 in every city measured — because the premise it takes has stood vacant since day zero and has long served its fourteen days; the window bites only on a premise that emptied recently. Under the law as written that is correct: nobody founded in that premise for forty-seven days. But an agent policy would have had no reason to found there before the customer existed, and "first refusal" means less than the number 14 suggests. Counting from the tick `canFound` first said yes would mean per-premise state on the hashed record; it was not built, and this is the item to weigh when a Phase 6 policy exists and the city-against-agent count is read for the first time.

**Superseded later the same day (2026-09-10): "[confirmed] The agent looks first", below.** Measured in the lone city, this is worse than the paragraph above states: a vacant premise with a nameable customer exists on **7 days of 120**, so there is no fortnight in which an agent could act and the window buys an agent nothing at all; the **per-week cap** is the only thing holding the city back. The principal's answer is order, not a longer or better-counted window: the founding policy runs in the same midnight, immediately before this walk. The window and the cap both stay as built.

**Reversal cost:** cheap for both numbers — two `param()` values with a `why`, three countries, and the pins move whenever a number does. Moderate for the law: it is the second founding path in the engine, it writes the founding record the restore rule reads, and it changes what a vacant premise means — an opportunity on a clock rather than one waiting indefinitely. Reversing it after Phase 6 means towns kept whole by the city start hollowing again, with every pin moving.

**Depends on:** "The server is the government of the city, and it intervenes under law" (2026-09-09) — the direction and the reading this builds; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" and its fail-closed amendment (2026-09-09) — the same `canFound`, the same price, and the first refusal the window protects; "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09) — the law the city's own firms are under; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — (b); "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09) — no fee to itself; "Civic is a department: the closing law does not reach the city's own firms" (2026-09-09) — this founds no civic kind and does not widen that branch; "A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms" (2026-09-09) — (d) writes the row it reads; "Idle hours are bought" (2026-09-09) — why the firms the city re-founds close again in their turn.

**[friday22 note]** Over a hundred and twenty days the city founded ten to eleven firms a city and agents founded none, which is exactly the shape the principal was warned of when this was recorded: until Phase 6 the last resort *is* the only resort, and every reading taken before then will look like the state as sole entrepreneur. The two things that keep it honest are in place and printed — the window and the one-a-week cap on one side, the city-against-agent count on the other. The churn is worth watching too: in the lone city the two firms the city founded on days 48 and 55 both closed within six weeks and were replaced, which is the closing law reading a customer the town cannot really pay for, not the founding law failing.

### 2026-09-10 — The ownership lane, measured — context, not a decision
tuesday22's reading, seed 42, a hundred and twenty days, taken before any ownership law was written. Recorded because all three answers below were put to the principal with it in hand.

**Money is conserved and piles up where nothing can draw on it.** In federated Halvik, private firm accounts went from **NHK 601,932 to 4,312,411** over 120 days while households fell **9.54m to 7.65m** and the treasury **6.52m to 4.28m**. Nothing is created or destroyed — what the firms hold is what the households and the treasury no longer do — and it accumulates at about **1.8× the private weekly wage bill every week**. A firm has an account, no owner, and therefore no claimant; the money stops there.

**It is not spread evenly, and the unevenness is what the closing law reads.** Nine of seventeen open private firms hold **NHK 3.93m above four weeks of wages** — one bar at **306 weeks**, the pharmacy at **113**. The other eight sit below four weeks, and those are the ones that close.

**The longer the world runs with no owners, the fewer agents can found.** Households able to afford the cheapest founding fall from **946 of 974 at day zero to 427 at day 120**. The entry law ("Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages", 2026-09-09) gets harder to use with every week that passes, because the money that would fund a founding is sitting in firm accounts.

**The fourteen-day window buys an agent nothing where it was measured.** In the lone city, a vacant premise whose `canFound` names a customer exists on **7 days of 120** — days 48 to 54. Everything else the city takes at the midnight the customer appears, the premise having stood empty since day zero. The **per-week cap** is what actually holds the city back; the window is not.

**Depends on:** "A closing firm pays final wages first; the rest goes to the treasury" (2026-09-09) — its own "When owners exist (Phase 5), the law changes to pay the owner"; "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built" (2026-09-10) — the window and the cap this measures; "Idle hours are bought" (2026-09-09) — why the eight short firms are short.

### 2026-09-10 — Who owns the day-zero firms — the principal's direction, read as: the founder is the owner, and null is the city

**The principal's answer, verbatim (typos intact): *"whatever option that benefits the residnet and teh city little by little dont gorget this economics policies hsould be so light that ecnonomys its own way should be very rewardign to be part of it long term to short term to longer term both for rresidnets to invite other agents as well as humans inviting other ( also tell me each section you are working and do everythign section by section to its easier for you"*. A direction, not a pick: none of the three options was chosen by name.**

**Put:** with the measurement above in hand — firm accounts holding NHK 4.31m nothing can draw on — three options, recommendation first: **the city** owns the day-zero firms (recommended); **nobody** owns them; they are **shared among the residents**.

**Read as [coordinator's interpretation, not the principal's words] — two halves:**
**(i) The founder is the owner, and `null` is the city.** One law, no special case: whoever founds owns, and the day-zero firms were founded by the city, so they are the city's. The `founder` field already exists and already reads `null` for a city founding ((d) of "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built", 2026-09-10); ownership is that same field read as a claim, not a new register.
**(ii) What the city draws as owner does not simply vanish into the treasury.** A **badged share** of it is paid straight out to **resident households, equally, as a standing dividend, month by month**; the rest funds the city's budget. That is what "benefits the resident and the city little by little" is read to mean — both sides get something, continuously, from the same draw.
It is the same principle as the fees-and-appreciation rule: **being part of this economy should pay** — in the short term and the long. The dividend is the "appreciation" of "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09) given a form, and it is what "very rewardign to be part of it" is read as asking for.

**Over:** nothing was rejected by name — the principal answered none of the three options. **Nobody owns** is what the world already is, and the measurement above is what it produces. **Shared among residents** is not discarded so much as absorbed: the reading gives residents the *flow* without splitting the *title*, because a title split 974 ways is a register the engine would have to carry and a claim on every closing settlement.

**Because:** the principal's — whichever option benefits the resident **and** the city, little by little; the economic policies are to be **light**; the economy should find its own way; being part of it should be **very rewarding, short term to long**, for residents, so that residents invite other agents and humans invite others.

**Reversal cost:** moderate. The owner field is cheap — it exists. The dividend is not: it is a standing monthly outflow from the treasury to every resident household, it moves every household's income and therefore rent-to-income and Engel, **every pin moves**, and taking it away later is taking money off households that have had it. The `null`-is-the-city reading is cheap to reverse while agents own nothing and expensive after they own something, because reversing it re-opens who owned what in between.

**Depends on:** "A closing firm pays final wages first; the rest goes to the treasury" (2026-09-09) — this is the Phase 5 its own sentence names, "When owners exist (Phase 5), the law changes to pay the owner"; "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09) — the same principle, and the dividend is its "appreciation" made concrete; "The server is the government of the city, and it intervenes under law" (2026-09-09) — a government that owns and pays out is still under a law; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" (2026-09-09) — the founder whose founding now confers a title; "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built" (2026-09-10) — (d), `founder: null`; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — the dividend is paid from what is drawn, not created; D-1 (2026-09-06).

**A working instruction, recorded as such and not as an economic one.** The second half of the message — *"also tell me each section you are working and do everythign section by section to its easier for you"* — is how the principal wants the work run: **name the section being worked on, and proceed one section at a time.** It applies to every lane from here, not only this one.

**Status:** landed and committed 2026-09-10/11 — the built shape, jarvis11's findings and the hundred-and-twenty-day reading are "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11), below.

**[friday22 note]** Three things to keep visible. **First**, the reading is the coordinator's, and it is a merge of two of the three options offered — the city holds the title, the residents get the flow. If the principal meant literal shared ownership, this entry is wrong in a way only he can say, and it should be said before the dividend is built. **Second**, the dividend's **share and cadence are numbers nobody has chosen**; they are recorded as the coordinator's to settle and badge (Open (xiv), below), and a share set high enough turns the treasury's owner-draw into a transfer programme, which is a fiscal law and not a settlement. **Third**, a city that owns every firm and pays a dividend from their surplus is, in every reading taken before agents own anything, a state that owns the economy and distributes it — the same shape the last-resort law was warned about, one layer up. The tell is the same: the day agents own firms, the draw stops being all the city's. Print the owner-draw split — city against agents — beside the founding count.

**[friday22 note — resolved 2026-09-10, later the same day.]** **First** is resolved: the merge was put to the principal as a question, in those words, with literal shared ownership and the city keeping it all as the alternatives, and he chose the merge by name — "[confirmed] The city holds the day-zero firms; residents get the flow", below. The reading above is now the principal's, not the coordinator's. **Second** is resolved: the share was not settled by the coordinator — it was put to the principal with the measured arithmetic and he chose fifteen percent, with fifty percent named as the transfer-programme line this note drew — "[confirmed] The residents' dividend is fifteen percent of the city's draw", below; the cadence is the month's turn the draw already has. **Third** stands, and the measurement bore it out before the dividend existed: the draw run as a throwaway at a hundred percent to the treasury moved not one cent to a resident (household totals unchanged to the cent, lone Halvik treasury NHK 4.10m → 7.78m). The dividend is what turns that shape into the principal's "rewarding to be part of"; the city-against-agents split stays the tell.
