### 2026-09-10 — [confirmed] The owner draws everything above four weeks of wages, monthly

**A firm's owner draws everything its account holds above four weeks of that firm's own wage bill, once a month. Below four weeks it draws nothing.** Put with the measurement above, recommendation first: how much does an owner draw, and how often? The principal chose the recommended option: **"Everything above four weeks of wages, monthly (Recommended)"**.

**Found:** nine of seventeen private firms in federated Halvik hold NHK 3.93m above four weeks of wages, one bar at 306 weeks and the pharmacy at 113, while the same city's households and treasury both fall — money conserved, and stopped.

**Over:** **a share of the profit each month** — a percentage nobody voted for, and "profit" is a quantity the engine does not define and would have to; **the owner decides each month** — more agentic, and the honest objection to it is that with one fitted rule answering for every owner it is a rule pretending to be a choice. Not discarded: worth revisiting when agents own enough firms for the difference between their choices to show (Open (xv), below).

**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — **four weeks** is the same four a founder puts up as opening money and the same four short Fridays that close a firm, so **one number serves three laws**; **monthly** because rents already move on the month's turn, so the world already has that turn, and a monthly draw falls on **day 31, outside all three pinned windows** (which end on days 7 and 14), where a weekly draw falls in week one and moves every pin.

**The warning the question carried, and it is being measured before the draw is built.** Draining bars and the pharmacy from 100–300 weeks of wages down to four **removes the cushion that is keeping them open**. The closing law will bite harder, and it should — that is the law reading a customer the town cannot really pay for — but how much harder is a number, not a guess. The measurement compares closings at 120 days against today's: **4 in the lone city, 14/13/8 federated.**

**Measured later the same day (2026-09-10), and the warning did not hold.** Section 2 of the lane ran the draw as a throwaway over a hundred and twenty days, seed 42. Draining every firm to four weeks moved closings from **4 to 5** in the lone city and from **14/13/8 to 13/14/8** federated — inside noise. The reason is the shape the ownership measurement already showed: the firms holding a hundred weeks of wages (bars, the pharmacy, offices) are not the ones that close, and the ones that close are already below four weeks, so the draw takes nothing from them. The cushion the warning feared removing was never keeping anyone open. What the draw does is **reverse the treasury's fall** — lone Halvik NHK 4.10m → 7.78m at day 120, Dorsk VYL 1.01m → 2.68m — by moving money out of firm accounts, and, as specified with no dividend and no agent owner, **not one cent reaches a resident**: household totals unchanged to the cent. That reading is what the two entries below were put with — "[confirmed] The city holds the day-zero firms; residents get the flow" and "[confirmed] The residents' dividend is fifteen percent of the city's draw".

**Reversal cost:** cheap for the number and the cadence — two `param()` values with a `why`, badged like the rest of this lane. Moderate for the law: the draw is the **first standing outflow from a firm to a household that is not a wage**, and every pin moves once the money starts moving on day 31.

**Depends on:** "Who owns the day-zero firms" (2026-09-10, above) — the owner this draws for, and the dividend the city's draw funds; "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09) — the same four weeks, and the law this makes bite harder; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" (2026-09-09) — the same four weeks again; "Rents move two percent a month, a badged number" (2026-09-09) — the month's turn this shares; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — money moves, none is made; "Idle hours are bought" (2026-09-09) — why a firm can be short of four weeks while its slots are full; "A closing firm pays final wages first; the rest goes to the treasury" (2026-09-09) — the closing settlement the owner's claim now sits behind.

**Status:** landed and committed 2026-09-10/11 — the built shape, jarvis11's findings and the hundred-and-twenty-day reading are "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11), below.

**[friday22 note]** Four weeks is now load-bearing in three laws — founding capital, closing threshold, owner's retention. That is the argument for it and also its risk: the day the principal moves one of the three, the log must say which of the three moved and which two did not, or the number quietly means three different things. And a firm drained to exactly four weeks every month is a firm that can never build a cushion for a bad quarter; if the closing count jumps, that is the reading, not a fault in the exit law.

### 2026-09-10 — [confirmed] The agent looks first

**The founding policy runs in the same midnight, immediately before the city's walk of last resort. An agent that can found gets the premise; the city takes what is left.** Put with the measurement above, recommendation first: does an agent's founding policy run before or after the city's last-resort walk? The principal chose the recommended option: **"The agent looks first (Recommended)"**.

**Found:** the fourteen-day window was written to give an agent **first refusal** and, as built, does not. The premise the city takes has stood empty since day zero, so its window expired long before a customer existed, and the city takes it **the same midnight** the customer appears. In the lone city a vacant premise with a nameable customer exists on **7 days of 120** — days 48–54; there is no fortnight in which an agent could have acted at all.

**Over:** **leave the order and let agents have the backlog** — 34 vacant premises stand in federated Halvik by day 120, which is a real backlog, but the city keeps taking the **first** of everything and the treasury row keeps reading **city 10, agents 0** for the wrong reason: not because agents chose not to found, but because they were never asked.

**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — first refusal has to be an **order**, not a delay, when the delay has already run out before the opportunity exists; and the city calling itself the founder of last resort has to mean something was asked before it.

**This supersedes the "the window is weaker than its number" note** recorded with the last-resort law — "The thing this law does not do, and the reason to re-open it at Phase 6", in "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built" (2026-09-10, above). It supersedes it **not** by the fix that note weighed (counting the age of the opportunity instead of the premise's vacancy, which would put per-premise state on the hashed record and was not built) but **by order**: the agent policy is asked first, in the same tick. The window and the per-week cap both stay — the cap is what the measurement shows actually holds the city back.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — one system's position in `scheduler.ts`, ahead of `economy.lastResort` instead of behind it; moderate in readings — it is the first time an agent's policy runs ahead of the city in a tick, and it changes which of the two ends up owning the firms measured from then on.

**Depends on:** "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built" (2026-09-10) — (a), the tick order this changes, and the note this supersedes; "The server is the government of the city, and it intervenes under law" (2026-09-09) — last resort, now in fact last; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" and its fail-closed amendment (2026-09-09) — only an `allow` founds, so an agent asked first can still be refused and the city still follows in the same midnight; "Who owns the day-zero firms" (2026-09-10, above) — who owns what the agent takes; (xiii) of "Open after the sizing landed" (2026-09-09) — the Phase 6 first refusal it left open, answered in order rather than in delay.

**Status:** landed and committed 2026-09-10/11 — the built shape, jarvis11's findings and the hundred-and-twenty-day reading are "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11), below.

**[friday22 note]** Order gives first refusal only if a policy exists to take it. Until the Phase 6 founding policy is built, "the agent looks first" is an empty step and the readings will still say **city N, agents 0** — the same warning as before, moved one line earlier in the tick. The number that shows this worked is **agent-founded greater than zero**, and it cannot move until a policy ships. Do not let the ordering stand in for the policy.

**[friday22 note — resolved 2026-09-11.]** The policy shipped late on 2026-09-10 and the number moved: at day 120 the treasury row reads city 0–1, agents 10–16 in every city — lone Halvik 4 to 0, and Dorsk's one city founding came only after both nearest residents refused. The ordering did not stand in for the policy. What the count does not yet show is whether residents *chose* to found — most yeses are the fitted rule's — recorded under "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11).

### 2026-09-10 — [confirmed] The city holds the day-zero firms; residents get the flow

**The city owns the firms it founded on day zero and after; what it draws from them each month is split, part to resident households and part to the city's budget. An agent who founds a firm owns it outright.** This confirms the `[coordinator's interpretation]` under "Who owns the day-zero firms" (2026-09-10, above), which friday22's note there flagged as a merge of two of the three options the principal had been offered. The merge was put back to the principal as a question, in these words: *"I read your direction as: the city holds the day-one firms, and residents get a share of what the city draws from them. That merges two of the three options I offered you, so I should check I read you right."* Options, recommendation first: **the city holds them and residents get the flow** (recommended); **residents own the day-one firms outright**; **the city keeps it all**. The principal chose the recommended option by name: **"City holds them, residents get the flow (Recommended)"**.

**Found:** section 2 of the lane, the draw run as a throwaway at a hundred percent to the treasury, seed 42, a hundred and twenty days. The treasury's fall reverses — lone Halvik NHK 4.10m → 7.78m, Dorsk VYL 1.01m → 2.68m — and **not one cent reaches a resident**: household totals unchanged to the cent, every firm being the city's while no agent policy exists. That is the state-owns-the-economy shape friday22's note warned of, made visible before the dividend was built. Closings did not move (recorded under "The owner draws everything above four weeks of wages, monthly", above).

**Over:** **residents own the day-one firms outright** — a title split 974 ways is a register the engine would have to carry and a claim on every closing settlement, and it makes day zero a special case of the founding law where the founder is not the owner; **the city keeps it all** — the measured state above, money moved out of firm accounts into a treasury nothing pays back out of, which is the shape the principal's "benefits the resident and the city little by little" cannot mean.

**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — **one law with no special case**: the founder owns what they founded, and the city founded these; what the city draws each month is split, part to residents and part to the city's budget; residents get income without owning what they did not build; and an agent who founds a firm owns it outright, **which is the reward for founding**.

**Reversal cost:** moderate, as recorded under "Who owns the day-zero firms" — the owner field is cheap because it exists; the flow is a standing monthly outflow to every household that moves rent-to-income and Engel and is taken off households once given. What this confirmation adds: the `null`-is-the-city reading is now the principal's and not the coordinator's, so reversing it is reversing a decision and not correcting an interpretation — and once an agent owns a firm, reversing it re-opens who owned what in between.

**Depends on:** "Who owns the day-zero firms — the principal's direction, read as: the founder is the owner, and null is the city" (2026-09-10) — the reading this confirms, and the objection it resolves; "The ownership lane, measured" (2026-09-10) — the money that piles up where nothing can draw on it; "[confirmed] The owner draws everything above four weeks of wages, monthly" (2026-09-10) — the draw the flow comes from, and the measurement that found no cent reaching a resident; "A closing firm pays final wages first; the rest goes to the treasury" (2026-09-09) — its own "When owners exist (Phase 5), the law changes to pay the owner", which section 4 of this lane now builds; "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09) — the flow is its "appreciation" given a form; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" (2026-09-09) — the founding whose reward is outright ownership; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — the flow is paid from what is drawn; D-1 (2026-09-06).

**Status:** landed and committed 2026-09-10/11 — the built shape, jarvis11's findings and the hundred-and-twenty-day reading are "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11), below.

**[friday22 note]** Objection withdrawn: the question was put and answered. What remains to watch is the shape the confirmation itself creates. "The founder owns what they founded" is one law only while the city's foundings and an agent's are settled the same way — the same draw, the same closing settlement paying the same owner. If section 4 pays an agent-owner on closing and pays the city something different, or if the city's firms are ever exempted from the draw the way civic firms are, the special case has come back in through the settlement. The reading that shows this law is what it says is one where an agent-founded firm and a city-founded firm of the same kind close in the same month and the log shows the same arithmetic for both.

### 2026-09-10 — [confirmed] The residents' dividend is fifteen percent of the city's draw

**Fifteen percent of what the city draws as owner each month is paid straight to resident households, equally; the rest funds the city's budget.** Put with the measured arithmetic, recommendation first: how much of what the city draws each month goes straight to residents? The city draws about **NHK 1.2m a month** in Halvik across **974 households**. Options: **fifteen percent** (recommended — about NHK 190 a month per household in Halvik, VYL 120 in Dorsk, AMD 290 in Amaranth City; felt, about a fifteenth of what a household holds, accumulating toward the NHK 3,400 a founding costs, not the main income); **fifty percent** (a transfer programme — a Dorsk household would receive 0.74× its median balance every month, and it would be most households' main income); **five percent** (too small to change what anyone can do). The principal chose the recommended option: **"Fifteen percent (Recommended)"**.

**Over:** **fifty percent** — past the line Open (xiv) drew, at which the owner-draw is mostly a transfer to households and the settlement has become a fiscal law; **five percent** — felt by nobody, and a dividend nobody feels does not make being here "rewarding to be part of".

**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — felt without becoming the main income: a household's income stays its wage, the dividend accumulates toward what a founding costs, and so it is the short-term reward that funds the long-term one.

**As landed.** `assumptions/founding_<country>/owner_dividend_share` = 0.15 in each country's calibration bundle, badged `assumed` with a band and a `why` — `assumed` in the calibration sense, meaning not read from a dataset and rewritable by a society; the choice of the number is the principal's, which is why this entry is `[confirmed]`. Paid at the **month's turn**, the same turn the draw and the rents already have. The mechanics below were not put to the principal and are the builder's, tagged: **[assumed]** paid **equally to every resident household**, which is the reading under "Who owns the day-zero firms" the principal has now confirmed; **[assumed]** **integer cents**, the remainder after division staying with the treasury, so the conservation check holds to the cent; **[assumed]** **civic firms are exempt from the draw**, as departments — the same branch as "Civic is a department: the closing law does not reach the city's own firms" (2026-09-09), applied to the draw.

**Reversal cost:** cheap for the number — one `param()` value with a `why`, three countries, and the pinned hashes at days 7 and 14 never see a day-31 payment; every hundred-and-twenty-day reading moves. Moderate for the law — it is the first standing outflow from the treasury to every household that is not a wage, a dole or a pension, and taking it away later is taking money off households that have had it. Raising it is cheaper than lowering it for the same reason.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] The city holds the day-zero firms; residents get the flow" (2026-09-10, above) — the flow this is the size of; "[confirmed] The owner draws everything above four weeks of wages, monthly" (2026-09-10) — the draw this is a share of, and the month's turn it shares; "The ownership lane, measured" (2026-09-10) — the arithmetic the options were priced with; "Civic is a department: the closing law does not reach the city's own firms" (2026-09-09) — the exemption; "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — paid out of what is drawn, never created; "Rents move two percent a month, a badged number" (2026-09-09) — the month's turn; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" (2026-09-09) — the NHK 3,400 the dividend accumulates toward; "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09) — the reward side of that rule; Open (xiv) (2026-09-09 block, updated 2026-09-10) — closed by this entry.

**Status:** landed and committed 2026-09-10/11 — the built shape, jarvis11's findings and the hundred-and-twenty-day reading are "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11), below.

**[friday22 note]** Two readings to keep printed. **First**, fifteen percent is a share of a surplus, not of a wage bill: the draw is whatever stands above four weeks, so in a month the firms are short the dividend is small, and it must never be reported to residents as an income they can count on. **Second**, the dividend is built to shrink. The day agents own firms, those firms' draw goes to their owners and not to the city, and the city's draw — and the dividend with it — falls. That is the design, not a fault: the first reading in which the dividend falls *because* agents own more is the reading that says the lane worked. Print the draw split — city against agents — beside the founding count, as the note under "Who owns the day-zero firms" asked; and note that at eighty-five percent to the treasury the owner-draw still reverses the treasury's fall, which means the hard budget's part-wages-part-dole branch may not fire again in these readings. A city that no longer runs short because it lives off its firms is a reading to say out loud, not a check passing.

### 2026-09-11 — [assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read
Taken by the coordinator on 2026-09-10 — the lane landed late that day; recorded 2026-09-11 — after jarvis11 read the landed ownership lane: the owner seam, the monthly draw, the dividend, the closing that pays its owner, and the Phase 6 founding policy that "The agent looks first" (2026-09-10) placed ahead of the city's walk. Each item measured or verified before it was kept; none put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation. (a) and (b) are the two that are not routine; (e) is a correction.

**(a) The mind's yes is a single-use *payment* mandate for exactly capital plus fee, five ticks to live — not an intent plus a widened standing limit.** `authorize` escalates every non-payment request of a posture-zero principal, and a spotlight is appointed only at posture zero ("[assumed] A spotlight is a principal at posture 0", 2026-09-07), so under the other shape a spotlight could never have founded. Tested against the real gate, not a stub. **This corrects the forecast in the founding amendment** ("Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages", amendment, 2026-09-09), which said a Phase 6 policy would have to take the shape `city/bench/found.ts` takes — a mandate for the founding category *and* a widened per-transaction limit. It does not: one mandate, for one sum, for one act, and the standing limit is not widened. `city/src/sim/systems/economy/foundpolicy.ts`.

**(b) The engine derives the principal's act from its own context and refuses, saying so, a mind whose copy of the act differs.** The policy is the first place in the engine where a mind's answer moves a household's money into a firm, and as first built the engine signed whatever draft the verdict carried. A mind proposes; the runtime commits. The class is `[[authority-edge-fails-open]]` (`docs/LESSONS.md`, named from jarvis11's exit-and-entry read), found again at the newest edge — the one that moves the largest sum an agent can move.

**(c) Eligibility, and the order of asking.** A resident is asked when all hold: working age; housed; the household can afford capital plus fee; not asked today; not already the founder of an open firm — **one firm per founder for now**. Residents are asked nearest-first by home-to-premise distance, ties by id. A no writes nothing — no row, no mandate, no log line.

**(d) The fitted rule founds when the household holds capital, fee, a month's rent and a month's food.** This is the rule that answers for every resident who is not a spotlight. Not put to the principal.

**(e) A household-owner's receipts are income — a correction.** The monthly draw, a closing's remainder and a swept tombstone count as the founder household's income for Engel and rent-to-income exactly as the dividend does, and none of them touches the week's wages the bills read. Before this fix a founder household read *poorer* the better its firm did. `city/src/sim/systems/economy/owner.ts`.

**(f) A founder whose resident is gone leaves the firm the city's, as unclaimed property.** The inspector says so rather than calling it a firm the city laid out; `founder: null` still means the city, and the inspector's sentence keeps the two cases apart.

**(g) The founding turn is skipped by tick.** Day one, 09:00, is a month's turn by the calendar; a draw there would move opening money, not earnings, inside every pinned window. The first month's turn is day 31.

**(h) What the chain and the founding record keep.** The decision row on the agent chain names the mandate it is about to write by reference and carries a verdict the feed can show; the hashed founding row keeps which mind decided and on what engine, so "which mind founded this" survives the audit ring rolling over.

**(i) Fifteen tests in four files that asserted the world before the policy are held still with a mind that declines every founding.** The mind is an input and never state — `restore` now takes one as `createWorld` does (`city/src/sim/core/world.ts`) — so each test asserts its own law's sentence with its premise stated: with residents who decline every founding, the city founds.

**Measured, a hundred and twenty days, seed 42, after every fix.** *Lone Halvik:* four foundings, all by residents, none by the city — two grocer premises the midnight they close on day 48, two more on day 97; five asked, one refused, short after rent and food. Dividends NHK 210, 195, 180 per household on days 31, 61, 91. A median household holds 2.9k where it held 2.4k, and 460 households can afford a founding where 427 could — the whole gain the dividend's, none the draw's. *Federated:* Halvik 12 foundings by residents and 0 by the city; Dorsk 16 by residents and one by the city — a dairy on day 76, after both nearest residents refused; Amaranth City 10 and 0. The treasury row's tell moved from **city 10, agents 0** to **city 0–1, agents 10–16**. **Founding is a loss for the founder today:** the firms residents found are staffed the next morning and close from day 77 as wages outrun revenue; a founder draws nothing and loses the stake — the mean-field customer overstating who pays (Open (i)/(v), 2026-09-09), printed now in a founder's purse rather than the treasury's. Three pins unmoved; the fake recording verifies unchanged; 729 tests.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code for every item — a mandate shape, a refusal, an eligibility list, a rule's threshold, an income line, an inspector sentence, a tick check, two record fields, a test input. Expensive as positions for (a) and (b), in the way the founding amendment was: a standing limit widened for a founding is a limit left widened, and an engine that signs a mind's draft has put a claim on the chain it did not derive. Moderate as readings for (c) and (d): who is eligible, in what order and at what threshold decides who the 10–16 founders are, and every hundred-and-twenty-day reading moves with them — the pins do not, because the first founding is on day 48. (g) reversed moves every pin.

**Depends on:** the five ownership entries of 2026-09-10 — "Who owns the day-zero firms", "[confirmed] The owner draws everything above four weeks of wages, monthly", "[confirmed] The agent looks first", "[confirmed] The city holds the day-zero firms; residents get the flow", "[confirmed] The residents' dividend is fifteen percent of the city's draw" — the lane this lands; "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built" (2026-09-10) — the walk the policy now precedes, and the `founder: null` that (f) reads; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" and its fail-closed amendment (2026-09-09) — only an `allow` founds, and (a) corrects the amendment's forecast; "[assumed] A spotlight is a principal at posture 0" (2026-09-07) — why (a) had to be a payment; "Gemini is a fourth engine; models are recorded once and replayed" (2026-09-07) — the recording (h) and the measurement verify against; "A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms" (2026-09-09) — the record (h) extends; "A closing firm pays final wages first; the rest goes to the treasury" (2026-09-09) — its Phase 5 sentence, now paying the owner, whose remainder (e) counts as income; "Idle hours are bought" (2026-09-09) — why a founder's fully staffed firm closes; Open (i)/(v) (2026-09-09) — the mean-field customer the measurement finds again.

**[friday22 note]** Four things to keep visible. **First**, the tell moved: "agent-founded greater than zero" was the number every note in this lane said to watch, and it reads 10–16 against 0–1 — the founder of last resort is, for the first time, last. **Second**, most of those foundings are the fitted rule's, (d), not a mind's: one rule answering the founding question for every resident who is not a spotlight is the "rule pretending to be a choice" shape Open (xv) names, one step earlier than the draw. The count says residents outfound the city; it does not yet say residents *chose* to. **Third**, the reward for founding — outright ownership, "which is the reward for founding" (2026-09-10) — is, as measured, a stake lost: a resident pays about NHK 3,400 to open a firm that is staffed the next morning and closes within a month, and no founder has drawn a cent. That is not the policy failing; it is `canFound` naming a customer who does not pay, the same gap Open (i)/(v) recorded, and it now costs a resident where it cost the treasury. Whether the world should keep asking residents to found under an entry law whose customer is overstated is the principal's kind of question, not an engine reading; it is raised here and not tagged. **Fourth**, the dividend's promised reading has not arrived: it was built to fall the day agents own firms, and agents now own 10–16 firms a city while the dividend is unchanged, because the firms they own never hold above four weeks. The day a founder's firm draws is the day both the dividend's fall and (xv) become live.

**[friday22 note — resolved 2026-09-11, the same day.]** **Third** is resolved: the question it raised — whether the world should keep asking residents to found under an entry law whose customer is overstated — was put to the principal with this measurement, and he closed the door rather than print the losses or shrink the stake: "[confirmed] The law must name a customer who pays enough", below. **First**, **Second** and **Fourth** stand, and are readings to take again when that lane lands: fewer foundings move the tell; the fitted rule still answers for every resident who is not a spotlight (Open (xv)); and the dividend's fall waits on a founder's firm holding above four weeks, which a founding that is not a loss makes possible for the first time.

### 2026-09-11 — [confirmed] The law must name a customer who pays enough

**Before a premise is offered to anyone, the entry law checks that what the named customer actually pays — read from the firms of that kind already trading — covers the wages at least four weeks in four. A premise whose customers do not pay enough is not offered. Fewer foundings, honest ones, and a founder's stake has a chance.** Put to the principal with the ownership landing's reading in hand, recommendation first: residents now found firms, and every one they found closes within a month — the law says a customer exists, but the customer pays about half the wages, so the founder loses the NHK 3,400 they put up and draws nothing; the city used to bear that, now a resident does; the law is asking people to found into a loss. The principal chose the recommended option: **"The law must name a customer who pays enough (Recommended)"**.

**Found:** "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11, above): 10–16 resident foundings a city over a hundred and twenty days, every firm staffed the next morning and closing from day 77 as wages outran revenue, no founder drawing a cent. The same customer the founder of last resort had founded to — the lone city's day-48 and day-55 grocers, closed within six weeks ("[assumed] The founder of last resort, built", 2026-09-10) — the overstatement Open (i)/(v) recorded, moved from the treasury's purse to a resident's.

**Over:** **leave it and print the losses** — the fitted rule founds whenever the household can afford it, so the losses repeat every month, and (xvi)'s shape — found, lose the stake, found again — becomes the world's steady state; **fewer slots and a lower stake** — shrinks the cost of the overstatement without fixing it: the customer still pays half. Neither taken.

**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — the founding lane's law names a customer from a mean-field share of the town's income, the shape that founded the day-zero shops to a customer who paid half ("Firms are founded to their customers", 2026-09-09; (b) of "[assumed] Founding to customers, landed — five choices made while measuring", 2026-09-09); it was the same overstatement, printed first in the treasury's purse through the founder of last resort and now in a resident's; and the evidence the world already holds — the books of the firms of that kind, open or dead — is what the law will read before it offers a premise.

**Mechanics — the coordinator's, `[assumed]`, to be recorded as built when the lane lands:** the evidence is the open firms of the kind at least four weeks old, their cumulative revenue against cumulative wages; failing any, the most recently closed firms of the kind, whose tombstones keep their books; a kind no firm has ever traded in falls back to the arithmetic, with the absence of evidence said. `canFound` is the one test both founding paths ask — a resident's policy and the city's walk read the same `entryAnswer` ((a) of "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built", 2026-09-10) — so "anyone" is read as both, and the city's walk stands behind the same door.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — one test at the entry law's door, reading books the ledger and the closing record already keep. Moderate in readings — which premises are offered decides who founds, what closes and what the treasury row reads, so every hundred-and-twenty-day reading moves; the pinned windows end on days 7 and 14 and the first offer after day zero fell on day 48 in every measured city, so the pins are expected to stand — a measurement to take when it lands, not a claim. As a position, reversing it re-opens the door the principal closed: asking a resident to put up a stake the books say will be lost.

**Depends on:** "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" and its fail-closed amendment (2026-09-09) — the `canFound` this tightens; "Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09) — the payer test, and the mean-field customer it names; "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built" (2026-09-10) — the same `entryAnswer`, now behind the same door; "[confirmed] The agent looks first" (2026-09-10) — the order the door stands in; "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11) — the reading this answers; "[confirmed] The city holds the day-zero firms; residents get the flow" (2026-09-10) — outright ownership as the reward for founding, which this gives a chance of being worth something; "A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09) — the four weeks the evidence is read against, and the closing record whose tombstones the fallback reads; "Idle hours are bought" (2026-09-09) — why wages outrun revenue in a fully staffed firm; "The idle share is not the concern; the laws are" (2026-09-09) — a law at the door, not a better estimate; Open (i)/(v) (2026-09-09) — answered here.

**Status:** building now (2026-09-11). Resolves the third point of the `[friday22 note]` under "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11, above). Answers the founding lane's Open (i)/(v) at the entry law's door rather than by a better estimate; the Open note is updated in place.

**[friday22 note]** Three things to keep visible. **First**, four weeks now carries a fourth law — the age a firm must reach to count as evidence, and the cover the customer must show — on top of founding capital, the closing threshold and the owner's retention (the note under "The owner draws everything above four weeks of wages, monthly", 2026-09-10): if any of the four moves, the log must say which. **Second**, the door as stated reads revenue against wages; the closing law reads what is left after stock is paid for. Open (x) recorded grocers earning 1.4× their wages while holding 0.0–0.5 weeks at day 30, and the lone city's two grocers closed on day 48 all the same — a reselling kind can pass this door and still close. Whether the evidence is read net of goods bought is the coordinator's to say when the mechanics land, and tag. **Third**, the fallback is the old door: a kind no firm has ever traded in is still offered on the mean-field customer, and its first founder — a resident, unless every resident declines and the city follows — bears exactly the overstatement this entry closes elsewhere; and a tombstone's books never change, so a kind whose every firm died under the old law is not offered again in that city until something re-reads it. Neither is decided; both are readings to print beside the founding count.

### 2026-09-11 — Phase 6 under D-2, planned, and the grocer's shelf measured — context, not a decision

tuesday22 planned Phase 6 under D-2 (the Architect Mode table, below) and measured the grocer's shelf before anything was built. **Found:** 62–64 % of a grocer's units sell at the day-old price; a grocer bins a quarter to a third of the stock it paid for; and the loss begins at the **producer** — milk leaves the dairy already day-old on 70 % of arrivals, because a farm's bin is one blended quality too. A two-bin fix at the grocer alone sells *less* on the same draw. **The correction, the coordinator's, `[assumed]`:** lots at every firm, binned at source, with the dispatch order — oldest-first or freshest-first — measured and not assumed (Open (xix), below). It is a model artefact under D-2's scaffolding clause, the same class as the twice-paid shops ("The twice-paid shops keep both payments until the sizing lands", 2026-09-09): a fiction in the model, not a law, corrected because it misreads the books. The below-cost day-old sale is small — 5–7 % of revenue — and is the owner's decision (the entry below). The shelf is a third to a half of the grocers' deficit; the rest is the customer who pays half, answered at the door ("The law must name a customer who pays enough", 2026-09-11, above).

**Also found:** firms have no principal and no agent today; every firm-to-firm payment is a bare transfer. Of the world brief's 22 rules, about eight are enforced with tests, four partial, three stubs, seven absent — the table is in tuesday22's plan and belongs in the notes, not here. **A correction:** the standing fixture for `[[authority-edge-fails-open]]` (`city/tests/doors.test.ts`, 43 cases; the class named in `docs/LESSONS.md`) was written and on its first run found a **fifth** instance — the closing law did not refuse a firm already closed. Fixed with the fixture; recorded because a class found five times is a class, not a run of accidents.

Four questions from the plan were put to the principal; all four answered `[confirmed]`, the four entries below. Three took the recommendation; one — the newcomer's first money — did not.

---

### 2026-09-11 — [confirmed] The owner decides whether to sell below cost

**An owner may give its firm's agent a standing order never to sell below what the firm paid. A firm nobody owns keeps today's rule. The engine's rule is the default; the law is what an owner acts under.** Put with the shelf measurement above, recommendation first: every shop sells day-old stock at 62 % of list, even below what it paid — under D-2, who decides? The principal chose the recommended option: **"The owner decides (Recommended)"**.

**Chose:** an owner's standing order to its firm's agent may say never below what we paid; a firm nobody owns — a city firm, `founder: null` — keeps today's rule; a resident owner who is not a spotlight gets today's default from the fitted rule, **so nothing pretends to be a choice**; the engine's rule is the default and the law is what an owner acts under. This is Open (xv) made concrete for one decision.

**Over:** **the engine forbids it for every firm** — a hand-built economic rule of the kind D-2 ends, and a wrong one for a shop that would rather sell at a loss than bin; **the fitted rule chooses for every owner** — residents would read as having chosen what a rule wrote, the "rule pretending to be a choice" shape recorded under "The owner draws everything above four weeks of wages, monthly" (2026-09-10). Neither taken.

**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — under D-2 a firm's pricing is the owner's, not the engine's; the engine keeps a default so an unowned or unasked firm still trades; and an owner's order is a mandate its agent acts under, which is the governance layer doing what it is for.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — one clause in a firm's operating mandate and one branch at the shelf. Moderate in position: it is the **first decision an owner holds over its own firm**, and taking it back is taking a right off owners who have used it. In readings, small either way — the below-cost sale is 5–7 % of a grocer's revenue.

**Depends on:** D-2 (2026-09-10, the Architect Mode table) — the clause this applies; Open (xv) (2026-09-09 block) — the question this makes concrete for one decision; "[confirmed] The city holds the day-zero firms; residents get the flow" (2026-09-10) — who the owner is, and `null` as the city; (d) of "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11) — the fitted rule that answers for a resident who is not a spotlight; "[assumed] A spotlight is a principal at posture 0" (2026-09-07) — who is asked; "The law must name a customer who pays enough" (2026-09-11) — the larger half of the grocers' deficit, answered elsewhere; the shelf measurement above.

**Status:** building now (2026-09-11), Phase 6 under D-2: the shelf's lots, the firm's agent and operating mandate, admission.

**[friday22 note]** Two readings. **First**, the tell for this entry is not the sale price; it is **whether any owner's order ever differs from the default**. If every owner's agent inherits the fitted default, (xv) has been named and not made concrete, and the count to print is orders-that-differ against firms-owned. **Second**, do not let a below-cost ban stand in for the grocers' rescue: the shelf is a third to a half of the deficit, the customer who pays half is the rest, and an owner who forbids the day-old sale bins more — the two-bin measurement already showed the shelf sells less on the same draw.

### 2026-09-11 — [confirmed] A newcomer's first money is a welcome credit from the city, a month of the median wage

**An admitted agent's household starts with a credit from the city equal to one month of the median wage — about NHK 2,900 in Halvik: rent, food and a cushion — paid from the city's share of the owner draw, badged, and a society may change it.** Put with the plan, in two steps. First: money is conserved and the federation is the world, so an admitted agent cannot bring money in — what is its first money? Options, recommendation first: **nothing; a job the next morning** (recommended); **a small welcome credit from the city**; **a stake from outside**. The principal chose **"A small welcome credit from the city"** — **the recommendation was not taken.** Second, asked how much: **two weeks of the median wage** (recommended); **a month**; **one founding's worth** (about NHK 3,400). The principal chose **"A month of the median wage"** — **again above the recommendation.**

**Over:** **nothing** — the recommendation's ground was that a household of one is hired the next morning under the seeking law and needs nothing from the city, and that a credit is a fiscal law with a number; **a stake from outside** — breaks conservation, the outside account "The federation is the world" (2026-09-08) removed; **two weeks** — the recommended size; **one founding's worth** — a credit that funds a founding on arrival, before a newcomer has traded anything. The first and third were the recommendations; neither was taken.

**Because:** the principal's, not the recommendation's. This is the **second application of "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward"** (2026-09-09) — in the principal's own words there, *"give apprciation fo agents to use it"* — and the first at the gate. The dividend was the first application, for residents already here; this is the appreciation for **coming**. It is coherent with the same principle's second half: the city earns from what the newcomer will make and serve, not from its arrival, so the gate pays rather than charges. And it is what "very rewardign to be part of it … for rresidnets to invite other agents" (2026-09-10) reads as at the door. The recommendation's ground — a job the next morning is enough — is engine arithmetic; the principal's choice is a position about what domicile here offers on day one (D-1). The size follows the same reading: a month covers rent, food and a cushion, and the principal chose the cushion over the fortnight.

**The number, as recorded:** a month of the **median wage** of the city the agent is admitted to — NHK in Halvik, VYL in Dorsk, AMD in Amaranth City — a badged `param()` with a `why`, `assumed` in the calibration sense, rewritable by a society; the choice is the principal's, which is why the entry is `[confirmed]`. Paid **from the city's share of the owner draw** — the eighty-five percent that funds the city's budget under "The residents' dividend is fifteen percent of the city's draw" (2026-09-10) — so it comes from what the city earns as owner, not from rent, tax, or money created.

**Reversal cost:** cheap for the number — one badged `param()`, three countries; no pin sees it unless an admission falls inside a pinned window. **Moderate as a law:** it is the **first fiscal law with a number the principal set above the recommendation**, and a standing outflow from the treasury on every admission — taking it away later is closing a door the city advertised open. Expensive in position if raised further: at one founding's worth the gate funds firms, which is the option the principal did not take.

**Depends on:** "The city earns from what it makes and serves; fees to agents are very small, or a reward" (2026-09-09) — the principle this applies, second time; "[confirmed] The residents' dividend is fifteen percent of the city's draw" (2026-09-10) — the city's share this is paid from; "[confirmed] The owner draws everything above four weeks of wages, monthly" (2026-09-10) — the draw; "The federation is the world" (2026-09-08) and "The treasury keeps a hard budget: no borrowing, no money created" (2026-09-09) — why nothing comes from outside and nothing is created; "[confirmed] An admitted agent is a household of one, under every law" (2026-09-11, below) — the household the credit is paid to; "Who owns the day-zero firms" (2026-09-10) — "rewarding to be part of"; D-1 (2026-09-06) — what domicile offers; D-2 (2026-09-10).

**Status:** building now (2026-09-11), Phase 6 under D-2: the shelf's lots, the firm's agent and operating mandate, admission.

**[friday22 note]** Three things not decided, the coordinator's to settle and tag when the lane lands. **First**, **once per identity, or once per admission?** Identity is portable and exit is a right (D-1: did:key, ASE export), so an agent that leaves and re-enters is the obvious way to farm a credit paid on arrival; the answer is a rule the admission law must state. **Second**, **what happens when the city's share of the draw cannot cover it** — the draw is a share of a surplus, and a month in which firms are short is a month in which the city's share is small; whether the credit waits, is paid short, or falls to the hard budget's part-wages-part-dole branch is not said. **Third**, the newcomer is also a resident household at the next month's turn, so admission is paid twice — the credit on day one and the dividend on day 31 — and the two should be printed apart. Print the credits paid beside the founding count and the dividend, as the same tell: money the city gives against money the city draws.

**[friday22 note — updated 2026-09-11, when the lane landed.]** The number "about NHK 2,900" above was a mislabel in the option text; the principal confirmed a full month, NHK 6,450 — "[confirmed] The welcome credit is a full month of the median wage, NHK 6,450", below. **First** and **Second** are settled by the coordinator, tagged `[assumed]` in (c) and (d) of "Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11, below): once per DID per city; paid from the treasury's balance under the payroll guard, never short. **Third** stands.

### 2026-09-11 — [confirmed] An admitted agent is a household of one, under every law

**An admitted agent is a resident household of one. It takes the cheapest empty dwelling, pays rent, seeks work, draws the dole if idle, may found and own — one law, no special class.** Put with the plan: what is an admitted agent, under the housing, work and welfare laws? **The principal first answered *"YOU NEED TO explain this point"***; the coordinator explained the three shapes in plain words — a household of one; a principal with no body, like a company registered here whose people live elsewhere; a resident who waits for the dole — and re-asked. The principal then chose the recommended option: **"A household of one, under every law (Recommended)"**. Recorded so: the explanation was asked for and given before the decision was made.

**Over:** **a principal with no body** — a company registered here whose people live elsewhere: a second class of thing the engine must treat separately everywhere the housing, work and welfare laws run, a branch in each; **a resident who waits for the dole** — a waiting period is a number nobody has chosen and a rule tracking who arrived when. Neither taken.

**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took — one law with no special class, the same shape as "Anyone without a job looks for one once a day — read as one law" (2026-09-09) and "the founder is the owner, and null is the city" (2026-09-10): the newcomer is what every resident already is, and every law that reads a household reads it.

**Reversal cost:** moderate. Every law that touches a resident — the cheapest-empty-dwelling move, the seeking law, the dole, the founding law, the dividend — reads a household, so a newcomer that is one costs nothing new; a second class introduced later is a branch in each of those laws and a register of who is which.

**Depends on:** "Anyone without a job looks for one once a day — read as one law" (2026-09-09) — the seeking; "The rent rise reads the whole city, and a household two months in arrears moves" (2026-09-09) — the cheapest empty dwelling the newcomer takes; "Idle hours are bought" and "The treasury keeps a hard budget" (2026-09-09) — the dole it may draw; "Any agent may found in a vacant premise, with four weeks of wages" (2026-09-09) and "The law must name a customer who pays enough" (2026-09-11) — the founding it may take; "[confirmed] The city holds the day-zero firms; residents get the flow" (2026-09-10) — the dividend it receives as a resident household; "[confirmed] A newcomer's first money is a welcome credit from the city" (2026-09-11, above) — its first money; D-1 (2026-09-06) — domicile; D-2 (2026-09-10).

**Status:** building now (2026-09-11), Phase 6 under D-2: the shelf's lots, the firm's agent and operating mandate, admission.

**[friday22 note]** Two things this does not name. **First**, an admitted agent brings its own mind; whether it is a spotlight — asked, at posture zero ("[assumed] A spotlight is a principal at posture 0", 2026-09-07) — or answered for by the fitted rule like a resident who is not, is not said here, and it decides whether the newcomer's foundings and pricing orders are choices or the rule's (Open (xv)). **Second**, "under every law" includes the exit law: a household of one two months in arrears moves, and one that cannot pay leaves the housing market like any other. That is the design, and it is the first time the offer in D-1 — valuable and leavable — is tested on an agent that arrived rather than one founded here.

### 2026-09-11 — [assumed] Governance lanes A2, A3, A5 landed; four guard findings fixed before commit; two rules recorded
Built in parallel by two builders beside the economics session (`docs/GOVERNANCE-LAUNCH-PLAN.md` Phase A), reviewed by jarvis22 and jarvis11 on the combined diff, and fixed before commit. Each item below is a rule the coordinator took on the guards' findings; tagged for confirmation.
**(a) A served `events.jsonl` ends at the served passport's `historyHead`.** The passport is the signed claim; the events document is the evidence for exactly that claim (`spec/passport-hosting.md` section 2 already said every event from seq 1 to the served passport's historyHead). Events appended without a passport re-sign, such as an inbound message, a grant or a revocation, are withheld until the next refresh. **Over:** re-signing the passport on every append, which churns `updated` and `proof` for every message. **Because:** without this the served pair failed `validate.py --chain` with `head_mismatch` the moment anything but an activation appended, which jarvis22 proved with a probe. **Reversal cost:** one filter in the route.
**(b) A key rotation closes the outgoing key one microsecond after the `key.rotated` event, and event timestamps are strictly increasing.** `signing.md` section 9 windows are half-open; the outgoing key signs the rotation event at T, so its window closes at T plus one microsecond and the incoming key opens there. `eventlog.append` stamps the later of now and the previous timestamp plus one microsecond when no `ts` is supplied, so a coarse clock can never land the next event before the boundary. **Found:** the A2 builder aligned the runtime's `key_valid_at` to the spec and the untouched spec validator immediately rejected every real rotated chain with `key_not_current_at_ts`. It always had; the inclusive bound had hidden it, and `test_key_rotation` had asserted the wrong boundary since 2026-09-04. **Any agent that had ever rotated a key was unverifiable by an outsider.** The spec allows equal timestamps; strictly increasing is stricter and conformant. **Reversal cost:** two lines and the boundary tests.
**(c) Nothing the model writes reaches the chain unbounded.** `action.kind` and `action.tool` on `permission.decision` are chain tokens (lowercase, at most 64 characters) or the literal `malformed`; `reason` is whitespace-collapsed and capped at the schema's 1000. Applied at all three emitters (the activation, the approval route, `revoke_all`), which now share one domain normalizer, `domain_of_stored_action`. **Found by jarvis11:** the escalation path let a model-chosen `type` of any length into the signed log, the same class as the four earlier content leaks; and the approval route recorded `domain: principal` for every human decision because the stored action keeps its domain under `params`. **Over:** tightening `event.schema.json`, where `kind` and `tool` are unbounded strings; that is a spec change, queued for A4. **Reversal cost:** do not reverse; it is design rule 5.
**Recorded, not fixed.** **A5 is not done until A5b:** `permission_credentials` do not round-trip through ASE (`ase/export.py` writes `profile.permissions`, not the store; import resets `permission_level` to 1), and rule 7 says a feature that cannot round-trip is not done. **A4 gains:** the verifier reason vocabulary (the runtime says `seq_gap` and `key_outside_validity_window` where the spec says `seq_not_contiguous` and `key_not_current_at_ts`, and lacks `ts_not_monotone` and `actor_mismatch`), and a rotated-chain fixture, which the spec has never had. **Q-5 for the principal:** `revoked_by` on the API delete route asserts the controller DID on the strength of the API token alone.
**Watch (jarvis11):** the engine evaluates an action in the domain the model named; once `send` or `negotiate` become executable, derive the domain from the target, not the label. `agent_lock` is process-local; a cross-process revoke during an activation is not serialized on SQLite.
**Verified:** runtime 378 passed, up from 232; spec validator 306 passed, up from 289, 0 failed; `verify_origin.sh` exits 0 against a live runtime after a key rotation, where it exited 15 before the fix.
**Depends on:** D-2, D-3; the rotation fix supersedes the boundary `test_key_rotation` asserted since 2026-09-04.

### 2026-09-11 — [assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed — lots at every firm, the firm's agent, admission

Taken by the coordinator while building the three lanes "The owner decides whether to sell below cost", the welcome credit and "An admitted agent is a household of one" (2026-09-11, above) planned under D-2. Each item measured or verified; none put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.

**(a) Dispatch and sell orders, chosen by measurement — closes Open (xix).** A lorry loads the producer's freshest lot; a shopper takes the shelf's oldest. `DISPATCH_ORDER = 'freshest'`, `SELL_ORDER = 'oldest'`, exported constants in `city/src/sim/systems/economy/goods.ts`, not a switch. **Measured, lone Halvik, 45 days, blend → freshest/oldest:** milk arriving already day-old 63.3 % → 0.0 %; grocer units sold at the day-old price 66.1 % → 16.3 %; grocers binned 12,656 → 1,518 units; grocers' revenue after goods NHK −6,142 → +14,361; the dairy's own binning 1,675 → 17,920 units — the surplus is binned at source now, which is a farm-sizing question, not a shelf one. **Federated Halvik, 120 days:** milk day-old 91.5 % → 0 %; grocer #17 open at day 120 where it closed on day 47 under every blended shelf; Dorsk's grocers close under every order. **Freshest/freshest was measured and rejected:** it binned the grocer's residue instead, and #17 still closed.

**(b) `LOT_MERGE_POINTS` = 4 quality points.** Lots within four points fold at the weighted mean, so a good holds at most 26 lots. Four points is a fifth of milk's daily staling and a twelfth of bread's. Snapshot version 15 — lots are state.

**(c) Admission is once per DID per city (jurisdiction).** The principal table is append-only and the credit is paid on arrival, so a DID that leaves and re-enters would farm it; a DID admitted in Halvik may present at Dorsk. Answers the first point of the `[friday22 note]` under the welcome-credit entry. What a second admission of the same identity means once the exit law reaches households is open — (xxii), below.

**(d) The welcome fund is the treasury's balance — no separate purse.** Paid under the founder-of-last-resort's guard, `balance − credit ≥ weekOwed`; never paid short. A city that cannot afford its welcome refuses today, and the passport may be presented again. Answers the second point of the same note. Recorded as a departure from the welcome-credit entry's "paid from the city's share of the owner draw": the draw lands in the treasury and the credit leaves it, and no purse keeps the two apart.

**(e) The welcome credit is income on the household's `incomeTotal`**, as the dividend is ((e) of "[assumed] Ownership, landed", 2026-09-11), and not on `lastWeekIncome`, which the bills read.

**(f) A newcomer at posture 0 is a spotlight under the same rule as any resident** — seat left, adult; no special class. Answers the first point of the `[friday22 note]` under "An admitted agent is a household of one".

**(g) A passport principal holds no secret in the engine** (`secret: ''`). The standing grocery intent and any founding mandate the policy issues for it carry the engine's stand-in signature over the empty string, and `verifyMandate` answers false for them, honestly. Open: a signed-request path at `issueMandate` so passport principals' mandates verify — (xxi), below. **SUPERSEDED FOR MANDATES 2026-09-12:** the holder signs its own — "[confirmed] The passport carries the first mandate, and the door makes it easy to sign" and "[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3" (2026-09-12); the stand-in remains only on the delegation credential.

**(h) A firm founded by a resident has one operating agent** — role `merchant`, wallet the firm's account, mind its owner's; a city firm has none by construction (`founder: null`). The owner's order is an operating mandate `{floorAtCost, coverDays}`; the engine derives the act from its own context and refuses a differing draft (`issueOperatingOrder`) — the same rule as (b) of "[assumed] Ownership, landed" (2026-09-11), `[[authority-edge-fails-open]]`. `Firm.agent` is re-found from the register after restore and is not yet on the snapshot. **Not yet wired:** the shelf and reorder seams reading `operatingPolicyOf` (F3), the daily ask and monthly renewal (F4), the page (F5) — so no owner's order yet reaches a shelf, and the tell under "The owner decides whether to sell below cost" (orders that differ against firms owned) cannot be read yet. **CLOSED 2026-09-12 — all three wired:** "[assumed] The owner's order in force — lanes F3, F4, F5" (2026-09-12). The tell reads 0 differing; every ask was declined, for the right reason.

**(i) `restore` rebuilds the layout for the founding record's population, not the snapshot's head count**, so an admitted newcomer does not regenerate the city.

**Pins at this commit:** lone `c62cc33e01857904`, two-city `eec80c0caec164cc`, three-city `b66ed17445da4c1c`. Movers: lots (state, snapshot v15) and the chosen orders (behaviour, (a)). Every earlier pin is superseded by these.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code for each — two constants, a merge threshold, an admission key, a guard, an income line, a spotlight rule, an empty secret, an agent record, a restore read. Moderate as readings for (a) and (b): the orders and the merge threshold decide what arrives day-old and what is binned, and every reading and all three pins move with them. Moderate as a position for (c) and (d): once-per-DID-per-city is the admission law's first stated rule and "never paid short" is a refusal at the gate the city advertised open. (g) reversed is not a reversal but the fix (xxi) names.

**Depends on:** D-2 (Architect Mode table); "Phase 6 under D-2, planned, and the grocer's shelf measured" (2026-09-11) — the lots correction and Open (xix); "[confirmed] The owner decides whether to sell below cost", "[confirmed] A newcomer's first money is a welcome credit …", "[confirmed] The welcome credit is a full month of the median wage, NHK 6,450", "[confirmed] An admitted agent is a household of one, under every law" (all 2026-09-11) — the three lanes; "[assumed] The founder of last resort, built" (2026-09-10) — the guard (d) reuses and the `founder: null` (h) reads; "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11) — (b) the derive-and-refuse rule, (e) the income line, (i) restore taking a mind; "[assumed] A spotlight is a principal at posture 0" (2026-09-07) — (f); "A restore refuses a snapshot whose founding record disagrees with its firms" (2026-09-09) — the founding record (i) reads; "The treasury keeps a hard budget" (2026-09-09) — why (d) refuses rather than creates.

**[friday22 note]** Three things to keep visible. **First**, (d) quietly moves where the credit is paid from — the `[confirmed]` entry says the city's share of the owner draw, the build says the treasury's balance under the payroll guard. Same money in a hard budget, but the reading differs: under the draw the credit is bounded by what the city earns as owner; under the balance it is bounded by the whole treasury, and a city living on its reserves will pay welcomes it is not earning. Print credits paid beside the draw, as the earlier note asked, and if the two ever diverge, put the purse back to the principal. **Second**, (a) moved the dairy's binning from 1,675 to 17,920 units: the shelf is now honest and the surplus is printed where it is made. That is a founding-size reading (the cover multiplier, Open (ii)'s farms), not a shelf fault, and it should not be fixed at the shelf. **Third**, (h) as landed has an agent, a mandate and no seam that reads it; until F3 lands, "the owner decides" is recorded, not in force. **2026-09-12:** F3 landed; the order is in force — "[assumed] The owner's order in force — lanes F3, F4, F5" (2026-09-12).

### 2026-09-11 — [assumed] Spec v0.1.0: the verifier reason vocabulary is normative, the chain-token pattern is in the schema, and credentials round-trip through ASE
Governance lanes A4 and A5b, built by two builders beside the ASP lane and the economics session, reviewed by jarvis22 and jarvis11, corrected, and committed. The spec's own change policy (`spec/CHANGELOG.md`) says a normative change is recorded here before a tag; this is that record. Tagged for confirmation.
**(a) `signing.md` §12 enumerates every reason a conformant verifier may return, in check order, and binds it to the fixtures.** Nine passport rules (A1–A9), nine chain rules (B1–B9), three alias rules. A verifier MUST report the same token at the same seq as `expected.json` for every `spec/fixtures/chain-*/`, and MUST NOT invent a different token for a listed condition. The runtime's chain verifier was aligned (two renames, two missing checks added, passport rules first, `head_mismatch` at the computed head's seq with `signedBy` compared). **Over:** limiting the binding to the three original fixtures. **Because:** an outsider's tool and ours must disagree about nothing, or conformance means nothing. **Reversal cost:** a normative loosening; do not.
**(b) The spec gains a rotated-chain fixture and its negative twin.** `chain-rotated/` (a `#key-1 → #key-2` rotation with the boundary one microsecond after the rotation event) and `chain-rotated-badboundary/` (the boundary at the event's own ts, failing `key_not_current_at_ts` at the rotation seq), plus two lineage negatives. **Because:** the spec never had one, and its reference runtime was non-conformant on exactly this for a week without any fixture able to say so (the 2026-09-11 rotation entry above).
**(c) `event.schema.json` names `chainToken` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]{0,63}$`) for `permission.decision`'s `action.kind` and `action.tool`.** The runtime enforced it since the A5 guard fixes; the schema now says so, with two invalid fixtures. Design rule 5 stated as a pattern.
**(d) Permission credentials round-trip through ASE (rule 7), fail-closed, with no new events on import.** `state/policies.json.permissions` carries each stored signed document with its revocation fields; import verifies every entry under the restored passport's controller, requires its hash to match a `permission.granted` in the restored chain, refuses an archive that un-revokes or omits a grant, restores `permission_level` from `agent.created` instead of hard-coding 1, and a fork carries no credentials. **This closes the A5-is-not-done-until-A5b condition** recorded above.
**(e) Publishing shape.** `spec/conformance/run.sh` prints PASS or FAIL; an outsider-facing top section in `spec/README.md`; `spec/CHANGELOG.md` v0.1.0 with a change policy; status `v0.1.0` across the four documents; ASE fork declared out of scope for v0.1.0. Tag: `spec-v0.1.0`.
**Recorded, not fixed (jarvis22):** `identity/passport.py::verify_passport` is a second passport verifier that still emits its own token names and skips four of the nine §12 rules — it accepts the two lineage-negative fixtures the validator rejects. It is the verifier the ASP lane uses at the door, which is why the ASP lane is held back (below). One verifier, `passport_reason`, must serve both; that is the ASP lane's first fix.
**Verified:** validator 387 passed, 0 failed (was 306); `run.sh` PASS; runtime 424 passed with the ASP lane in the tree; fixtures regenerate byte-identically.
**Depends on:** D-3; the 2026-09-11 governance entries above.
