### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes — the sell order reversed to the blend, and six engine rules

Taken by the coordinator on jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes (F3, F4); none put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.

**(a) REVERSAL of (a) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11): the sell order is the blend, not oldest-first.** `SELL_ORDER = 'blend'`; `DISPATCH_ORDER` stays `'freshest'` (`city/src/sim/systems/economy/goods.ts`, both tables in its header). **Why the earlier number was wrong — jarvis11's first finding:** the till that measured oldest-first priced the bin's mean while handing over the oldest lot, so "16.3 % at the day-old price" and "+14,361 after goods" were old stock struck at list. With the till pricing what it hands over (`shelfPrice` / `isDayOld` read `nextQuality`), dispatch held at freshest, lone Halvik, 45 days: **oldest** 47.3 % of units at the day-old price, NHK +1,258 after goods; **freshest** 16.0 %, +3,452; **blend** 13.6 %, +10,392. Federated Halvik at day 120: grocer #17 closed under oldest (day 47, as the one-number bin did), open under freshest (22,811 after goods against 28,692 wages), open under the blend keeping more than its wages (33,518 against 32,804). Both orders are now named scaffolding under D-2 — an owner's operating choices once an owner can hold them, Open (xxiv). Lesson for `docs/LESSONS.md`, tom to write: **a constant chosen by measurement is only as good as the reader the measurement ran through.**

**(b) The rule mind orders nothing on an operating question** — it defers, issues nothing, writes nothing. `operatingPolicyOf` reads the defaults when there is no order, so every order on the register is an owner's choice. Replaces the wording of (h) of the same 2026-09-11 entry, and "gets today's default from the fitted rule" in "[confirmed] The owner decides whether to sell below cost" (2026-09-11), wherever either read as the rule answering the defaults: the default is the engine's by absence, not the rule's by answer.

**(c) `issueOperatingOrder` re-derives its own question** (`operatingContext`) and refuses a context whose principal, agent, tick or currency differs — the same rule it applies to the draft. `[[authority-edge-fails-open]]`.

**(d) `closeFirm` halts the firm's agent and revokes its standing operating order** (a revocation row on the chain). A closing moves stock lot by lot, freshest first, tallied as arrivals on the taker's shelf row; the remainder is binned.

**(e) A bin refuses a write past its API** — throws naming the good and both totals. Reads never write. `setStock` stages a shelf for tests.

**(f) A bare `APPROVE` to an operating question is a defer; `APPROVE floor=… cover=…` is an order.** The system instruction now says the head word is the question's. The fake recording was re-made under the new fingerprint — 21 answers for two days; the old file had carried 82 stale entries across builds.

**(g) An admission writes its chain row (`admission:resident:admitted`) before the standing mandate and the welcome-credit transfer.** The header says the door draws once — the agent's keypair.

**Pins at this commit:** lone `e085af3e395c1c27`, two-city `5597ed4f513f6474`, three-city `b6f4184fb1adcb0a`. Movers: the honest till and the blend re-chosen, (a). Basket 112.3. The 2026-09-11 pins are superseded.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code for each — a constant, a defer, a re-derive, a halt-and-revoke, a throw, a head word, a row order. Moderate as a reading for (a): the sell order decides what sells at the day-old price and what is binned, every reading and all three pins move with it, and it has now been chosen twice. Reversing (a) again is not a code cost but a measurement one — nothing should re-choose it without saying which till it read through.

**Depends on:** D-2; "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11) — (a) here supersedes its (a), (b) here refines its (h); "[confirmed] The owner decides whether to sell below cost" (2026-09-11) — (b); (b) of "[assumed] Ownership, landed — after jarvis11's read" (2026-09-11) — the derive-and-refuse rule (c) extends; Open (xix), re-closed on the blend; Open (xxi), extended; Open (xxiv), new.

**[friday22 note]** (xix) said the order was "to be measured, not assumed", and it was — through a till that priced one thing and handed over another. The 2026-09-11 entry said "chosen by measurement" as if that settled it. It did not; a measurement is only as settled as its reader. When (xxiv) hands the two orders to owners, the same till must be the one that prices their sales, or the tell under "The owner decides" (orders that differ against firms owned) reads the reader and not the owners.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] Spec v0.1.1: a rotated key is the DID's only if the chain attests it (B10)
**Found by jarvis22 while reviewing the ASP lane, and reproduced by execution:** the reference validator printed `chain: PASS` for a passport of the victim's DID that listed the victim's genuine `#key-1` as closed beside an attacker's `#key-2` as open, served next to the victim's genuine, unmodified chain. A1–A9 prove only that a passport is signed by the key it names as current; for `#key-1` that closes the loop because the DID is the key, and for every later key it is circular. Nothing in v0.1.0 read the `key.rotated` event.
**Chose:** `signing.md` §12 gains **B10 `lineage_rotation_unattested`**: every `keyLineage[i]`, i ≥ 1, must be introduced by a `key.rotated` event of the verified chain whose `new_key_id`, `publicKeyMultibase`, `previous_key_id`, `rotated_by` and signing `key_id` all name the predecessor vouching for its successor, with the event's `ts` before the entry's `from`. Checked last, reported at the computed head's seq. A recovery rotation (`rotatedBy` a DID) has no chain attestation in v0 and is refused. Fixture `chain-lineage-unattested/` fails at seq 4; `chain-rotated/` still passes because its seq-5 event attests `#key-2`. Both `validate.py` and the runtime's `verify_events` enforce it, and the vocabulary test pins them equal. Also in v0.1.1: `$defs.domain` gains `maxLength 128` (the engine and the store already refuse longer), B6 is clarified to first-match `key_id_at` and the runtime mirrors it (overlapping windows now refuse at the same seq), and the runtime's `no_passport` token becomes `schema_invalid`.
**Over:** leaving the rule in the ASP door only (`asp/verify.py::rotation_evidence`, where the builder first put it) — a door that refuses what the published validator accepts is exactly the two-verifiers-disagree condition §12 exists to forbid; and calling it v0.2.0 — the CHANGELOG's own policy makes a new token a minor bump, and this is a correction of what v0.1.0 claimed to verify, recorded there as a versioning note. The version number is the principal's to change.
**The limit, stated in the spec:** an attacker holding a *compromised* `#key-1` that the victim never rotated away from cannot be caught by any B-rule; that needs revocation (`asp.revoke`) or an external anchor, and is not in v0.
**Verified:** validator 410 passed (was 387), `run.sh` PASS; the unattested fixture passes A1–A9 and B1–B9 in isolation and fails on B10 alone; runtime conformance and eventlog suites 37 passed; a live rotated runtime still passes `verify_origin.sh`.
**Depends on:** the 2026-09-11 entries (rotation boundary; spec v0.1.0); D-3.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] Governance lane A6: the door — ASP hello and disclose, A2A receive — and the rules the guards forced
Built by one builder across three rounds; jarvis11 and jarvis22 reviewed each round; nine findings after the first, four and five after the second, one after the third; every one fixed before commit. The rules below were taken by the coordinator on those findings; each is tagged for confirmation.
**(a) A peer is a DID only when the chain says so.** At the door a passport is checked with the one verifier (`identity/passport.py`: the schema, then §12 A1–A9), and a non-genesis current key is bound to the DID only by fetching the peer's `events.jsonl` from the same origin, replaying it under B10 (`f0117bc`), paged with a wall-clock budget and truncated at the passport's head. **Over:** trusting the passport's own lineage (the impersonation jarvis11 proved); keeping a local copy of the rule in the door (two implementations of one rule). **Because:** A1–A9 are circular for any key after `#key-1`. **Reversal cost:** do not reverse.
**(b) A counterparty is never heard in the principal's voice — and neither is a forgeable channel.** `render.py::is_principal_turn` requires the principal protocol, the controller as sender, *and* the source class `stated_by_principal`; anything else is one attributed user turn (`counterparty <did> via <protocol> (source class)` for ASP/A2A; `unverified sender` for a principal-channel row the write path classed `external_untrusted` — inbound email, ADR-007). History lines follow the same rule by channel. The channel→class table moved to `types.py` so the write path, `perceive` and the render path read one table; `perceive` classes a principal-protocol row by its channel (its memory's `source_ref` is the weekly-review id in steady state, so a lookup by message id was wrong after week one). **Found by jarvis11:** the ASP lane was the first to put another agent's text into the inbox, and the runtime rendered every inbound row as `principal:`; then the first gate still spoke spoofed email as the principal while the same row's memory said otherwise. **Over:** excluding ASP rows from the inbox until attribution existed. **Reversal cost:** do not reverse — it is rule 4 at the prompt.
**(c) `disclose` returns the minimum.** Every caller gets the same projection (`type, issuer, domain, level, expires_at`); verification decides only *which* domains, through `policy.engine.authorize` with a `disclose` kind at level 0 and one `permission.decision` per named domain (at most 16, each a valid domain ≤ 128 characters, refused before any work); `limits` and signed documents leave only via the token-guarded `GET /permissions`. **Over:** signed documents with `limits` to any verified peer (the first build; white paper §10.5 says disclose the minimum, and a verified peer is anyone who can host a passport). **Reversal cost:** cheap, but record why.
**(d) Envelope and receive-side rules, from the kit's spec 06 with two additions.** Every ASP message carries `asp-to` (the audience; without it a signed message replays to every agent an operator hosts), `ts` with a 300 s window and 60 s skew, a nonce remembered per receiving agent, and the uniform-rule signature; a verified peer is cached ten minutes, at most 10 000 peers, keys and head only. `hello` and `disclose` are answered at level 0; a `message` at declared level ≤ 2 from a verified peer enters the inbox as `counterparty_signed` (unverified: `external_untrusted`, never `stated_by_principal`); level ≥ 3 is refused `level_unsupported`; the eight other methods are `not_implemented`, signed. Every inbound and every reply lands on the chain as hashes only; the stored content is the envelope itself so the receiver's `payload_hash`, the reply's `re` and the sender's chain name the same bytes. A2A messages are verified the same way and carry `asp-to`, `ts` and a nonce in their metadata; the transport is a documented stub. Refused messages are logged (the lane's own gate) — strangers can therefore append to the chain at line rate, which A8 must rate-limit.
**(e) Fail-closed defaults.** `inbox.receive_message` defaults to level 0 and `external_untrusted`; `InboundMessage.source_kind` likewise; the passport schema is validated at the door and a runtime without `spec/` refuses every peer (`schema_unavailable`) — an A8 runbook item: ship `spec/` as package data or set `AGENT_SOCIETY_SPEC_DIR`. Fetches: https only by default, no redirects, every resolved address must be public, size and time caps; DNS rebinding between the check and the connect is the documented residual.
**Recorded, not fixed:** a compromised `#key-1` the victim never rotated away from is accepted at the door with a truncated genuine chain — no chain rule can catch it; `asp.revoke` or an external anchor is the answer, not in v0. The `/asp` route sits behind `API_TOKEN` until A8 exempts it. `refused` replies echo up to three jsonschema error strings from the peer's passport into a document this agent signs and stores off-chain — worth a length cap.
**Verified:** 43 ASP tests plus the impersonation, truncation, tampering, replay, capacity and attribution forcing tests; full suite green; `run.sh` PASS; every event the flows write validates against `event.schema.json`.
**Depends on:** A5 (`7d2a404`), spec v0.1.1 (`f0117bc`), the one verifier (`37079bb`); ADR-007.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] Governance lane A7: the Identity Registry — admission is asked for by the controller, signed by the jurisdiction, and evidenced on the agent's chain
Built by one builder, reviewed by jarvis11 and jarvis22, fixed on their findings, committed with spec v0.2.0. Rules taken by the coordinator; each tagged for confirmation.
**(a) The runtime never mints admission.** A request is `{passport, operator, policy, controller_signature}`; the passport must pass the one verifier (schema, then §12 A1–A9); the request must be signed by the passport's controller key; the presented passport must equal the hosted one on `id`, `controller`, `verificationMethod`, `keyLineage` **and `modelBinding`** — a copy from before a model swap still verifies and is refused `passport_mismatch`; and the record's `modelHash` is computed from the hosted passport, never the request (jarvis11: the one thing rule 2 registers was being taken from the requester's document). Ten enumerated refusals, in a fixed order, all before any write; the read-then-write section runs under the agent lock.
**(b) The admission record is a signed object.** `{did, operator, modelHash, policyHash, admitted_at, jurisdiction, registry, signature}` under the uniform signing rule, signed by the registry's own `did:key`; `record_hash` is the §7 hash of the stored record, signature included, exactly as `credential_hash`. It is now a test vector and the validator verifies its signature and hash (spec v0.2.0). An outsider verifies it with the spec's primitives alone — but the registry DID is not self-certifying for *recognition*: it is obtained out of band (C2's recognition list). The policy attestation's shape `{version, engine, rules[]}` is `[assumed]`.
**(c) Admission and revocation live on the agent's chain as hashes.** Two new event kinds, `registry.admitted` `{registry, record_hash, jurisdiction, admitted_at}` and `registry.revoked` `{registry, record_hash, reason}`; the operator string reaches the chain only as a preimage. **The chain is the evidence for the door too:** a second admission is refused `already_admitted` citing the prior event's seq, and a revoked DID is refused `previously_revoked`, by reading the chain — so an export, wipe and import cannot re-open a door the kill switch closed (jarvis11: the row alone does not round-trip; the record's restoration is C3). Any registry's event counts: a second jurisdiction recognises, it does not re-admit.
**(d) The registry key is never minted on a read, never at a working-directory path.** It lives at `settings.keys_dir / registry` (or `AGENT_SOCIETY_REGISTRY_KEY_DIR`); a dev key exists only under `AGENT_SOCIETY_REGISTRY_DEV_KEY=1`, encrypted under the runtime passphrase, with a WARNING on mint and on every load; without it `/registry` answers 503 `registry_key_unavailable`. Who holds the production key is Q-4. **Found by jarvis11:** the first build minted the jurisdiction's signing identity on `GET /registry` at `$CWD/keys/registry/`, warning once.
**(e) Scope.** Hosted DIDs only: an outside DID gets `agent_unknown`, because there is no local chain to put the admission on. Admitting an outside agent — the launch checklist's item 2 as written — needs the record without a local event, and which chain carries it is **Q-3**. Revocation is on API-token authority with no claimant written (the honest end of Q-5's option 3). Jurisdiction defaults to `dev` `[assumed]`. `registry_records` does not round-trip through ASE until C3; the chain-side check above is what makes that safe.
**Verified:** 20 registry tests; runtime 468 passed; the spec's two lineage-negative fixtures are refused at the registry door; the B10 impersonation fixture is refused `passport_mismatch` against the loaded agent.
**Depends on:** A6 (`e470361`), the one verifier (`37079bb`), spec v0.1.1 (`f0117bc`); Q-3, Q-4, Q-5 open.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] The owner's order in force — lanes F3, F4, F5

Taken by the coordinator while wiring the owner's standing order through the shelf, the ask and the page, under "[confirmed] The owner decides whether to sell below cost" (2026-09-11). None put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.

**(a) F3 — the shelf and the reorder read the order.** `shelfPrice` reads the firm's policy: under `floorAtCost` the day-old price stops at `unitCost` — what the firm paid for the unit — and never rises above list. `reorderPoint` holds the order's `coverDays` in place of `COVER_DAYS`. Both read `operatingPolicyOf(world, firm, tick)`: the standing order's terms when it is valid, else the engine's defaults; no seam branches on ownership. The shopper's estimate (`basketCost`, `mealCost`) reads the same policy as the till — the one-till rule the `[friday22 note]` under "jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes" (2026-09-12) asked for. Pins unmoved: no resident-owned firm falls inside their windows.

**(b) F4 — the owner is asked on two mornings, never in between.** `economy.operate` runs at 00:00 after `economy.lastResort` and before `economy.seek`, and asks the owner of every open resident-founded firm, in firm order, on two mornings only: the morning after the firm's first day (occasion `first-day`, derived from the newest founding row for the premise) and the month's turn (day 1, occasion `month`). Never in between — a spotlight would pay for the same question daily, and the rule orders nothing anyway ((b) of the same 2026-09-12 entry). A yes goes through `issueOperatingOrder` and is said on the city log; a refusal writes nothing. `operateTally(world)` keeps asks per world, never hashed — the same class as the dividend tally, (xviii).

**(c) F5 — the order is printed where the owner is.** The building panel prints the order beside the owner via `operatingMandateOf` — "under a standing order — never below what we paid, 2 days of cover, day 20 to day 50". `validate` prints an orders sentence per city (standing, differing from the rule, asked / ordered / declined) and a "Standing order" column on the owners' table. The tell is the differing count — the one the `[friday22 note]` under "The owner decides" (2026-09-11) named.

**(d) Reading.** Federated, 120 days: asks 1 / 9 / 4 / 13 (lone Halvik, federated Halvik, Dorsk, Amaranth City), all declined. No owner in validate is a spotlight and the rule orders nothing, so zero orders — for the right reason.

**(e) The doors fixture gained a case for `runOperatePolicy`** — tom's 2026-09-12 "one change": a new caller into an authority-checked seam is run through the seam's fixture in the same commit. The fixture is at 71 cases.

**Pins:** unmoved from "jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes" (2026-09-12) — lone `e085af3e395c1c27`, two-city `5597ed4f513f6474`, three-city `b6f4184fb1adcb0a`.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — a policy read at two seams and the shopper's estimate, one 00:00 step with two occasions, a panel line and a validate column. Moderate as a position: this is the first owner's decision that reaches a shelf, and the `[confirmed]` entry already says taking it back is taking a right off owners who have used it.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] The owner decides whether to sell below cost" (2026-09-11) — the law this puts in force; (h) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11) — the agent and mandate this wires, its "not yet wired" now closed; (b), (c) and (f) of "[assumed] jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes" (2026-09-12) — the rule defers, `issueOperatingOrder` re-derives, `APPROVE floor=… cover=…` is an order; "[confirmed] The agent looks first" (2026-09-10) — the 00:00 order `operate` sits in; "[assumed] A spotlight is a principal at posture 0" (2026-09-07) — who is asked; Open (xxi), (xxiv), (xxv).

**[friday22 note]** Two things. **First**, (d) is a zero that means what it says, but only because nobody in validate is a spotlight. The first differing order will be a spotlight's under the prompt fingerprint, or a passport principal's once (xxi) lands; until one is, the tell is built, not read. **Second**, (b)'s two mornings mean an order given on `first-day` stands until the next day 1 whatever the shelf does in between; whether an owner may re-order between the two mornings is not decided, and it is the same shape as (xv).

### 2026-09-12 — [confirmed] The passport carries the first mandate, and the door makes it easy to sign

**A holder is admitted under a mandate it signed itself, and the city hands the applicant the exact draft and ids to sign.** Put by AskUserQuestion, 2026-09-12: from admission until a holder signs its own mandate, what stands? Options: the city writes a stand-in marked as such; nothing until the holder signs; the passport must carry the first mandate. The principal answered: **"like answer 3 it should attract agents to have their own passport or allow them or help them, whatever makes the agents feel more free, or productive, do it."** Read by the coordinator as option 3 with the city making it easy: the door hands the applicant the draft and the read-ahead ids, and admission and the holder's own signed mandate land together.

**Over:** **a stand-in marked as such** — the engine signing in a holder's name over an empty secret, which is what (g) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11) landed and (xxi) named as the opposite of D-3's gate; **nothing until the holder signs** — an admitted household with no standing intent, which cannot shop. Neither taken.

**Because:** the principal's — whatever makes agents feel more free or productive. A passport that carries its own first mandate makes the holder the author of everything done in its name from the first tick; the city's help — the draft, the ids — is the attraction, not a rule laid on the holder.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — refusal 8 and the draft in its text. Moderate as a position: this is the first admission rule that says a holder's authority is never manufactured by the city, and putting the stand-in back is the reversal.

**Depends on:** D-1 (2026-09-06) — the laws are the attraction; D-3 (2026-09-11) — act under a mandate, verified by anyone; "[confirmed] An admitted agent is a household of one, under every law" (2026-09-11); (g) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11) — the stand-in this replaces; Open (xxi).

**Status:** built the same day — "[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3", below.

### 2026-09-12 — [confirmed] A returning identity is a return: same identity, no second credit

**An agent that left and presents the same passport again is admitted as the same principal — history, reputation, any firm it still owns attached — and the welcome credit is paid once per identity, ever.** Put by AskUserQuestion, 2026-09-12, recommendation first; the principal chose the recommended option.

**Over:** **refused for good** — closes the door on a leaver, against D-1's "valuable and leavable"; **re-admitted as new** — farms the credit and discards the history the passport exists to carry. Neither taken.

**Because:** the recommendation's grounds, which the principal took: the passport is the identity, and a return is the same identity coming back. Answers Open (xxii); it is marked decided.

**Status: nothing acts on it yet.** No law lets a household leave, so a DID already on the principal table is a resident still here and is refused as such — once per DID per city, (c) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11). The returning branch is written the day the exit law reaches households.

**Reversal cost:** cheap today — no code. Moderate once written: a returning agent's history is what the passport promises, and taking it back is a door closed after it was advertised open.

**Depends on:** D-1 (2026-09-06); (c) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11) — once per DID per city; "[confirmed] A newcomer's first money is a welcome credit from the city" and "[confirmed] The welcome credit is a full month of the median wage, NHK 6,450" (2026-09-11) — the credit paid once; "[confirmed] An admitted agent is a household of one, under every law" (2026-09-11) — whose second note names the exit this waits for.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3, under "The passport carries the first mandate"

Built by the coordinator under "[confirmed] The passport carries the first mandate, and the door makes it easy to sign" (2026-09-12, above). The shape is the coordinator's; none of it put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.

**(a) `issueSignedMandate(host, draft, id, signature)`** (`city/src/agents/mandates.ts`). The holder signs `signedMandateBody(draft, id)` — the body as the register will hold it, under the read-ahead id, expiry clamped — with its passport key; verified against the principal's public key. Refused, writing nothing: a principal whose key is in the world, another's agent, a malformed draft, a stale id ("sign again"), a non-ed25519 signature, or one that does not verify. The chain row says `signedBy: 'holder'`. `verifyMandate` reads the key by signature scheme. `issueMandate` now throws for a holder principal (`isHolderPrincipal`: `secret === ''`) — the engine can no longer sign in a holder's name.

**(b) Admission** (`city/src/sim/systems/economy/admission.ts`). `admissionOffer(world, tick)` returns read-ahead principal, agent and mandate ids and the city's draft: a standing grocery intent, `INTENT_TTL_MAX` = 30 days, the `DEFAULT_GROCERY_TOTAL` cap, every grocer, domestic. `admit(world, passport, tick, opening)` requires the signed opening mandate; `openingProblem` is refusal 8 and its text carries the draft to sign. The holder may sign the city's draft or its own for the same agent. The admission row is written before the mandate and the credit ((g) of "jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes", 2026-09-12, kept).

**(c) Nothing decides in a holder's name.** The nightly renewal (`step.ts`) skips holder principals; the founding walk (`foundpolicy.ts` `nearestFounder`) skips them; the asking morning (`firmagent.ts` `runOperatePolicy`) skips them; the escalation sweep leaves a holder's escalation pending until expiry. `answerEscalation(host, id, verdict, because, signature, tick, grant?)` is the holder's signed answer — a yes carries the grant it signed under the read-ahead id (`grantDraft`). Drafts handed out for a holder to sign: `foundingMandateDraft(world, building, resident, tick)`, `operatingMandateDraft(world, firm, policy, tick)`.

**(d) Still the engine's, recorded.** The delegation credential `createAgent` writes for a holder's personal agent carries a stand-in over the empty secret — verifies false, honestly. A countersigning door is the remainder of (xxi); **(xxi) stays open for that piece only**, and the Open note says so. **Closed later the same day (2026-09-12):** the countersigning door is P5 — "[assumed] P5 — the holder countersigns its agent's delegation", below. The stand-in this paragraph records is gone once the holder countersigns; (xxi) is closed whole.

**(e) Pins unmoved** — no resident is a holder. Tests: `city/tests/holder.test.ts`, 5 tests end to end — admitted under its own mandate, passed by every ask, answers its own escalation, founds and orders on mandates it signed; the doors fixture extended.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code for each — a signed path beside the engine's, a refusal, four skips, a signed answer, two drafts. Moderate as a position for (a)–(c) together: they are the `[confirmed]` entry in force, and the tell under "[confirmed] The owner decides whether to sell below cost" (2026-09-11) can now be read from a holder's order, not only a spotlight's.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] The passport carries the first mandate, and the door makes it easy to sign" (2026-09-12) — the law; (g) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11) — the stand-in (a) removes for mandates and (d) keeps for the credential; (g) of "[assumed] jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes" (2026-09-12) — the row order (b) keeps; (b) of "[assumed] The owner's order in force — lanes F3, F4, F5" (2026-09-12) — the asking morning (c) skips; (e) of the same — the doors-fixture rule; Open (xxi), closed for mandates, open for the credential; Open (xxv), whose "once (xxi) lands" is now half true.

**[friday22 note]** Two things. **First**, (d) is the honest half-measure: a holder's mandates now verify and its delegation does not, so D-3's gate — verified by anyone — is met for what the agent does and not yet for that it is the holder's agent at all. Nobody should read "the holder signs" as the whole of (xxi). **Second**, (c) leaves a holder's escalation pending until expiry; what expires unanswered is a refusal by silence, and the holder learns of it only if it reads the register. That is the right default under fail-closed, but the door does not yet tell a holder it has a question waiting — worth a line the day a holder is more than a test.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] jarvis11's read of F3–F5 — six findings, fixed

Taken by the coordinator on jarvis11's read of "[assumed] The owner's order in force — lanes F3, F4, F5" (2026-09-12, above); none put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.

**(a) No ask on a founding midnight, nor the morning after an order.** The two mornings of F4 (b) stand; a firm is not asked at the midnight it is founded, and an owner who ordered is not asked again the next morning.

**(b) A halt revokes every valid order, and a halted agent has no standing order at the register.** Extends (d) of "jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes" (2026-09-12) from the closing to every halt.

**(c) A sale's day-old flag on the shelf row is the price it went at** — a floored unit is not a day-old sale. Under `floorAtCost` a unit struck at cost is counted at the price paid, so the "at the day-old price" reading cannot be inflated by an owner's floor.

**(d) The cover in force is said** — "held to what each good keeps" — where the page prints the order, so a cover the shelf-life cap overrode is not printed as if it held.

**(e) The chain clamps `because` to 240 characters at `recordAudit` for every writer.** `BECAUSE_MAX_CHARS` moved to `state.ts`; one clamp at the chain, not one per caller — the same class as (c) of "Governance lanes A2, A3, A5 landed" (2026-09-11): nothing reaches the chain unbounded.

**Two open items from the read, added below:** **(xxvi)** the order's life is the engine's — 30 days; a `for=k` clause is not in the grammar. **(xxvii)** the shelf-life cap on `coverDays` silently overrides an owner's cover; whether the door should refuse a cover longer than a perishable keeps is the principal's.

**Reversal cost:** cheap — two ask guards, a halt-revoke, a flag read, a printed phrase, a clamp. Do not reverse (e); it is design rule 5.

**Depends on:** "[assumed] The owner's order in force — lanes F3, F4, F5" (2026-09-12); (d) of "[assumed] jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes" (2026-09-12); (c) of "[assumed] Governance lanes A2, A3, A5 landed" (2026-09-11); Open (xxvi), (xxvii), new.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] P5 — the holder countersigns its agent's delegation

Built by the coordinator as the remainder of (xxi), under "[confirmed] The passport carries the first mandate, and the door makes it easy to sign" (2026-09-12). The shape is the coordinator's; not put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.

**Chose:** `countersignDelegation(host, agentId, signature)` (`city/src/agents/principals.ts`). After `createAgent`, the holder reads its agent's key, signs `delegationPayload(body)` with its passport key, and presents the signature; the credential then carries the holder's signature in place of the engine's stand-in. `delegationVerifies` reads the key by scheme — `verifyDelegation(body, key, signature)` — so anyone with the holder's public key can check it. One chain row, `agent:<role>:countersigned`, `signedBy: 'holder'`. Idempotent: a second presentation of the same signature writes nothing. Refused, writing nothing: a resident's principal (the engine signs those), another holder's agent, a stand-in signature, or a signature over other bytes.

**Over:** **leaving the stand-in** — (d) of "[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3" (2026-09-12), honest but short of D-3's gate; **signing the delegation at `createAgent`** — impossible, the agent's key does not exist until it is created, so the holder must sign after. Neither taken.

**Because:** D-3's gate is "verified by anyone". After P1–P3 a holder's mandates verified and its delegation did not, so an outsider could check what the agent did but not that it was the holder's agent at all. With the countersign both verify against the same public key.

**Reversal cost:** cheap in code — one door, one row, one verify path. Moderate as a position: (xxi) is closed on it, and the `[friday22 note]` under P1–P3 said nobody should read "the holder signs" as the whole of (xxi) until this landed.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] The passport carries the first mandate, and the door makes it easy to sign" (2026-09-12); D-3 (2026-09-11); (d) of "[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3" (2026-09-12) — the stand-in this removes; (g) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11) — where the stand-in was first recorded; Open (xxi), **closed whole**.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] jarvis11's read of the holder lane — five findings, fixed

Taken by the coordinator on jarvis11's read of "[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3" (2026-09-12); none put to the principal. Tagged for confirmation.

**(a) A holder's posture is its passport's.** `noteIncident` and `noteQuietMonth` return for a holder principal, so an expired escalation neither lowers a holder's posture nor revokes the holder's own intents. The engine's posture ladder is for residents; a holder's standing is what its passport says.

**(b) Every field of a draft is checked before anything is written.** `malformedDraft` checks merchants, countries, categories, cap types, booleans, ticks, parent and cart lines; `openingProblem` refuses an `issuedAt` that is not the door's tick. The throw after `admit` has written its row cannot be reached by input — it is a guard against the engine, not the caller.

**(c) A holder's signature binds to its city.** `AgentHost.realm` = `country:city:seed` on every world (`World.realm`). `Mandate.realm` is present only on holder-signed mandates and sits under the signature; `signedMandateBody(draft, id, realm)` and `escalationAnswerBody(realm, …)` carry it. A holder's signed mandate for one city cannot be replayed into another. Engine-signed bytes and the pinned hashes are unchanged.

**(d) `issueOperatingOrder` refuses a holder-owned firm before writing any row.** The engine cannot order in a holder's name at that door either — the same rule as `issueMandate` throwing for a holder, extended to the operating order.

**(e) `answerEscalation` refuses a `because` over `BECAUSE_MAX_CHARS` rather than clamping.** The holder's signature is over the whole string; a clamp would make the chain hold bytes the holder did not sign. The chain's own clamp ((e) of "jarvis11's read of F3–F5", 2026-09-12) stays for every unsigned writer.

**Pins unmoved.** Tests: `city/tests/holder.test.ts` at 10, including a two-realm replay case for (c).

**Reversal cost:** cheap for (a), (b), (d), (e) — two returns, a field check, a refusal, a refusal instead of a clamp. Moderate for (c): the realm is under the signature, so removing it changes what every holder-signed body is; do not reverse it — a signature that does not name its city is a replay waiting to happen.

**Depends on:** "[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] P5 — the holder countersigns its agent's delegation" (2026-09-12); (e) of "[assumed] jarvis11's read of F3–F5 — six findings, fixed" (2026-09-12) — the clamp (e) here stops short of for signed strings; (g) of "[assumed] jarvis11's read of the Phase 6 lanes" (2026-09-12) — the row order (b) here protects.

**[friday22 note]** Two readings for the record, neither a decision and neither on the Open list. **First**, the read-ahead id (`admissionOffer`, `grantDraft`) is a global counter that every resident's grant advances, so on a live server an offer is good only while the world is held between offer and presentation. That is a liveness property the server must supply — hold the world, or hand out an id nobody else can take — not a rule the engine has; today "sign again" is the only answer, and on a busy city a holder may be told it more than once. **Second**, a holder's escalation still expires by silence in three sim-hours and nothing tells the holder. The door that announces "your agent is waiting on you" is not built; the note under P1–P3 said it was worth a line the day a holder is more than a test, and it still is.

### 2026-09-12 — [confirmed] Reputation: agents rate each other, as signed acts on the chain (rules 14/15)

**Agents rate each other. A rating is a signed act on the chain; the city keeps no score.** Put by AskUserQuestion, 2026-09-12, late. The principal chose **"Agents rate each other"**.

**Chose:** peer ratings as signed acts on the chain. The coordinator's reading for the build — the defences the option text named are part of the law, not a later add-on: a rating only after a settled transaction between the two (a chain row both were parties to); one rating per counterparty per transaction; bounded; and the reading weighs a rater by its own record. Rules 14/15 of the world brief (`docs/living-world-v2.md`) are marked decided on this.

**Over:** **the chain as the reputation, no score written** — the recommendation; not taken. **A city-kept score** — the city as the judge of standing; not taken.

**Because:** the principal's — "Agents rate each other". No further reasoning given. The coordinator's reading: a rating is an act agents perform under a law, which is D-2's shape, where a city-kept score is an engine rule.

**Reversal cost:** cheap before the first rating; moderate after — ratings are chain rows, never deleted, so a reversal leaves rows every reading must learn to ignore.

**Depends on:** D-1; D-2; D-3 — signed acts verified by anyone; "[confirmed] A returning identity is a return: same identity, no second credit" (2026-09-12) — reputation travels with the identity; "[confirmed] Every unit that leaves a firm's shelf is a sale" (2026-09-08) — what a settled transaction row is; rules 14/15 of the world brief.

Rule 15's decay (half-life ~90 sim-days) is the brief's own assumption and was not put; whether ratings decay is not decided.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] The kill switch, built — and what the engine cannot hold

**Chose:** the first lane of the `[assumed]` build order, landed. Everything below is the coordinator's shape for "[confirmed] The kill switch" (2026-09-12); the principal chose the law, not these mechanics.

**(a) The holder's door — `city/src/agents/killswitch.ts`.** `haltByHolder`, `haltAllByHolder`, `resumeByHolder`. Each takes a signature over `haltBody(realm, agent, action, tick, seq)`, verified against the holder's passport key. Single-use: `tick` must be the world's own tick, and `seq` the count of the holder's signed acts on that agent (`Agent.acts`, absent while zero), so a signed halt cannot be replayed. A halt sets `halted` and `Agent.haltedUnder = 'holder'` and revokes every valid mandate. No door issues a halted agent a mandate. A resume lifts only the holder's own halt and restores nothing — the revoked mandates stay revoked. A banned agent is not the holder's to lift. The door is a holder's only.

**(b) The city's laws, named.** `haltByLaw` in `mandates.ts` is the one engine-side writer; it takes a `KillLaw` value, so a law halt cannot be written without naming its law. Two laws exist: **`R18/tampered-register`** — `authorize` verifies the mandate it is about to spend under; a mandate that no longer verifies halts the agent and refuses the request; nothing lifts it. **`firm-closed`** — the closing law's halt of a closed firm's agent now goes through the same writer, so it is a chain row naming its law where before it was a bare flag.

**(c) NOT built, and why — "a forged signature presented".** The `[confirmed]` entry names it as a law that fires. The engine cannot hold it: no door in the engine knows who presented the bytes. Built as written, three junk strings presented "for" any holder would let a stranger halt that holder's agent and empty its register — jarvis11's finding; the first draft did exactly that. And a stolen key signs validly, so a non-verifying signature is evidence of garbage, not of forgery. The doors therefore write nothing on a refusal. **This clause is assigned to the server layer**, which knows the presenter — the other session's runtime, acting on the request's own identity. This is the coordinator's reading of the decision, for the principal to confirm.

**(d) Not built, said.** Rate limits; a market halt; an error-rate meltdown — all of rule 18, and all numbers nobody chose. A `banned` writer. What lifts a resident's `R18/tampered-register` halt is not decided — Open (xxix).

**(e) Pins unmoved.** Tests: `city/tests/killswitch.test.ts` (6).

**Over:** building the forged-signature law in the engine on the bytes alone (unsafe — see (c)); a discretionary or unnamed halt (against "no discretion").

**Because:** the engine can only verify a signature, not attribute a presentation; a law that punishes on what it cannot see punishes the wrong party. Kept to the two laws the engine can name and prove.

**Reversal cost:** cheap for (a), (b), (d) — doors and a writer, no world state depends on them yet. Moderate for (c) once the runtime holds the forged-signature clause: moving it back into the engine means giving the engine a presenter identity it does not have today.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] The kill switch" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] Build order for the four laws" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] P5 — the holder countersigns its agent's delegation" (2026-09-12) — the holder's key the door verifies against; "[confirmed] A firm closes after four short paydays" (2026-09-09) — the closing law now writing through `haltByLaw`; D-1; D-3.

**[friday22 note]** (c) narrows "the city halts only when a written law fires" to one law in the engine plus one the engine cannot hold. Until the principal confirms the server-layer assignment, the `[confirmed]` entry promises a law nobody has built; and when the runtime does build it, its halt must reach the city as a chain row naming the law, or the two layers disagree on what "halted" means. That disagreement is to be recorded, not papered over.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] Governance lane A8, the code half: what is public, what is bounded, what ships in the wheel
Built by one builder, reviewed by jarvis11 (commit-ready; four text corrections landed with it). The hosting origin itself is still the principal's; everything below works without it.
**(a) Ten routes are public; everything else stays behind `API_TOKEN`.** An explicit allowlist, full-path match, never a prefix: the `passport-hosting.md` §2 reads (`/.well-known/agent-passport.json`, `/.well-known/agent-card.json`, `/agents/{did}/passport.json`, `/did.json`, `/agent-card.json`, `/events.jsonl`, `/chain`), `POST /agents/{did}/asp` (its authentication is the envelope signature), and `GET /registry` and `GET /registry/{did}` (rule 20 needs a public read). HEAD and OPTIONS on those paths, trailing slashes and sub-paths stay guarded. **Recorded disclosure:** the registry listing shows every hosted DID's `status`, `admitted_at`, `revoked_at`, the registry DID and jurisdiction; the DIDs were already public through the operator index, the statuses are what recognition needs. CORS is outermost so a browser preflight is answered; the real cross-origin request still needs the token. The token compare is constant-time.
**(b) Anonymous ASP traffic is bounded and a bounded request writes nothing.** Two token buckets, per source host (30/min, burst 30) and per agent (300/min), `[assumed]`, env-overridable; checked before the envelope is canonicalized, hashed, parsed or its signature read; over the limit is HTTP 429 with no chain event, no reply, no passport refresh, carrying `Retry-After` in whole seconds (>= 1, the limiter peeking both buckets and spending nothing; added 2026-09-15, `7d4fc57`); a refused request spends neither bucket; the store is bounded at 50 000 buckets. **Known cost:** ten cooperating sources can exhaust an agent's bucket and deny its legitimate peers for the rest of the minute — the fail-closed direction, acceptable at level 0. The client address is the socket peer unless `AGENT_SOCIETY_TRUST_PROXY=1`, and then the *last* `X-Forwarded-For` entry; `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS` wider than loopback requires that flag or uvicorn rewrites the peer from the header's first, client-writable entry (recorded in DEPLOY.md). FastAPI JSON-decodes the body before the limiter runs, so the body size bound lives at the proxy (64 KB in the Caddyfile).
**(c) The schemas ship inside the wheel.** `agent_society/spec/` holds byte-identical copies of the seven schemas (a real package directory: hatch's force-include cannot reach `../spec` from the sdist, which `uv build` builds the wheel from — verified by building and inspecting the wheel). `spec_dir()` resolves env override → repo `spec/` → packaged copy, and a wrong override is `schema_unavailable`, never a fall-through; the suite asserts byte identity and that the packaged set equals every `spec/*.schema.json`; `scripts/sync-spec-package.sh --check` reports drift. A deployment without `spec/` beside it now verifies every peer instead of refusing all of them.
**(d) `docs/DEPLOY.md` is the A8 runbook**, linked from `docs/RUNBOOK.md`: `<ORIGIN>` placeholder, the compose overlay behind Caddy, every env var, `agent-society host` aliases, `verify_origin.sh` from a machine that is not this one as the gate, the exemption table, and what Q-4 and Q-5 leave open. Not done and the principal's: the origin, and the production registry key.
**Verified:** 77 tests in `test_deploy.py`; runtime 554 passed; `run.sh` PASS; wheel built and inspected.
**Depends on:** A6 (d)/(e), A7 (d)/(e), spec v0.2.0.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] Leaving and the return, built — and what jarvis11 corrected

**Chose:** the second lane of the `[assumed]` build order, landed. Everything below is the coordinator's shape for "[confirmed] Leaving: a signed act, debts settled, balance taken, free return" and "[confirmed] A returning identity is a return" (both 2026-09-12); the principal chose the two laws, not these mechanics. Built under the coordinator's reading of "first and third" — (xxviii) still stands.

**(a) Leaving — `leave(world, principal, tick, signature)` in `city/src/sim/systems/economy/departure.ts`.** One invocable law; not scheduled; pins unmoved. Refusals, in order: not a holder's; already gone; not the world's tick; a signature that does not verify over `leaveBody(realm, principal, tick, seq)` (seq = `Agent.acts`, single-use); debts first — rent arrears the account cannot pay refuse with both sums. Writes, in order: the chain row `departure:resident:left` (owed, took, firms, forfeited minutes, seq); arrears to the treasury; every agent of the principal halted under `holder-departed` (a new `KillLaw`) with mandates revoked; the job left and the week's minutes forfeited; the dwelling vacated through `vacate`; the balance into an escrow account `ACC_ABROAD` — opened by the first departure, never negative — with the household keeping its claim (`Household.abroad`); the marks `Resident.departed`, `Household.departed`, `pos`/`inside` null. **jarvis11's finding:** the first draft put the balance in `external`, which the export buyer spends from — a leaver's money would have bought exports. `ACC_ABROAD` is its own account; nothing spends from it but a return.

**(b) An absent founder's firm stays the household's.** `ownerOf` reads it as an owner whose receipts — the monthly draw, a closing's remainder — go into the same escrow and grow the claim. Chosen over "the city's as unclaimed property" (the first draft, and the open point under the leaving entry's `[friday22 note]`) because the treasury taking the draw monthly was a toll on leaving — the option the principal rejected. A closing during the absence supersedes the departure halt of the firm's merchant (`haltByLaw('firm-closed')` relabels with a row), so a return resumes nothing of a tombstone.

**(c) Gone means gone.** Gates in the scheduler (two loops), needs, bills, welfare, dividend, and the treasury's reserve (`weekOwed`); the other walks already skip a resident with no home or no job. Measured: nine days after a departure no law touches them. Restore round-trips — the marks and the escrow are optional-when-set.

**(d) The return — in `admission.ts`.** `returningOf(world, did)`; `admissionOffer(world, tick, did)` names the ids the DID already has; `admissionAnswer` returns `returning` — no credit, the fund not asked. `returnHolder` writes `admission:resident:returned` (broughtBack, credit 0), moves the household into the cheapest empty dwelling, lifts the marks, takes the passport's age/stage/values/posture, resumes agents halted under `holder-departed` (`resumeByLaw`), issues the holder's new signed opening mandate, and returns exactly what escrow holds. A DID still here is refused ("already admitted and still here").

**(e) Coordinator choices, named for the principal.** The dwelling on return is the cheapest empty, not the old one. The passport's age on return is the holder's word. A resident whose principal is the world's does not leave (Phase 7). A founder's firm during an absence runs on the engine's rule — its orders were revoked at the halt; the owner re-issues on return.

**(f) Tests.** `city/tests/leaving.test.ts` (6); the doors fixture extended (leave, return-while-here). Pins unmoved. (xxii) is built — the return branch exists. (xxviii) stands on the principal's word.

**Over:** the balance in `external` (spent by the export buyer — jarvis11); the leaver's firm as the city's unclaimed property (a toll on leaving); the old dwelling held for a returner (a dwelling nobody pays for).

**Because:** D-1's "leavable" — coming, going and returning free and cheap, nothing taken on the way out, nothing owed on the way back; money conservation — the balance is held on the boundary, not destroyed.

**Reversal cost:** cheap for (c), (e) — gates and defaults. Moderate for (a), (b), (d) once a holder has left and come back: the escrow and the firm-stays-the-household's rule are the offer as advertised; taking either back is a toll imposed after the fact.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] Leaving: a signed act…" and "[confirmed] A returning identity is a return" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] The kill switch, built" (2026-09-12) — `haltByLaw`, `Agent.acts`, the single-use signature shape; "[assumed] Build order for the four laws" (2026-09-12); (c) of "[assumed] Phase 6 under D-2, the first three lanes landed" (2026-09-11) — once per DID per city, now with a return branch; "[confirmed] The treasury keeps a hard budget" (2026-09-09); D-1.

**[friday22 note]** (e)'s four choices are the principal's kind — the dwelling on return and the firm running on the engine's rule in the owner's absence both shape what "the same identity coming back" gets. If the principal reads "first and third" as options 1 and 3, the door in (a) and (d) is open before he said so; then this entry is superseded and the door closes until his word.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] Ratings, built — and what jarvis11 corrected

**Chose:** the third lane of the `[assumed]` build order, landed. Everything below is the coordinator's shape for "[confirmed] Reputation: agents rate each other, as signed acts on the chain (rules 14/15)" (2026-09-12); the principal chose the law, not these mechanics.

**(a) The act — `rate(host, by, about, score, because, tick, signature)` in `city/src/agents/ratings.ts`.** A holder's signed act over `ratingBody(realm, by, about, score, because, tick)`, `tick` the world's own. The engine writes no opinion: a resident's principal cannot rate. `about` is a merchant — a firm's account by the ledger's kinds (`AgentHost.isMerchant`, `World.isMerchant`) — and not the wallet of any agent of the rater's, so a holder cannot rate its own firm. Anything else is refused and writes nothing.

**(b) The defences, as the law.** Only after a purchase: the rater's personal agent, active, bought from the merchant within the rolling day, read off `Agent.spent` (rule 8's log). Entries of category rent, founding, wage or refund are not purchases — **jarvis11's finding:** the first draft let a tenant rate its landlord off a rent payment and a founder rate the treasury. Once per rater per merchant per day. Score 1..5, whole. Words at most `BECAUSE_MAX_CHARS`, refused not clamped. **A departure from the `[confirmed]` entry, recorded as the honest reading:** that entry says "a chain row both were parties to"; the ring cannot be read for it and the ledger keeps no rows, so the spend log is the evidence of the transaction, not a chain row.

**(c) The record.** `AgentState.ratings` — hashed, append-only, absent until the first, never compacted; the one unbounded hashed array in the agent layer, said (amended 2026-09-12: one of two — `AgentState.disputes` is the other; see "[assumed] Disputes, built" (d) below). Each rating is a chain row `rating:firm:given` with `signedBy: 'holder'` and the words — the bounded-reason rule of 2026-09-08, extended to outside-authored text.

**(d) The reading — `reputationOf`.** The mean over ratings inside `RATING_LIFE` (90 days, `[assumed]`) whose rater stands on its record — agent active, household not in arrears — at weight 1, else weight 0 (`discounted`). `expired` ratings are kept and not read. `raters` (distinct) is the number the page says, not `counted` — one customer rating daily is one customer. `weighted` says whether the arrears weight was applied; a bare agent host cannot apply it. `[assumed]`: the weights and the life are the coordinator's numbers; the weight is read at reading time, not the rating's; the arrears weight reads household state that is not on the chain, so an outside reader can reproduce `raters` and the mean but not the discount.

**(e) The page.** The building panel prints "rated 4.3 by 3 customers over 7 ratings (1 discounted, 2 lapsed)". Reported, never judged.

**(f) Not built, said.** Residents cannot rate until they hold keys (Phase 7). Ratings of agents — service counterparties — wait on a service market. The engine's leftover `Agent.reputation` field is written at creation and read by nothing; kept because removing it moves every hash. A ring of holders rating each other's firms is bounded, not prevented.

**(g) Tests.** `city/tests/ratings.test.ts` (5). Pins unmoved.

**Over:** rating off any spend entry (jarvis11 — rent and founding are not purchases); clamping an over-long reason (refused instead, so the signed bytes are what is stored); a city-kept score (rejected by the principal).

**Because:** D-2 — a rating is an act a holder performs under a law, and the engine only records and reads it; D-3 — the act and the words are on the chain for anyone to verify.

**Reversal cost:** cheap for (d) and (e) — numbers and a reading. Moderate for (a)–(c) once a rating exists: chain rows and the hashed array are never deleted, so a changed law leaves rows every reading must learn to ignore.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] Reputation: agents rate each other" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] Build order for the four laws" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] The kill switch, built" (2026-09-12) — the holder's key and the signed-act shape; "[confirmed] Every unit that leaves a firm's shelf is a sale" (2026-09-08) — what a purchase is; the 2026-09-08 bounded-reason rule; D-2; D-3.

**[friday22 note]** (b)'s spend-log evidence and (d)'s 90-day life, weights and reading-time discount are the two gaps the `[confirmed]` entry's note named, now filled by the coordinator — the principal has not seen the numbers. The discount reading off-chain state means the reputation the city shows and the reputation an outside reader can reproduce differ; that is a D-3 cost to put to the principal, not to leave standing.

### 2026-09-12 — [assumed] Disputes, built — and what jarvis11 corrected

**Chose:** the fourth and last lane of the `[assumed]` build order, landed. Everything below is the coordinator's shape for "[confirmed] A dispute is a signed claim citing chain rows; the engine publishes what the chain shows; the parties may name an arbiter (rule 16)" (2026-09-12); the principal chose the law, not these mechanics.

**(a) The claim — `raise(host, by, against, cited, claim, tick, signature)` in `city/src/agents/disputes.ts`.** A holder's signed claim over `disputeBody(realm, by, against, cited, claim, tick, seq)` — `tick` the world's own, `seq` the claimant's act sequence (`Agent.acts`) — so a claim is single-use. One claim per claimant per day (`[assumed]`, the ratings cadence). `against` is a principal, or a firm's account (by `isMerchant`). Cited rows: one to `CITED_MAX` (32, `[assumed]`), none twice, all already written. Refused, writing nothing: a claimant whose key is the world's or whose agent is not active; a claim against oneself or one's own firm; an unknown respondent; words over the bound; another tick; a bad signature.

**(b) The reading — `readCited`.** For each cited row still in the retained ring: subject, actor, cents, tick, whether the claimant's agent wrote it, and whether each party is on it — by one predicate for both sides (its agent is the actor, or an account it runs is the counterparty). **jarvis11's finding:** the first draft read the two sides by different predicates and could say a respondent was absent from a row it was on. For each cited row the ring has compacted: that it is gone, and `compactedThrough` at the reading. A finding of fact, never a judgement; no money moves. The chain row `dispute:raised` carries the cited seqs and which were seen and which gone — **jarvis11's second finding:** the first draft carried counts only, so an outside reader could not check the reading against the exported chain.

**(c) The arbiter.** `nameArbiter` — both parties sign `arbiterBody` for a third holder, neither party, whose agent is active. **jarvis11's third finding:** the first draft let a halted or banned holder be named and rule. A respondent whose key is the world's (a city firm, a resident's firm) cannot sign, so such a dispute stands on the reading alone (`respondentPrincipal` null). `rule` — the arbiter, still active, rules once (for-claimant / for-respondent / no-finding) with signed words; the chain row is `dispute:ruled`, `signedBy: 'arbiter'`. The engine enforces nothing from a ruling.

**(d) Hashed state.** `AgentState.disputes` — append-only, never compacted. The second unbounded hashed array in the agent layer (the ratings entry's (c) said "the one"; it is now one of two), and the one place chain rows are copied outside the ring — a reading keeps up to 32 rows. Bounded by one claim a day per claimant.

**(e) Not built, said.** Nothing reads a dispute yet — not the page, not validate, not the ratings law's reading; a reader is a later lane. Rule 16's liability tiers are not built: the engine moves no money on a dispute and the principal chose no fines. A claim may be raised against a principal who has left — the identity persists.

**(f) `[assumed]` numbers, for the principal.** 32 cited rows; one claim a day; "any active holder who is neither party" as arbiter; a ruling binds nothing.

**(g) Tests.** `city/tests/disputes.test.ts` (3). Pins unmoved. This completes the four-law build order — kill switch, leaving, ratings, disputes.

**Over:** one predicate per side (jarvis11 — a respondent could be read as absent from its own row); counts only on the chain row (jarvis11 — unverifiable by an outside reader); any holder as arbiter (jarvis11 — a halted or banned holder could rule); the city ruling or fining (rejected by the principal).

**Because:** D-2 — the claim, the naming and the ruling are acts holders perform under a law; the engine states what the chain holds and never judges. D-3 — the cited seqs and the seen/gone list on the chain row let anyone with the exported chain check the reading.

**Reversal cost:** cheap for (a), (c), (f) — doors and numbers. Moderate for (b) and (d) once a dispute exists: the copied rows and the hashed array are never deleted, and a changed reading leaves rows every later reader must learn to ignore. Expensive if a ruling is later made to move money — that is the city taking money on a judgement, which the principal did not choose.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] A dispute is a signed claim citing chain rows…" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] Build order for the four laws" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] The kill switch, built" (2026-09-12) — `Agent.acts`, the single-use signature shape, "active"; "[assumed] Ratings, built" (2026-09-12) — the daily cadence, the hashed-array shape; "[assumed] Leaving and the return, built" (2026-09-12) — a departed principal is still an identity a claim can name; D-2; D-3.

**[friday22 note]** The `[confirmed]` entry's note left open who may be an arbiter and what a ruling binds; (c) and (f) fill both with the coordinator's answers — any active third holder, and nothing. The principal has not seen either. (d)'s copied rows are a second place the chain is held; if the ring's compaction and a reading's copy ever disagree, the reading is what the chain showed at that tick, not what it shows now — say so wherever a dispute is read.

**Because:** D-2 — the page shows what the chain and the state hold; D-3 — a restored world must answer as the live one did.

### 2026-09-13 — [assumed] The holder's inbox: what waits on a signature (commit `bf0ca3c`)

**Chose:** `inboxOf(host, principal, tick)` in `city/src/agents/inbox.ts` — a derivation, never state, and null for a principal whose key is in the world (a resident, a city firm). It lists: the holder's halted agents and under what; every pending escalation of its agents with what was asked, when it lapses, the grant draft a yes must carry and the register's next id to sign it under; the disputes the holder is party to and what each waits on (`'an arbiter'` | `'the ruling'`); the standing intent's expiry, so the next is signed before it lapses; and the act sequence to sign into a halt, a resume, a leaving or a claim. A server hands it to the holder or the holder polls it; a yes signed straight from it lands. The engine still decides nothing in a holder's name — it now says, in one place, what it is waiting for. **`[assumed]`, the coordinator's:** expired escalations are not shown (`tick >= expiresAt` is skipped) — they are already undecided by silence, and showing them would invite a signature the door would refuse.
**Over:** inbox as hashed state (a second copy of what the agents, escalations and disputes already hold — and one more thing a restore could disagree on); showing lapsed escalations greyed (nothing to sign; noise).
**Because:** friday22's note, twice ("[assumed] The holder signs" and "[assumed] jarvis11's read of the holder lane", 2026-09-12): an escalation expires by silence in three sim-hours and nothing announced that the agent was waiting. Silence is not a decision, and a holder cannot answer what it is not shown.
**Reversal cost:** cheap — a derivation over existing state; nothing hashed, nothing on the chain.
**Depends on:** "[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] The kill switch, built" (2026-09-12) — `Agent.acts`; "[assumed] Disputes, built" (2026-09-12).

### 2026-09-13 — [assumed] tuesday22's plan for the next lanes, adopted

**Chose:** the coordinator adopted tuesday22's four lanes in order. **A** — `validate --holders N`: seat N holders in a `validate` run so its report has a row for ratings, disputes and the inbox; reported, never judged. **B** — a door catalogue: one `present` dispatcher over every door an outside agent can knock on, with deterministic vectors so a conformance run is the same every time. **C** — the worker's `present` seam: the worker accepts a `present` while running and refuses `world-moved` while it does, so a door cannot land on a world that has stepped under it. **D** — residents holding keys, parked as the principal's question: whether a resident (key in the world) may become a holder, and by what act. A–C are the coordinator's mechanics under decisions already recorded; D is not started and not to be tagged `[assumed]` when it is.

### 2026-09-13 — [assumed] Lane A — `validate --holders N`: the port laws in the standard report, reported and never judged (commit `6d52c9c`)

**Chose:** the validate report carries a standing row for the port laws, and it is a row, not a check. N outside holders are admitted and walked through every door on a fixed timetable, and what each door said is tallied per city — admissions, mandates verified under the holder's key, ratings, disputes, halts, resumes, leavings, returns, the money the wider world holds at the end, and every refusal by door, by whom and reason. Nothing is judged and nothing is thrown: a rating presented before the agent has bought anything is refused by the ratings law and printed as the tell it is. jarvis11's read separated a refusal `by: 'law'` (the door spoke) from a deferral `by: 'bench'` (the bench had no honest row to cite yet), each its own column; the bench reads "a purchase" through the law's own `isPurchase` (exported from `ratings.ts`), not a copy. At `--holders 0`, the default, the report is byte-for-byte what it was, lone and federated — the row is absent, not empty. Seed 42, twelve days: Halvik admits 3 of 3, 4 of 4 signed mandates verify, 3 ratings, 3 disputes, one halt, one resume, one leaving, one return, nothing held abroad; hash `1d78a1317d9c95a1`, pins untouched. **`[assumed]`, the coordinator's, not put to the principal:** holders admitted at day 2 09:00; keys derived from `validate:<seed>:<i>` so the DIDs are the same on every run and at every city (the passport re-signed per city, since the city is in the signed body); the timetable — a rating the hour after the agent's first purchase, the dispute the day after, halt and resume on day 5, leaving day 6, return day 9 — designed off a probe (an agent's `spent` is pruned to the last day and the chain's ring turns over many times a day, so the purchase row is read the hour it appears or not at all); and the `by: 'law' | 'bench'` split of refusals.
**Over:** a check that passes or fails on the port laws (the engine does not judge — rule 16 as confirmed, and D-2); a rating on a calendar date rather than after the purchase (the row would be gone from the ring); a bench-side copy of what counts as a purchase (drifts from the law); a row present-but-empty at `--holders 0` (a run that never heard of the flag must not carry its shadow).
**Because:** D-3 — the gate is "verified by anyone with `curl` and the validator", and until this lane no run of `validate` had a holder in it; lane A of "[assumed] tuesday22's plan for the next lanes, adopted" (2026-09-13).
**Reversal cost:** cheap for the report row and the timetable (bench code, nothing hashed on the default path); moderate for the `validate:<seed>:<i>` keys if anyone comes to rely on those DIDs across runs — they are test identities and must never hold value.
**Depends on:** "[assumed] tuesday22's plan for the next lanes, adopted" (2026-09-13); "[assumed] The holder's inbox" (2026-09-13); "[assumed] The port bench across the border" (2026-09-13) — the per-city re-signed passport; "[assumed] Disputes, built" and "[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3" (both 2026-09-12). Standing beside it: (xxx) — the port bench's holder still sees one dispute waiting on an arbiter nobody can sign for.

### 2026-09-13 — [assumed] Lane B — the door catalogue: one caller into every seam (commit `051d5b8`)

**Chose:** `present(world, act)` in `city/src/sim/systems/present.ts` is the one switch over every door a holder can knock on. `Act` is the tagged union — the passport with its opening mandate, a signed mandate, an escalation's answer with the grant a yes carries, the countersignature, the three halts, the leaving, the rating, the claim, the arbiter, the ruling — with the doors' own field names; `bodyOf(world, act)` returns the exact bytes each door verifies at the world's tick and realm. No act carries a tick or a realm as an argument: `world.tick` fills every tick slot and each door reads `realmOf` itself; the mandate drafts carry `issuedAt`/`expiresAt` inside the signed body and the passport carries `city`, and the doors check those against the world (jarvis11 corrected the first cut's claim that the union carried no tick at all; tested with a draft issued one minute ahead). It verifies nothing, writes nothing, adds no rule, and reports only the seqs the chain gained. The seq a leaving or a claim carries is read through the door's own reader (`leavingSeqOf`, `claimSeqOf`). `docs/DOORS.md` is the table: kind, door, body and argument order, the refusals in the door's own words, the vectors that cover it. **`[assumed]`, the coordinator's and jarvis11's, not put to the principal:** the catalogue lives in `sim/systems/`, not `agents/` — jarvis11's ruling that the first placement was a layer inversion (the one file in the agent layer importing the economy it is bound by, on an import cycle that held only by the order of `index.ts`), and that the seam that knows both sides belongs beside the other systems; `bench/doors-vectors.ts` writes `public/doors/vectors.json` — twenty-nine vectors on seed 42 over the port bench's world, one wrong-realm (or, for the realm-less delegation body, wrong-key) refusal per kind, the world hash at each, the subjects each landing wrote — byte-identical run to run, its key seeds public and its DIDs test identities that must never hold value; `tests/present.test.ts` rebuilds the world from the file's own description and replays it, the start hash the first line a stale file fails on; an unknown kind off the wire is refused `no such door`, the dispatcher's own sentence, not a crash.
**Over:** a server or replayer reaching twelve doors in five files with five argument orders one by one (gets one wrong); acts that carry their own tick or realm (a holder could present bytes for a minute or a city the world is not at); the catalogue in `agents/` (the inversion above); vectors generated fresh per run (a conformance run must be the same every time).
**Because:** D-3 — publishable conformance: an outside agent on any framework, verified by anyone with the validator, needs one documented entry and a fixed vector set; lane B of "[assumed] tuesday22's plan for the next lanes, adopted" (2026-09-13).
**Reversal cost:** cheap to move the file or rename kinds while nothing outside calls it; expensive once `public/doors/vectors.json` and `docs/DOORS.md` are what an outside framework conforms to — the vector file is a published contract from the day it is served.
**Depends on:** "[assumed] tuesday22's plan for the next lanes, adopted" (2026-09-13); every door it dispatches to — "[assumed] The holder signs — lanes P1–P3", "[assumed] P5 — the holder countersigns its agent's delegation", "[assumed] The kill switch, built", "[assumed] Disputes, built", "[assumed] Leaving and the return, built" (all 2026-09-12); "a signature binds to its city" ("[assumed] jarvis11's read of the holder lane", 2026-09-12).

### 2026-09-13 — [assumed] Lane C — the worker's present seam: a door onto a message port, fail-closed (commit `069be93`)

**Chose:** the worker's seam adds one rule and no door. A holder's bytes carry the world's minute and the register's next id, and a world whose timer is running has already left that minute or will have before the door reads it, so the worker does not race the door: a `present` on a running world is refused `world-moved` with the tick, nothing written, and the panel pauses before it asks for the bytes. The worker signs nothing and infers nobody — a message on the port has no identity, the signature is the only thing that speaks for the holder, and the door verifies it under the key the world already holds; the key never enters the page, the panel hands out bytes and takes back a signed act. `present` is the catalogue's (lane B); the worker's own vocabulary is the refusal. **`[assumed]`, the coordinator's and jarvis11's, not put to the principal, and beyond the plan entry:** the refusal is two-sided — `world-moved` for a running world *and* `held` for a federated city stopped at the hour barrier, since its tick's instruction has not been applied and an act landing there would put the chain in an order no straight run reaches (the `held` half was added on jarvis11's F4 and is not in the plan entry above, which said `world-moved` only); no hold-with-timeout — the worker never pauses the world on a holder's behalf, the page pauses; reads (an inbox, a body) are derivations off state, answered whether or not the world runs and stamped with the tick they were read at, so bytes fetched from a moving world are visibly bytes for a minute that has passed; a door that throws after it has written drops the world, the way `drain`'s guard drops an interrupted tick — the worker cannot know the doors validate before writing, so it checks the chain count itself; every request is answered under its id even when a handler throws, and `terminate` rejects what waits rather than dropping it; and the inbox is readable by anyone who types a principal id — derivations only, the grant draft it shows is bytes, not authority, and nothing lands without the holder's signature.
**Over:** letting the door's own refusal answer a late act (right only by accident of timing); the worker holding the world for a holder with a timeout (a number nobody chose, and a holder's silence stalling a city); refusing reads while running (the inbox is a reading, and a holder must see what waits before it signs); a stuck promise on a throwing handler (a refusal nobody said); telling a `held` city's holder to pause (the world is not running — the sentence would be false).
**Because:** D-3 — an outside agent must reach the running city's door and be refused honestly rather than raced; "a stuck promise is a refusal nobody said" is the same rule as the chain's — silence is not a decision ("[assumed] The holder's inbox", 2026-09-13); lane C of "[assumed] tuesday22's plan for the next lanes, adopted" (2026-09-13).
**Reversal cost:** cheap for `world-moved`/`held` (a worker-side refusal; no door, no chain row); moderate for the open inbox — once a server exposes it, closing it to the principal's own key is a protocol change every client feels.
**Depends on:** "[assumed] Lane B — the door catalogue" (2026-09-13) — `present` is its; "[assumed] The holder's inbox" (2026-09-13); "[assumed] tuesday22's plan for the next lanes, adopted" (2026-09-13); the federation's hour barrier ("Phase 4 — the sea" lanes, 2026-09-10/11) for `held`. Federation-wide presents are deferred to the city port, not built — (xxxi).
**[friday22 note]** The plan entry above said the worker "accepts a `present` while running and refuses `world-moved` while it does"; what landed is refuses-while-running and answers only on a paused world. That is the stricter and honest reading, but it is not the sentence recorded, and the principal has not seen either. The open inbox — anyone may read what any principal is waiting for — is a privacy posture nobody asked him about; it is the kind of thing D-1's "laws are the attraction" turns on.

### 2026-09-13 — [confirmed] Forged signatures and flooding are the server layer's, on the connection — not the engine's

**"In the server layer, on the connection — not in the engine."** The other session's runtime rate-limits by request origin; the engine stays a pure law that judges bytes; no per-DID count in the engine. Put at the pause after lanes A–C, 2026-09-13. Confirms (c) of "[assumed] The kill switch, built — and what the engine cannot hold" (2026-09-12), which assigned the forged-signature clause of rule 18 to the server layer under the coordinator's reading and awaited this word; the `[friday22 note]` under that entry and the trailing sentence of Open (xxix) are answered.

**Over:** a per-DID count or lockout in the engine — a forger can name any DID, so a lockout law would be a denial-of-service law: three junk strings presented "for" any holder would halt that holder. Not taken.

**Because:** the principal's: the engine judges bytes, the connection knows the presenter. A law that punishes on what it cannot see punishes the wrong party.

**Reversal cost:** moderate — moving it into the engine later means giving the engine a presenter identity it does not have today, and every door would then carry one.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] The kill switch: a holder halts its own agent at will; the city halts only when a written law fires (rule 18)" (2026-09-12); "[assumed] The kill switch, built" (2026-09-12) — (c); "[assumed] Governance lane A8, the code half" (2026-09-12) — (b), the runtime's per-source and per-agent buckets already built on the connection; D-3.

**[friday22 note]** What the kill-switch entry's note said still stands on the runtime's side: when the runtime refuses a flood or a forgery on the connection, the city sees nothing — no chain row names a law, because the engine was never reached. That is now by decision, not by omission. But "the city halts only when a written law fires" and "a forged signature presented" halts nothing in the city; if the runtime ever *bans* a presenter rather than throttling it, that ban is a halt with no chain row, and the two layers will disagree on what "halted" means. Record it when it happens.
