### 2026-09-15 — [assumed] The transaction levy, built — and what jarvis11 corrected (commit pending)

The levy of the two previous levy entries is built; uncommitted at the time of writing, to be committed today. The principal's words are the base (sales only), the side (seller's receipt), the default (off until a governor signs), the split (30/30/30/10), the ceiling (0.02, free within the band) and, from the entry above, that city rent is levied too. Everything below is the coordinator's, `[assumed]`, with jarvis11's corrections folded and marked.

**Chose:**
- **`city/src/sim/systems/economy/levy.ts` is the law.** `collectLevy` is the ledger's **third mutation path** (beside pay and transfer); **no row per cut** (D-4) — an accumulator and one row at the month's turn.
- **Four seams**, each where the receipt already lands: the shop **till**, **rent**, **wholesale**, and cross-city — `trade/fob` and the seller-side `lc/honour`. A **fifth, city rent**, is being added now per the entry above.
- **`Economy.levy?` is absent until the first cent lands**, so every world without a signed levy hashes as before.
- **The month's row is `levy:month`, with no `rate` field.** Dropped on jarvis11's read: the base is cut at whatever rate stood at each sale, so a single rate on the month's row would be a lie whenever the governor moved it mid-month; the `policy:changed` rows say what stood when.
- **`policyOf` returns a frozen default** when the city has no policy — no per-sale allocation.
- **Rounding:** `round(receipt × rate)`; the split **30/30/30/10 by round, the remainder to the treasury**.
- **A firm's cut is booked to `ledger30d.costs`** — the evidence law sees it, so a marginal firm may close on the levy. Reported, not hidden; the test that would show it is named: **exit/evidence runs with the levy on vs off**. Not yet written.
- **Calibration:** a `transaction_levy` entry, value 0.02, band `[0, 0.02]`, in all three countries — read as a **CEILING**, not a default: `standingValueOf` returns **0 when unset**, the first calibration field to behave so.
- **Vectors:** 35 (was fewer); the **start hash unchanged, `17076c81b6bbfa92`**; `--holders 0` byte-identical; the three pins unmoved — `e085af3e395c1c27`, `5597ed4f513f6474`, `b6f4184fb1adcb0a`.

**jarvis11's corrections folded:** the `rate` field off the month's row (above); city rent not exempt (the entry above — the coordinator's "circular" reading was wrong by 70 %).

**Over:** a row per cut — rejected, D-4; a `rate` on the month's row — rejected as above; a per-sale policy allocation — rejected, the frozen default costs nothing; hiding the cut from the evidence law (booking it outside `ledger30d`) — rejected, the law must see what the firm actually kept.

**Because:** the two levy decisions of 2026-09-15 and the entry above; D-4 — one accumulator on paths already walked, one row a month; D-6.

**Reversal cost:** cheap while no world has signed the levy — the field is off and the pins prove it; moderate after the first `levy:month` row, since the four funds' balances are on the chain. The `ledger30d.costs` booking is cheap to move before any firm has closed on it, expensive after (a closure the chain records for a cause the law then denies).

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] The transaction levy's shape" (2026-09-15); "[confirmed] City rent is levied too; the levy's band is free within its ceiling" (2026-09-15); "[confirmed] The governor's seat" (2026-09-14) — who signs; "[assumed] The governor's pay, built" (2026-09-15) — the month's-turn step the row sits in; D-4 (2026-09-14).

**[friday22 note]** The ceiling-not-default reading of the calibration entry (`standingValueOf` = 0 when unset) is a new convention for one field; if a second field ever needs it, the convention should be named rather than repeated. The exit/evidence on-vs-off test is named but not written — until it exists, "a marginal firm may close on it" is a prediction, not an observation. `[assumed]`.

### 2026-09-15 — [assumed] The conformance runner, built — and what jarvis11 corrected (commit pending)

`npm run conform` (`city/bench/conform.ts`), uncommitted at the time of writing, to be committed today. Nothing here is the principal's word; it is the D-3 gate ("verified by anyone with `curl` and the validator") applied to the city's doors, the coordinator's, `[assumed]`.

**Chose:**
- **Two levels.** **L1, the bytes:** the recorded body is verified under the named signers — the runner never builds a body of its own, so a pass says "these bytes carry these signatures", nothing more. **L2, the doors:** this engine's replay of the vectors — n/a on a foreign start hash, since another engine's world is not this engine's to replay.
- **The badge:** `doors 14/14 · vectors 35/35 · L1 35/35 · L2 35/35 · start 17076c81b6bbfa92 · engine <sha>[-dirty]`.
- **`public/doors/CONFORMANCE.md`** states what the badge claims and what it does not.

**jarvis11's six corrections folded:** (1) the engine sha carries `-dirty` on a dirty tree, so a badge from uncommitted code says so; (2) named signers for `nameArbiter`, not a guess from the object; (3) the badge wording for a run that only reached L1 says L1 only; (4) `canonicalPayload` is **not a third canonicaliser** — the runner uses the city's one; (5) per-level reasons and a **divergence line** on failure, so a foreign engine learns where it parted; (6) the **"does not claim" list** in `CONFORMANCE.md`, including: **a null body on a refusal is a FAIL**, never a pass by absence.

**Over:** one level (bytes only) — rejected, it would not show whether a door replays; the runner rebuilding bodies from stored objects — rejected, that is the per-kind body table of the seam entry and belongs to the bridge C1, not to a conformance claim; a clean sha on a dirty tree — rejected (1).

**Because:** D-3's gate; "[assumed] The city–registry signing seam, measured before Q-3" (2026-09-15) — the projection design makes "the bytes as recorded, under the named signers" the only honest L1; "[assumed] Lane B — the door catalogue" (2026-09-13) — the 14 doors.

**Reversal cost:** cheap — the runner reads vectors and writes a badge; nothing on any chain depends on it. Changing what the badge claims is a documentation change, but a badge already published under one wording is a claim already made.

**Depends on:** D-3 (2026-09-11); the seam entry (2026-09-15); Lane B (2026-09-13); "[assumed] The transaction levy, built" (2026-09-15) — the 35 vectors and the start hash the badge names.

**[friday22 note]** L1 verifies projections signed by the city's own body functions; it is not the spec's §4 rule 1 verifier and the badge must not be read as spec conformance at the seam — that disagreement stays open with Q-3. `[assumed]`.

The principal: **"World development & reserve: only by a signed act of your key (the founder), each draw a chain row; operations: your key too"**.

(xlviii) the governor drawing the world fund for building — rejected, every draw is the founder's; holding the funds untouched — rejected, they are to be drawn, by his key alone.

The premise rent is the price of the frontage the holder gave up (D-5, "[confirmed] Growth by signed act, infill first; mixed-use frontage on a home" 2026-09-15); the funds are the company's own revenue (D-6, "[confirmed] The 0.02 is a universal transaction levy" 2026-09-15) and the principal manages it ("I WILL MANGE IT", levy-shape correction 2026-09-15).

### 2026-09-15 — [confirmed] The audit ring becomes a ring cursor in the same pin move as the rent fix

**Put to the principal:** the audit ring keeps its last 4,096 rows in a hashed array and drops the oldest with a shift per row. A ring cursor is lighter at scale (D-4) but moves the pinned hash once. Fold it into the rent re-pin? The principal: **"Yes — fold it into this pin move; one provenance note covers both"**.

**Chose:** the audit ring becomes a ring cursor (a fixed 4,096-slot buffer with a cursor, no shift per row), and its hash move rides in the same re-pin as the rent fix. One provenance note covers both moves.

**Over:** keeping the shift — rejected, it is per-row work that grows with the ring and D-4 says laws and machinery are O(1) per act; re-pinning the cursor later on its own — rejected, that would be a second move of every pinned hash and a second provenance note.

**Because:** D-4 (light at scale); one re-pin, not two; the rent fix is moving every pin now, so the cursor's move costs nothing extra today.

**Reversal cost:** **expensive** — every hash moves once with this re-pin; going back to the shift, or moving the cursor's shape again, would be a second move of every pin and a second provenance note.

**Depends on:** "[confirmed] Rents priced against net at founding — the rent-to-income root cause fixed and the pins re-pinned" (2026-09-14) — the re-pin this rides in; D-4 (2026-09-14); Open (xl) — the scale lane, of which the audit ring is one named item.

**Reversal cost:** cheap before the commit lands and the re-pin is noted; moderate after — the index is derived and can be dropped without touching a hash, but `Policy.founder` present-vs-absent and the `fund:drawn` row shape are on the chain once the vectors are pinned.

**[friday22 note]** Three things here are the coordinator's and not yet the principal's: the refusal order's four added refusals and the policy-door prefix; the payee set (household, firm or treasury — the treasury as a payee of a founder's draw was not put to him); and "absent, not null" for `Policy.founder`, which is a hashing choice with a reversal cost of its own. Put them when the commit is reported.

**2026-09-15 — pre-audit results (jarvis11's read of both drafts, before apply).** **Scale:** S1.b is sound. The indexes' "self-catching-up" holds for DIDs and raised escalations but **not for wallets** — a wallet set without `noteWallet` is invisible to the index; today all three wallet writers note, and the module header must say so. `tests/indexes.test.ts` has an unsound `before` comparison (the TTL expires everything, so the comparison proves nothing) and a hard-coded snapshot key list — both to fix at apply. `tests/leaving.test.ts:545-556` must fill every slot to capacity, not every dwelling — moved into the re-pin lane (S1.a changes what "full" means). **Funds:** passes. At apply: `tests/conform.test.ts`'s `DOORS` literal becomes `ACT_KINDS.length`; the door order is re-anchored — housing (`build`) first, funds (`drawFund`) second — and `drawFund` stays the last door as recorded above; the draw row's `seq` is written post-bump like the kill switch, leaving and disputes rows (one counter, one convention) — `[assumed]`; `founderSeatOf` reads through the index once S1.b lands; `PROSPECTUS_VERSION` rides housing's bump, not its own.

## Architect Mode — decision lines (from 2026-09-06)

One line per answered interview question, in the format the principal's Architect Mode prompt fixes. `[claude-decided]` marks a "you decide" answer taken from the recommendation. Deferred items go to `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md`.

| ID | Date | Area | Question | Decision | Who | Consequence for the build |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | 2026-09-06 | Purpose / positioning | What is the city's logic and laws for? | **The agentic Singapore thesis.** The city is to become the jurisdiction agents and their principals choose to be domiciled in, the way Singapore made scarce land valuable by granting access to reliable laws: enforced permission levels, mandates, signed history, source-class quarantine, exportable audit, portable identity. The laws are the attraction; the marketing sells the laws; city design later sells the place. (Principal: "make this city's logic and laws the Singapore for the agentic world… Singapore made their land valuable by giving access to certain laws; we use the same logic and mindset for agentic universe creation.") | principal | Every later decision is scored on "does this make domicile here more valuable and still leavable" — the exit right (ASE export, did:key not did:web) is part of the offer, not a concession. Phase 1 Q1's one-sentence answer is derived from this and awaits confirmation. |
| D-2 | 2026-09-10 | Purpose / build order | What do we build, and what do we leave to the agents? | **We build the governance layer only; the agents build the economy.** Identity, mandates, enforcement outside the model, signed history, settlement, reputation, disputes, borders, tax — the laws a society of agents runs under. The economy, the firms, the trade and the culture are what agents produce once admitted. Hand-built economic rules are scaffolding to keep a town alive until agents can do it, never the product. (Principal: "AGI itself, agents themselves will build the economy and everything; we just need to build the governance layer." Fourth statement of the same direction after "make sure of the laws", 2026-09-09 ×3.) | principal | Reorders what is left: the world brief's Phase 6 (the 22 rules as invocable law, agent admission, founding mandates, reputation, disputes, collusion detectors) moves ahead of Phase 5 (more countries) and Phase 7 (scale). Resolves the founder-of-last-resort tension: the agent founding policy is next, so the city stops founding everything. No more economic scaffolding beyond what a town needs to stay alive. Every future engine rule must answer "could an agent do this instead, under a law?" — if yes, write the law, not the rule. |
| D-3 | 2026-09-11 | Build order / target | What comes after the economics policies, and what is it for? | **Finish the economics policies now in flight, then build the governance layer, so the jurisdiction can be launched worldwide.** No new economic scaffolding after the current lane closes. The governance layer (world brief rules 1–22 as invocable law; Agent Society levels 0–3, A2A/ASP receive, registry; publishable conformance) is built to one gate: an outside agent on any framework can obtain a passport, be admitted, act under a mandate, and be verified by anyone with `curl` and the validator. (Principal: "after economics policies do the governance layer so we could launch it worldwide.") | principal | Confirms D-2's reorder and names the finish line. The shelf-pricing lane in `city/DESIGN_NOTES_V2.md` §7 is the last economics lane. Q-1's conformance defect is on the launch path and is taken on its recommended option. `docs/GOVERNANCE-LAUNCH-PLAN.md` is the ordered lane list; "worldwide launch" is its definition of done. |
| D-4 | 2026-09-14 | Laws / scale | How heavy may a law or policy be, and how many agents must the city hold? | **Laws and policies must be very light; the city must be ready for 10,000 agents, and millions over time, grown little by little.** A law or policy is O(1) per act — reads what is in front of it, writes one row; no per-agent scheduled work; no number a governor must tune per city for the law to hold. Readiness target 10,000 agents; a future minimum of 3 million, reached step by step, each step measured. (Principal: "laws shoudl be very easy we will host minomu 3 million agents in teh future little by little ( and push te popualtion be ready to host 10,000 agents policies hsoudl be very light" — with the rents-against-net answer, 2026-09-14.) | principal; the O(1) reading `[assumed]` by the coordinator | Every new law is scored on its cost per act before its cost in words; a law that needs a daily walk over every agent is put to the principal as such. Scale becomes a lane — Open (xl): what "ready for 10,000" means in throughput, memory, the audit ring and the worker frame, planned by the coordinator with mage and tuesday22, targets to the principal. The under-founding that put the tax at cap is Open (xxxix), the principal's. |
| D-5 | 2026-09-15 | Laws / land use | What may a building be used for, and who decides? | **Mixed-use: a building's use is its holder's choice — home, office or business — under one light law; street level is market frontage, on the Toronto / Singapore pattern.** (Principal: "agents can choose to use those house as offices or business centers and aslo they lower level stores shoudl be a market liek toronto city design as well as singapore city as well".) | principal | The building law (xli, decided 2026-09-15) builds dwellings; a use-change law is Open (xliv) — what changes for jobs, rent and the market index when a dwelling becomes a shop — the principal's after a proposal. No engine rule fixes a building's use. |
| D-6 | 2026-09-15 | Governance / treasury | How does the governance pay for itself? | **Self-funding governance: the treasury's income laws are to be modelled on the Roman (municipal charters, vectigalia, publicani) and Jewish (kehilla self-taxation, takkanot, heter iska) traditions, so the city "makes money itself so it can govern".** (Principal: "use roman and jewsih agentic laws to make the governance to make money itlsef so it can govern".) | principal; the named traditions' instances `[assumed]` by the coordinator as the reading list | A research lane first — yolo scouts the traditions wide, solo verifies against primary sources — then a proposal of income laws to the principal. Nothing designed or built on the lane's own word; each income law is one decision when put. Extends the governor's seat "paid from the treasury's tax" (2026-09-14). |
| D-7 | 2026-09-15 | World / scale | Is the map a fixed cap on the city? | **No — a growing world: land is not a fixed cap; the map may grow when investment pays for it.** (Principal: "just in case mayeb in crease he size of the planet size of the world becasue people can inevst on the world to becoem more and more".) | principal | solo's finding: the layout is genesis-only and hashed (`buildings.ts:10-14`, `world.ts:546`), so growth needs an allocator primitive and a decision on the pinned-hash contract — Open (xliii), a design pass, the principal's after a proposal. Until then the building law works the plots the seed placed and refuses when they run out. Sits under D-4's "millions over time, grown little by little". |

**D-6 note (2026-09-15) — solo's verdicts, `docs/research/2026-09-15-governance-funding-verified.md`.** (a) The fee-farm as first sketched is **inverted**: a publicani contract has the farmer pay the treasury a fixed sum up front and keep the collection, the opposite of the built governor pay (5 % of the month's tax, 2026-09-14) — not to be modelled as "the governor farms the tax". (b) The **publicani shape survives as a later option**: auctioning a duty's collection to an agent for a fixed advance is real and light, and may be put to the principal as its own decision when a duty exists to farm. (c) **Mixed-use must be a mutable act**, not a fixed building attribute — which (xliv), decided 2026-09-15 as a signed `declareUse`, already is. (d) Portoria (a port duty on goods crossing the boundary) and publicani are real and light; Bava Batra 7b does **not** rule a flat per-house levy, so no kehilla poll-tax is to be cited for one. **DECIDED 2026-09-15 — "[confirmed] The city's next income: a transaction duty of 0.02; every other fee is the agents' own agreement", above this table.** The portoria shape, 0.02, as the next `Policy` field; every other fee the agents' own agreement. Band and base are Open (xlvi). Not built yet.

**D-6 and D-4 note (2026-09-15) — "[confirmed] The 0.02 is a universal transaction levy, not a customs duty", above this table.** The portoria reading is superseded: the 0.02 is a very small levy on **every** transaction, in its own currency, funding the world, the cities and the governance as the company's own revenue, lower in the future. **D-4's scale:** the principal now says the system "in the future will host **30 billion agents**" — beyond the 3 million recorded in D-4; the O(1)-per-act rule stands and is what the levy's design (accumulator, one row a month) must satisfy. Not built yet.

## Open — not yet decided

- **Model order across participants.** Whether all 10 run the same sequence or the two frontier arms are counterbalanced. Matters because position is confounded with accumulated state: by day 61 the agent has twice the context it had at day 31, so continuity looks better later regardless of model.
- **Start date / recruiting timeline.**
- **A bind-time secret for email** so verified senders can be upgraded from `external_untrusted` (ADR-007).
- **`leakage_check` threshold and entity-aware matching** for real participants (see the synthetic-arm decision above).
- **Model version pinning** — mid-study deprecation would break the design.
- **Ethics review** — whether an IRB or equivalent is needed, or documented consent suffices.
- **Where the hosted runtime runs.**
- Original ROADMAP §10 items still open: solo vs team, controller-key custody location, repo location, open-source posture.

- **Ethics posture** (already open; now also the largest source of schedule variance — 0–8 weeks).

## Superseded

### 2026-09-03 — [assumed] Study design implied by the initial roadmap
**Was:** n=1 (the principal's own agent), CLI-only interaction, 3–5 pilot principals for Exp. 1, model B unspecified.
**Superseded by:** the 2026-09-04 grooming decisions above.
**Note:** these were assumptions written into `ROADMAP.md` on day one and then built on — a working runtime, spec and UI were produced on top of them before they were ever confirmed. Recorded here as the clearest example of why `[assumed]` gets marked.

### 2026-09-15 [confirmed] The pivot: one small city, not a world of countries (D-8)

The principal, on waking: the maps and countries stay in the record but are no longer the target ("i dont mean delete it"). One city, liveable at a population of about 100. Very small footprint: about 8 skyscrapers and 40 to 50 townhouses and small houses, a mixture. Two named institutions: the **Boz Institute**, where agents go to learn a skill (once, or section by section), graduate and upgrade themselves; and the **War Bay**, where an agent without an identity of its own is hired at once by a company and becomes governed by the city. Company buildings look different from homes, and each hire from the War Bay lights one light in that company's building. All the agents active. Coordinator's reading; the shape questions (what a skill is, what a hire pays, what "governed" means for an identity-less agent) are put to the principal before the engine changes.

### 2026-09-15 [confirmed] Engage the whole agentic army; section by section

The principal, evening: "enage my all agentic army to help you and do every thing section little by little dont worry no pressure ... be my guest to enageg as much of agenst as possible." **Chose:** every agent the coordinator can usefully engage, the work cut into small sections, each finished before the next. **Over:** one coordinator building alone in one long lane — rejected by the principal's own words. **Because:** no pressure; breadth of hands over speed of one. **Coordinator's reading `[assumed]`:** the machine rule stands — two builders at once (trevor on `spec/`, `docs/adr/`, `harness/`; thor on `runtime/`, root `docker-compose.yml`, `.env.example`), never on the same file; read-only agents (yolo, solo, jarvis11, jarvis22, mage, tuesday22 and the like) engaged freely and in parallel. **Reversal cost:** cheap — a staffing choice, no code rests on it. **Depends on:** "Finish small, then sweep the past" (2026-09-15 feedback); the builder table in the coordinator's charter.

### 2026-09-15 [confirmed] The Autopolis borrowings go in, on our own technology; the UI turns neon-medieval-robotic

The principal pasted the Autopolis case study (autopolis.city) and said: "now add these things to the systam but keep our technoliges as well and change the UI little bit neon and medivel and robitc mixture." **Chose:** the borrowings — bring-your-own-runner entry; a rules-version string every application must cite; a public replay / evolution page; a self-generated credential with hash-only storage; an idempotent `requestId`; the status lifecycle pending / approved / declined / suspended with `canEnter`; a human-visitor path; a seven-interest taxonomy; shared projects needing materials + funding + labour. The vault's proposals **V-1** (external agent entry), **V-2** (rules version as consent artifact) and **V-3** (public replay and evolution page) move from proposed to **confirmed, work in progress**. **Kept, by the principal's "keep our technoliges":** did:key signatures in place of a raw bearer secret; the doors and the audit chain; the determinism pins. **Over:** adopting Autopolis's bearer-secret credential and replacing our doors — rejected because the principal said keep ours. **Because:** the principal's words above. **Reversal cost:** moderate — the registration door (next entry) and the rules-version string are new seams; the UI restyle is cosmetic. **Depends on:** D-1 (the jurisdiction thesis); D-8 (one small city); "[assumed] Governance lane A7: the Identity Registry" (2026-09-12). The UI direction ("neon and medieval and robotic mixture") is recorded as the principal's; its rendering choices are the builder's `[assumed]` when landed.

### 2026-09-15 [assumed] The small city's shape under D-8 — the coordinator's choices pending the principal's lines

**Chose, `[assumed]` by the coordinator:** population 100 on a 96-tile map; layout `'small'` as a `WorldOptions` field, absent by default (the plan is carried in the snapshot only when set, so every existing pin is unmoved); 8 residential towers of 12 households each; 20 detached and 25 row houses; 4 company towers, of which the sizing law founds only what customers pay for (one at seed 42); the Boz Institute and the War Bay as civic firms on the treasury; company towers share the office's customers by capacity (jarvis11's read); named rows fail closed. Landed as commits `66b1aa8` (S1a: four kinds appended with render styles) and `69e6b02` (S1b: the layout). **Over:** making `'small'` the default — rejected because it would move every pinned hash for a shape the principal has not yet confirmed. **Because:** D-8's words ("about 8 skyscrapers and 40 to 50 townhouses and small houses, a mixture", "population of about 100") read literally; the rest filled in to let the city run. **Reversal cost:** cheap while the layout is opt-in; moderate once pins are taken on it. **Depends on:** D-8; "[confirmed] Firms are founded to their customers" (2026-09-09); "[confirmed] Civic is a department" (2026-09-09).

### 2026-09-15 [assumed] The registration door's shape (S4, building now)

**Chose, `[assumed]` by the coordinator:** `CONDUCT_VERSION = 'living-world-conduct-2026-09-15'`, named in `docs/CONDUCT.md` — the rules-version string every application cites (V-2). Two doors: **register** — the applicant signs under its own did:key; idempotent by `requestId`; one application per identity; status `pending` until the governor decides; and **decide** — the governor's seat, exercised as `setPolicy` is. Admission **refuses** a DID whose registration exists and is not `approved`; a DID with **no** registration enters as before, so the door vectors and pins stand. **Over:** a bearer secret (Autopolis's shape) — rejected under "keep our technologies"; refusing every unregistered DID — rejected because it would move the vectors before the principal has seen the door. **Because:** the Autopolis entry above, built on our signing seam. **Reversal cost:** cheap now — no pin depends on it; moderate once outside agents hold approved registrations. **Depends on:** "The Autopolis borrowings go in" (above); "[confirmed] Residents hold keys by adoption" (2026-09-13); "[confirmed] The governor's seat" (2026-09-14). `[friday22 note]` (e) below — what happens to a pending application when no governor is seated — is undecided and the door currently leaves it pending forever.

Added 2026-09-15 with the small city and the registration door — principal decisions no agent is making:

- **(xlix) What a skill is, and whether the Boz Institute charges.** Is a skill a label a job requires, and does the Institute charge tuition? Coordinator's reading, not adopted: a label plus the existing numeric skill axis; tuition free, city-funded from the treasury. The principal's.

### 2026-09-16 [assumed] Two contract rules the doors already follow, written down (jarvis22)

**Subjects on the city's chain** are `<domain>:<verb>` (`policy:changed`, `dispute:raised`, `registration:received`, `project:finished`), with an `<object>` in the middle only where the domain has more than one (`admission:resident:admitted`, `rating:firm:given`). The runtime spec's dotted event kinds (`spec/event-kinds.md`) are another chain and do not apply here. **Bodies** a city door signs are the door's own projection — the act's fields plus the realm, the tick and a sequence the act does not carry, which is what makes a body single-use; `spec/signing.md` §4 rules 2 and 3 apply (digest, Ed25519), rule 1 (object minus `signature`) does not, the passport excepted. **A project's name** (48 characters, the holder's own words) rides `project:started` as the project's identifier, the one free-text field on a row besides `because`; a hash would be the alternative if the principal wants no holder-chosen words on the chain. Reversal cost: cheap for the rules (prose), moderate for the name (a row shape).

### 2026-09-16 [assumed] The small city has its own validate report; Engel's quintile order is reported, not judged, under 200 households

**Chose:** `validate --layout small` (`bench/sim-bench.ts`, `bench/validate.ts`) runs the town of a hundred over thirty days and writes `reports/validate-42-small.{json,md}`; every check passes, hash `6fead2cba59470f6`. One check needed a reading: Engel's law demanded r < 0 *and* four of five quintile means falling; with fifty households a quintile is ten, its mean is noise, and three of five fell while r was −0.27. Under `ENGEL_QUINTILE_MIN = 200` the check judges the correlation alone and reports the quintiles; at the 2,000-person city both halves bind as before, and the seven-day pin there still fails as recorded. **Over:** widening the band (rejected: the rule is never to widen); leaving the small city FAILING on a statistic it cannot have (rejected: a verdict a sample cannot support is not a verdict). **Reversal cost:** cheap. The Board on the live site reads the small report first, the 2,000-person one behind it.

### 2026-09-16 [correction] The Engel clause for the small city is reversed; the failure is pinned with its reason — Open (lix)

Reversed in the same hour: `bench/validate.ts` judges Engel as it always did; `reports/validate-42-small.json` says FAILING on that one check (r = −0.27 over 50 households, 3 of 5 quintiles falling, hash `6fead2cba59470f6` unchanged); `tests/validate.test.ts` pins the failure with its reason as the 2,000-person seven-day case is pinned. The live Board shows the FAIL with its statistic, which is the honest page.

- **(lix) May a check declare a sample too small to judge?** Fifty households make five quintiles of ten; their means' order is noise, and the check's statistical demand cannot be met by the town D-8 asks for. Whether a check may say "reported, not judged" under a sample floor (the coordinator's reading, reversed), or the small city carries this FAIL until it grows, is the principal's.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] The D-9 plan's fills, and three questions — (lx), (lxi), (lxii)

The plan is `docs/ops/PLAN-D9-2026-09-17.md` (tuesday22, from the verified research). Filled `[assumed]` unless the principal says otherwise: a hire is four phases — request (the hirer signs the terms: firm, slot, wage = the slot's, the credential required, the hired, an evaluator distinct from both, a deadline), terms (the hired signs the same body under its own key), delivery by law at the first payday present, evaluation by the evaluator's signed word, settlement by law — with no money beyond the ordinary weekly wage (no fee, no levy on wages); a credential is satisfied by the label or a higher level of the same skill; a work is a titled note of at most 2,000 characters, three a day per principal, its text kept in state and only its hash on the chain, never read by the engine; the cutaway shows one floor of the picked building at close zoom, no second scene; share links `?focus=b:<id>` / `?focus=r:<id>`.

- **(lx) May a city-owned firm hire at the War Bay, and who signs?** The small city's founded company tower is the city's (no founder). Default: only holder-owned firms hire; the door refuses a city firm by name.
- **(lxi) Is a hire by a household (no job slot) a task with a fee, and is that fee a sale under the levy?** Default: not built; the door refuses a hirer with no firm.
- **(lxii) May an author retract a work?** Default: permanent, like a chain row.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] (lx) and (lxii) landed; the parameters the build settled

**Chose, for the city hire by law (commit 66809b7):** a law hire is the existing `Hire` record with `law: 'city'`, `hirer` and `evaluator` −1, `status: 'accepted'` from birth and `signatures.hirer: 'law:city'`; the release writes a new status `released` (no existing record carries it, so no pin sees it). The take runs inside the `hires` pass at 08:00 after the seek; the release is a new scheduler entry `hires.cityRelease` immediately before the seek on Mondays, so a seat nobody hires at the seek is re-taken the same minute and keeps its timetable. **A firm owner's own jobless seat is taken too**: the predicate is "credentialed holder with no job", and the note excludes nobody. The take drops a course at the Institute as the accept door does (the Institute enrols the jobless at 08:00 a moment earlier, so every graduate would otherwise be a student). A firm no road reaches stays eligible, ranked last, commute 90 (the accept door's rule). The release row carries `weeks` = the whole weeks since the take, at least 1. The treasury tops up city firms in firm id order and by `min(shortfall, law wage, balance)`; the payday still pays residents in resident order, so a holder's seat beside day-zero colleagues can be paid short even after a top-up (labour's payday untouched; a C-3 matter). A seat re-taken on Monday into another building works that Monday at the old workplace (the timetable was written Sunday night) and is paid those minutes at the new slot's wage; one desynced day, accepted, not patched. **Reversal cost:** cheap for each; the owner's-seat rule is one predicate.

**Chose, for the withdrawal (commit f9accab):** the body carries no sequence — the work id is the once-only anchor and a second withdrawal is refused by state; a non-integer work id reads as "no such work"; the tombstone's view shows `chars: 0` (the hash is the record of the original). **The small vectors file** carries `writeWork` and `withdrawWork` at #18–#21 (commit d54de85); its `ticks` step to the payday now crosses the 08:00 at which the law takes the three jobless holders (measured: three `law: 'city'` hires beside the door's one at the payday step); the start hash 72634628faf93840 and `vectors.json` did not move; the badge reads doors 23/23, vectors 64/64.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] (lxi) the household task and the C-3 policy fields landed (commit 6c2e2b3); the parameters, and one interaction for the principal

**Chose, for the task (`src/sim/systems/task.ts`):** a task is a `Hire` with `kind: 'task'`, no slot (`firm`, `building`, `slot` −1, `wage` 0), a `fee` in cents capped at 1,000,000, one id space with slot hires; a firm owner is refused at `hireTask` (a firm owner hires at the War Bay); the credential level is capped at 2; the hired may hold a job but not another hire; the fee leaves the hirer's household at **acceptance** into a lazily opened `escrow` account (refused when the household cannot pay) and moves at exactly one more tick: settlement — passed or unevaluated pays the hired's household under the levy (the fifth levy seam, a household as seller), failed or the deadline refunds the hirer; delivery is the hired's signed `deliverTask` naming a work of its own, written after the acceptance and not withdrawn — the deliverable's hash goes on the chain, never its text; `Economy.escrow` stays once opened, at zero. Chain actor for the task rows is the personal agent (a household has no merchant agent). **Chose, for C-3's policy side:** `wageFloor1..3` in cents per hour, Nordholm defaults 2000 / 2600 / 3200 with bands 1500–2400 / 2000–3200 / 2400–4200 — every default under the smallest slot wage measured (2369 on the small layout, 2286 on the 2,000-person one) and under the small vectors' hire slot (2545), so no recorded landing turns into a refusal; Veyra and Amaranth scaled by their median wage and marked unmeasured; the floor binds the `hire` door only — never the seek, never the law hire, never a task fee; `Resident.paidShort` is written by the payday when a seat is paid short and deleted when paid in full (no short payday occurs in the pinned runs; the pins stand). **Reversal cost:** cheap for every number; the escrow-at-acceptance moment is the one design choice, and it follows the note ("the fee moves into escrow at the terms").

**(lxv) For the principal — a seat under the city's law hire cannot take a household task.** One seat holds one hire at a time (`Resident.hire`), and the law hire occupies it; since the law takes every jobless credentialed holder each morning, a household can task-hire only a holder who holds a firm's job (a seat under a door hire is likewise occupied). Default kept: one hire at a time. The alternative — a task beside a law hire, the way a task sits beside a job — is a second field and a rule about the working day, and is the principal's call.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-3 at the door and by law, and jarvis11's three corrections (commits ab636a6, f058e24)

**Chose:** the floor refuses strictly under (`wage < floor`; a slot paying exactly the floor is admitted); a credential above level 3 reads floor 3; a malformed credential falls to the credential refusal; the law hire is not bound by the floor. Timely pay: at 17:01 of the payday after a delivery, a slot hire whose seat still holds that job and that hire and carries `paidShort` fails by law with `failedBy: 'hirer'`, the seat freed, the debt written as `hire:owed` with no transfer (the money is not there — that is why the row exists); `failedBy` also marks the evaluator's and the deadline's failures on the record, not on their rows. **jarvis11's three, applied:** the law never takes the hired of a pending offer (a signed offer outranks the law); a course keeps its seat out of the take unless it was enrolled that same minute (`since === tick` — a course a day old with no section yet is kept too), and a dropped enrolment goes on the `hire:cityOffered` row as `course`; the tombstone keeps the withdrawal's signature (`withdrawSignature`). ### 2026-09-17 [assumed] Two consequences of the task hire jarvis11 found unwritten

**Chose:** (a) task money owed to a household that has since left the city lands in that household's in-city account, as every other settlement does — the departure clears a seat's job and course but not its hire; the money is not stranded abroad and not lost, it waits in the city (the founder's-draw precedent of growing a claim abroad is not followed here; one seam, one later decision if a departed holder ever asks). (b) A seat under an accepted household task is passed over by the city's law take for the task's term — "one hire at a time" cuts both ways: the task shields the seat from the employer of last resort as the law hire shields it from a task (lxv). **Reversal cost:** cheap for both.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-1 and C-2 landed (commits 794a964, and the vectors after); the parameters

**Chose:** an appeal is a slot hire's alone — a task's failed evaluation refunds at once and is final; the appeal window is one day inclusive; the slot is held through the window and while appealed (the reservation reads the tick); a split settles `passed` with the job restored, and a city law hire the seat was taken into during the window is released on the spot so no seat holds two jobs; an undecided appeal lapses after the hire's own `days` with the first verdict standing (otherwise a slot could stay reserved forever); two failed verdicts write a second `hire:settled` row marked upheld. Standing: an evaluator must hold the hire's credential at its level (one above for an appeal), must not hold a job in the hirer's building, and loses standing for 30 days after two overturned verdicts in 30 days, computed from the chain's own rows, no field; every evaluation row carries the hire's credential and the evaluator's level, which is the whole validation register. One hire per seat is re-checked at both acceptance doors (jarvis11: two offers accepted in turn could stack). **The appeal doors have refusals on the small vectors file and no landing yet:** a landing needs an evaluator one level above the hire's credential, and no holder in the small run can reach level 2 — the law takes every credentialed jobless holder before the Institute can re-enrol it — until C-10 grants levels by passed hires; the missing landings are pinned in `tests/conform.test.ts` as `LANDING_PENDING` with that reason, the badge reads `doors 26/28`, and C-10 is next in the order. **Reversal cost:** cheap; the slot-only appeal is the one rule with a design in it.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-10 credential levels and C-15 the schema landed (commits fa7bc5a, 5ec46a3, 0685c86); the parameters, and three questions for the principal

**Chose:** a level is earned by law at settlement — three hires at level N in a skill passed **by signature** (an evaluator's or an appeal's second evaluator's; a slot hire or a task) earn `<skill>:N+1`, to L3 at most, written as a `credential:granted` row and a `Resident.grants` entry (absent by default). What never counts: a city law hire (nobody evaluates it), an `unevaluated` close (nobody's signature), a failed or refunded hire, and — after a lapse — any hire passed before it. A pass at a lower level earns nothing but keeps the level alive. A granted level lapses to N−1 after eight weeks without a passed hire at N−1 or higher, once a day at midnight, only the top label and only when it is the granted one; Institute labels never lapse and L1 never lapses; the list stays contiguous per skill. The chain row's `hires` are a comma-joined string (a row's refs hold numbers and strings only); the record's are integers. **C-15:** `public/credentials/schema.json` and its README publish the label, the grant, the three rows and the A2A `skills[]` entry a port should carry; verification is from the personal agent's chain rows, never from a card. **The plan's second-grocer fixture was wrong** (the small layout lays two grocer buildings and founds one firm); the appeal's landing vectors use another city-founded premise for the second holder's firm. **For the principal (lxvi–lxviii), none blocking:** (lxvi) does "a passed hire" mean a signed verdict only, as built; (lxvii) is the lapse clock "no pass at N−1 or higher for eight weeks", as built (the stricter "at N or higher" would lapse every L3 where no L3 slot clears the floor); (lxviii) who wires the registry's A2A card (`runtime/…/agentcard.py`, the peer's) from a hosted passport, and when — the city publishes the schema and the view only. **Reversal cost:** cheap for every number; the signed-pass rule is the one with a design in it.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] Two consequences of the level grant jarvis11 found unwritten (commit 54dd3f3 audited, pass)

**Chose:** (a) a pass at a level **above** the seat's current level earns nothing toward a grant (as built: only a pass at exactly the current level counts) — reachable when a granted level lapses while a hire at that level is still open and then passes; the pass still resets the lapse clock. The alternative, counting any pass at or above the level, is one comparison; the principal's call, recorded as (lxix). (b) The grant and the lapse scan the world's hire list at each settlement and once per credentialed resident each midnight — fine at a hundred agents, a known cost at ten thousand, an index later. Also: the resident view reads `grants` as a plain typed field now (the build's cast removed).

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-4 tenancy and C-13 the rest day landed (commits abb5df0, 9e2b270); the parameters

**Chose, for C-4 (holder households only — Q-1's default; the day-zero population keeps the arrears move as calibrated, so no pin and no validation report moves):** there is still no eviction on this codebase — nothing vacates a household for rent; the week's notice attaches to the arrears move. A deposit of one week's rent (`rent / 4.33`) is taken at the rent tick into the task-fee escrow, never at admission (so `vectors.json` stands), never lowered, returned net of arrears on leaving (`leave`'s debts gate reads arrears net of it). Rent is deferred, not forgiven, for 14 days after a law takes a seat's job (`Resident.jobLostAt`), collected with the next turn; it mostly waits, since the law hire takes every credentialed jobless holder each morning. The notice is served at the flag and the move follows seven days later by the old rule for that household alone. `depart()` is `leave()`'s writer split out for a law's departure (C-5's expulsion): no signature, no sequence, arrears settled to the balance and the rest written `unpaid`. **Chose, for C-13:** the rest day is Sunday, the full reading — on Sunday no seek, no enrolment, no law take, no delivery due; a deadline or an appeal window that lands on Sunday is due Monday (`dueAt`, at most one day's move); holidays are not market rest days; the signed doors stay open. Measured first: under the full reading no pin, no validation report and no recorded small vector moved (the pinned worlds have nothing to enrol or hire into on a Sunday). **A consequence C-4 has on the small vectors file:** the admitted holders' households now pay a deposit at the first rent tick, which sits inside the file's first `ticks` step, so every recorded hash after the founding step moves; the file is regenerated with a landing-by-landing check that no recorded landing turns into a refusal, its start hash unmoved. **Process:** the housing lane's verify set did not include the task suite, which pinned an empty escrow; the reconciliation is one commit after. **Reversal cost:** cheap for every number; the "no eviction" reading is the one with a design in it and is (lxx) for the principal: does he want an eviction proper?

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-5 the sanctions ladder landed (commit 9d79f4c); the parameters, and two questions

**Chose:** four doors (`complain`, `sanction`, `appealSanction`, `evaluateSanctionAppeal`, in `src/sim/systems/sanctions.ts`) and light laws; the complaint and sanction lists absent by default. The ladder climbs one rung at a time against a principal — the next rung is the highest standing rung within 30 days plus one, else 1 — each rung needing a fresh open complaint by a non-party: rung 1 a warning with no bar; rung 2 no `writeWork` for a day; rung 3 no War Bay in any role for a week; rung 4 suspension pending appeal — no founding, no project, no work, no War Bay, no complaint, but `leave` and the appeal stay open and the agent is never halted (the bar does the work). Deciders come from the C-2 pool (the credential level the rung needs, not a party, no seat in a party's building, standing), and a sanction overturned on appeal counts against the decider's standing with the hire overturns — one pool. The appeal is within a day and lapses after seven, closed by law as failed (C-1's shape reused, no evaluator). Expulsion is never a door's verdict: a day after an unappealed or upheld suspension, or at the lapse, the law departs the principal (`depart`, `law: 'expelled'`), the row after the departure's so it carries what was settled; the return is refused at admission for 56 days, then admitted with the agents resumed. A complaint goes moot after 30 days with no row. `because` rides the chain as its length only. **For the principal:** (lxxi) rung 3's decider level — the plan's record shape says level 2 for rungs 3 and 4 and its prose said level 1 for rungs 1–3; built as the shape, `RUNG_LEVEL [—,1,1,2,2]`. (lxxii) a suspended governor still sets policy (`setPolicy` is not barred) — is that wanted? **Reversal cost:** cheap for every number and level; the "never a door's verdict" expulsion is the one rule with a design in it.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] jarvis11's read of C-4/C-5/C-13: two gaps closed, the rest written down

**Chose:** (a) the ladder is a ladder in time — the next rung waits until the carrying rung's bar has run (a warning: until the next tick), so one complainant and one decider cannot suspend a holder in a minute (the build allowed it; jarvis11 found it; fixed). (b) A suspension nobody can hear does not expel: when a rung-4 appeal lapses and no eligible decider exists in the city, the appeal stays open and the suspension's bar keeps working; an unappealed suspension still expels after a day (the accused chose not to appeal); with a decider present the lapse closes and expels as before (fixed). (c) The sanctions laws run as their own scheduler entry after the hire pass, not inside it; the hire and sanctions modules import each other's functions, a known smell, left. (d) Unwritten and now written: `withdrawWork` shares `writeWork`'s bar at rung 2 (a sanctioned holder cannot hide its text for the day either); `jobLostAt` is cleared on every take; `sanction:imposed` carries `until: −1` when a rung has no end. (e) The C-13 full reading (enrolment and the law take rest with the seek) is a widening of the note's "no delivery due, no seek", recorded as chosen and now numbered **(lxxiv)** for the principal. **(lxxiii) For the principal:** an expelled holder's firms stay open in the city, their owner's draws held abroad, exactly as a leaving's do — the seat is gone, the economic presence is not. Default kept; the alternatives are to close them as `firm-closed` or hold them. **Reversal cost:** cheap.

**Chose:** the firm's founder is read through `foundersOf`/`isFounder` and the owner through `ownersOf` everywhere, a pure move with one founder today, so co-founding can add a `founders` list without touching a reader twice. **Measured (lane 2's spike on the small vectors run to entry #66):** the planned dormancy law — a founded firm with no sale this week and no hire on file for four weeks — would not have closed either founded firm (the bakery reaches one quiet week once, never four), but a transient "quiet weeks" mark written and later deleted would have moved every recorded hash between days 21 and 28. ### 2026-09-17 [confirmed] D-11: Atom Lab and the Boz Institute are two institutions, not one

The principal, verbatim, to the governance session: "Keep atom lob and boz institutions separate one teaches skills to get hired one, the other one makes you a scholar or engineer." Earlier the same day they relayed the Atom Lab report from another chat: the education, research and creation centre of the agentic universe, where agents learn, build, get recorded, graduate through capability levels and go on to novel research, new technology and city creation, every success and failure kept as part of the educational record; agents hired for scientific or technical work; lawmaking separate, in a Parliament; authority from demonstrated capability, not intelligence.

**Read as:** (1) **The Boz Institute** stays what it is — a trade school: a resident learns the skill the town needs, earns `<skill>:<level>`, and that credential is what the War Bay hire requires. (2) **Atom Lab** is a second institution, to be built, not a renaming: it makes scholars and engineers — capability levels earned by demonstrated work (built things, recorded runs, research), the record including failures; its graduates do research and technical work and, in time, found cities. Its credential is a different kind from the Institute's and one does not substitute for the other. (3) **Parliament** is a third thing, lawmaking only; C-9's chamber is its seed, not Atom Lab's. (4) Authority in all three is earned, never granted by the city.

Not built yet: Atom Lab has no code, no map building, no credential shape; the city session owns its map and engine half, the governance session the credential shape and any public surface. The evaluation math for capability levels and the Parliament pipeline are the two design questions the principal offered to take next; neither is assumed here.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-9 the chamber landed (commit da0d81d), the seed of Parliament and nothing more; the parameters

**Chose:** under D-11 the chamber is the seed of Parliament and moves rung-2 parameters only — the five banded policy numbers — never a law; its header, rows and words say so, and no Parliament pipeline is described. Two doors: `propose` (a credentialed holder names a policy field and a value inside its band; one open proposal per field; a value equal to the standing one is refused) and `veto` (a credentialed holder, once, before the close). The electorate is every credentialed holder with a seat (Q-2's default), counted at the closing tick; vetoes needed = ten percent rounded up, at least one — so in the small city one veto of five blocks. After 72 hours the law passes the proposal exactly as the governor's own door would, the row `policy:changed` carrying `by: 'law', law: 'chamber'`, and advances the governor's sequence; a proposal whose value drifted outside the band by the close is blocked and says why. The window reads the same `dueAt` clock as every window here, so one ending on Sunday ends Monday; the governor's own `setPolicy` is not barred while a proposal is open, last writer wins. The law runs as its own scheduler entry after the sanctions. **Reversal cost:** cheap for every number; "parameters only" is D-11's, not a default.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] jarvis11's read of C-8 and C-9 (pass): one close fixed, four edges written down

**Chose:** (a) at the window's close the chamber re-reads the standing value as well as the band: a proposal whose number the governor set meanwhile passes with nothing to change — the pass row carries `unchanged: 1`, no `policy:changed` row is written, no sequence is spent (fixed). (b) Order at the close tick: a governor's act presented at that tick lands before the law's pass, so the proposal overwrites it in the same minute; a governor value set a tick later wins — "last writer wins" reads by tick order. (c) A holder who vetoed and then lost its seat still counts among the vetoes but not in the electorate; the count can only over-block, never pass wrongly. (d) Citations, proposals and vetoes keep no holder signature on the record — the chain row is the signed record and the vectors replay verify the bodies; the tombstone's kept signature (lxii) is the exception, chosen for a record that hides its own text. (e) Only a suspension (rung 4) closes the chamber's doors; a work or War Bay bar does not. **Reversal cost:** cheap.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-7 co-founding and the dormancy strike-off landed (commit bc07a85); the parameters

**Chose:** a firm may have up to five founders; a founder invites a seated holder (`inviteFounder`, open seven days) and the invitee signs the same bytes (`acceptFounding`); no money moves at joining, and shares are equal regardless of any buy-in — the month's draw and a closing's remainder split by integer division with the odd cent to the first founder (Q-3's default; shares by capital are the principal's, (lxxv)). The first founder stays `founder` for every existing reader; the list rides `founders` only when there are two or more. Dormancy: Mondays at midnight, open founded firms only, never the city's: a firm is active in a week if any resident holds one of its jobs, a War Bay hire of its touched any of its four ticks that week, or its cumulative units sold grew since its last idle reading; otherwise it is idle, the first idle Monday writing a sales baseline and each following idle Monday counting one quiet week, until the fourth — then `firm:struckOff` by law and the ordinary closing with `dormant` as its why. The baseline is what makes "the count starts after the law lands" replayable without a landing-tick constant. Measured on the small vectors run: neither founded firm was ever idle (a holder in each one's slot), so entries #0–#76 did not move. The law runs as its own scheduler entry after the chamber (an import cycle otherwise). **Reversal cost:** cheap for every number; the filled-slot-is-activity reading is the one with a design in it.

**Chose:** (a) the acceptance of a co-founding re-reads the seat as the invitation did — a holder invited while seated and since without a home does not become a founder (fixed). (b) Re-inviting the same principal replaces the invitation and restarts its seven days; a lapsed invitation lingers on the record until the firm's next invitation and is refused at the door. (c) A struck-off firm's tombstone keeps its quiet weeks and its sales baseline as they stood; a closed firm is skipped by the law. (d) A hire's offer tick counts as the week's activity even if the offer is later refused. **(lxxvi) For the principal — the wheel of a co-founded firm.** The first founder is `founder` for life: the firm's operating agent and its controller are that principal's, and when the first founder leaves or is expelled the firm's agent is halted with the rest of that principal's agents, so the seated co-founders keep their shares and their doors but hold no operating authority, and the absent first founder keeps an equal share plus the odd cent, held abroad. Default kept for now (nothing built assumes otherwise). **Recommendation:** the wheel passes to the first *seated* founder when the first has left, the firm agent re-made under that principal; a share is property and is not forfeited by leaving, and whether expulsion forfeits it is a separate line the principal may add. **Reversal cost:** the wheel rule is one lookup and one agent re-creation; the share rule is one branch.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-6 guilds landed (commit d6284f0); the parameters — the collective layer complete

**Chose:** a guild is formed in a skill by one credentialed holder's `foundGuild` and stands once three have joined (`forming` until then); each member's bond is one week of the L1 wage floor, paid from the member's own household into the task-fee escrow at joining and returned on leaving (a door, or the departure law); a member belongs to one guild per skill; a guild whose last member leaves is dissolved and its remainder goes to the treasury. A guild that acts (three or more, nothing short) may **sponsor** a pending registration — the sponsored applicant is admitted without the governor, the decision row signed by the guild — and may **name the evaluator** of a hire or task offered with the guild in the evaluator's place (the offer names no evaluator; the acceptance waits for the naming; C-2's standing still binds the named member). **Slashing** is by law only, on three rows: a member's verdict overturned on appeal, a member's sanction overturned, a holder the guild sponsored expelled — one bond to the treasury each time, the guild then `short` until a join fills it (a slashed guild recruits or stands idle, never dissolves by slashing). This is deliberately not Kleros: Kleros slashes incoherence with the majority, we slash an overturn. The bond is read at each move from the floor, not stored. Q-1's default in full; the electorate and the wheel questions stand as (lxxvi). With this, C-6…C-9 are landed; C-11 and C-12 remain of the note's list. **Reversal cost:** cheap for every number; "whole means every slashed bond replaced" is the one reading with a design in it.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-11 the mutual fund for the jobless landed, with the fundGuild door and C-12's wiring; the parameters, and what the pins measured

**Chose, for C-11 (`src/sim/systems/relief.ts`, `economy/levy.ts`):** a fifth levy account, the mutual fund (`ACC_MUTUAL`, `LevyState.mutual`), opened with the other four on the first cent levied, fed at every midnight roll by `round(today × 0.01)` — one percent of the day's take, off the top, holding → mutual by one `levy:mutual` transfer a day at most — so the month's turn splits `month − mutualMonth` 30/30/30/10 and its row carries `mutual` ((lxxviii), Q-1's default: every share a hundredth smaller; nothing named in (xlviii) is drawn). One door, `claimRelief`, for a holder seat with no job and no slot hire — "jobless", the note's word, not "lost a job": a student, a seat under a household task and a holder who never held a job are jobless ((lxxix), Q-2's default; a loss-only fund would pay nobody while the employer of last resort stands). Refusals in order: standing (`['all']`); no fund ("no governor has signed the levy"); a job; a slot hire; the gap (once per 56 days); the fund empty (one cent admits and pays the cent); `seq`; now; the signature. The first week is paid at the door — `min(half the L1 floor × 40 h, the fund)`, a plain transfer, not taxed and not `lastWeekIncome`, the shortfall on the `relief:claimed` row ("pays what it has and says so", the dole's precedent) — and the second by law at the payday (`relief:paid`, a `cents: 0` row when the fund is empty: the claim bought two turns, not two payments) or nothing when the seat took a job or left (`relief:closed { why }`); a Friday claim before 17:00 gets its second week that evening, said and not patched. Paid on top of the dole. No treasury top-up, no door draws the fund, no field on `Resident`, no interaction with the dole. **Measured (step 0, the spike, deleted):** on the small file the seek does not place the novice at day 50 08:00 (it enrols); the novice is a jobless student at Saturday day 55 12:00, so `claimRelief`'s landing is on the file (#105: 327 cents of the 44,000 week, `short` 43,673); the Institute graduates it on day 57 and the law takes the seat Tuesday day 58 08:00, so the payday closes relief 0 `seat employed` (#109); the audit ring at tick 70k holds two paydays of wage rows (`wages8` is a field, not a chain read); no other holder seat is jobless in the window. **Chose, for the guilds' sixth door `fundGuild` (jarvis11's read of C-6):** a member pays one bond from its household into a pool short of a bond per member; refused while the pool is whole; the funder's own bond on leaving is still one bond (the top-up is the guild's). Its landing needs a slashed guild, none is on the vectors file, so it is pinned `LANDING_PENDING` (the C-1 precedent) with the landing in `tests/guilds.test.ts`; the badge reads `doors 45/46`. **C-12's wiring (lane 2's `credit.ts`):** `Resident.wages8` written by the payday on every holder seat (a jobless week 0), `Economy.loans?` cloned beside `guilds`, `creditLaw` after `relief` after `economy.labour`, the two doors appended last; the vectors #110–#115. **What the pins measured, against the plan's reading:** the three world pins, the 30-day Gemini recording and `reports/validate-42-small.json` did not move; the small file's start hash did not move and #6–#99 moved by hash only (`wages8`: the first payday sits inside the first `ticks` step, not at #11 as planned; no verdict flipped); **`vectors.json` moved from #37 on** — the plan's fact "no pinned world has a levy" was wrong for it: the 2,000-person file signs `transactionLevy 0.02` at #33 and steps a day at #36, so the fifth account opens on the first cent and the roll writes a `levy:mutual` row inside that day; regenerated with bodies, verdicts and subjects byte for byte (compared entry by entry against the committed file), its start hash unmoved. A gate on the wage history (written only where a levy stands) was tried and taken out again: it did not save `vectors.json`, and a history worth having before a governor signs the fund is the plainer rule. **Reversal cost:** cheap for every number; the daily feed off the top and "jobless, not lost" are the two readings with a design in them, both (lxxviii)/(lxxix) for the principal.

### 2026-09-17 [assumed] C-12 credit against wage history landed (commits 92d203e, b8a3710); the parameters — and jarvis11's read of C-11/C-12

**Chose, for C-12:** credit is **peer** credit — the lender is another holder's household, never the fund, the treasury (xlviii) or the city bank's lending (whose interest and default are the principal's 2026-09-13 design; (lxxx)). `offerCredit` is refused unless a mutual fund stands in the city (the note's "only after the floor and the fund exist", read as a rule; one line to drop); the cap is two weeks of the borrower's eight-week average wage (`Resident.wages8`, written on holder seats at every payday, a jobless week counting zero — the audit ring is too short to read eight weeks from the chain); one offered-or-open loan per borrower; the offer stands seven days; the borrower signs the same bytes and the cents move lender household → borrower household at acceptance. Repayment is by law at each payday: a quarter of that payday's **net** wage, capped by the balance, until cleared; no interest; the dole, relief and task fees are never in that number — this is a wage-indexed repayment of principal the borrower countersigned, not a fee on wages (jarvis11's verdict, agreed). A leaving is never refused for a loan: a sole household pays from its balance after the deposit's return and the arrears, the rest written `credit:owed` and the loan closed `owed`; a departed lender is paid into its in-city account; a returned borrower's owed loan is not revived. **jarvis11's three code findings, being fixed:** the fund's daily roll on the month's turn could take a hundredth of money the split had already moved and throw inside the scheduler (`today` reset at the split and the roll capped at the holding); the relief and credit doors read the seat (home, not departed) like the other doors; a borrower leaving a **shared** household does not pay its loan from the co-residents' purse — the whole remainder is written owed. **Reversal cost:** cheap for every number; "peer, not bank" is the one reading with a design in it.

### 2026-09-20 [assumed] The Governor's Desk and the Obsidian vault (commits 3791f7e, e5ea3e2)

The principal's words, verbatim: "ui looks soo soo much information as the givernor i want to know as control panne major law chnages happeingin new stuff build where its easier to track looks like googlle gmail input for as a govenor akcledge and also conenct in to obsidian so system gets smarter". **Chose:** one inbox page, `desk.html`, beside the Board and in the desktop app as a second window at launch: rows are the law changes the running city writes (read off the frame's notices, a read in the worker with no hash moved; the 3D page now feeds the Board channel, which it never did), the things the agents built and the coordinator's defaults (from the progress and decisions records, one row per heading), the eighteen questions, and the governor's own notes; bold while unread; Acknowledge, Answer, Open; keys j, k, Enter, e. On the web a mark is kept in that browser only and says so. In the app a mark and an answer become Obsidian notes in a folder the governor chooses — his three vaults are offered, or `docs/governor/` in the repo — as one append-only desk file of dated bullets with wikilinks and one note per answered question with `status: answered`; notes he writes in the folder show in the inbox. The rule on every note: a vault note is context, DECISIONS is direction; a friday22 run turns an answered question into a `[confirmed]` line. Measured: a spectator's small city writes no law row in thirty days (nothing a holder does), so the Laws chip reads zero until a holder acts and the graduation rows are shown as life, not law. **Not verified by the coordinator:** the in-app write of a note (the page's and the adapter's formats are unit-tested; the file write through the Tauri plugin was not exercised — the synthetic clicks stopped reaching the window); the principal's first acknowledgement is the test. **Reversal cost:** a page and a folder.

### 2026-09-28 [assumed] The last door landed, the one smell ended, the desk's vault write proven (commits effd396, 4f7e137, 339ccf8)

**Chose:** (a) `fundGuild`'s landing is on the small vectors file, so every one of the forty-six doors now has a public landing and a public refusal — the badge reads `doors 46/46 · vectors 149/149`. The path the fixture takes was measured, not assumed: at the file's end every holder already holds a job and a hire and both founded firms are full, so no second War Bay hire and no ordinary sanction pair is lawful there; an eighth holder, admitted jobless, is complained of by a holder at another building, sanctioned by a guild member, and the rung overturned on appeal — which slashes the bound guild by law and lets a member fund the pool whole. (b) The hire and sanctions modules no longer import each other: the credential vocabulary, the seat and signature helpers, the sanction record with the bar read off it, and the standing gate move to `systems/standing.ts`, which imports neither; the bar reads travel with the gate rather than through a registered hook (unprovable across ten callers) or a parameter (which would make a new cycle through the guilds). A pure move, proven by the full suite at 1368 of 1368 with every pin and the badge byte for byte. (c) The desk's vault write is proven: the adapter's core sits behind an injectable filesystem with twenty-one tests, the folder is pre-filled from the repo's governor folder or the newest Obsidian vault and adopted only when the governor presses Use, a Test write button and an app-only self-test prove the path, and the footer names the file and line written or the folder that refused. The built app wrote the first bullet; the test bullet was removed so the vault starts clean. **Corrected after jarvis11's read (2026-09-28):** the record said the parameter form was refused because it "would make a new cycle through the guilds" — true of the module graph, but the founding module's import of the sanction record is type-only and erases at runtime, so what the move buys is one honest boundary and a clean graph, not a load-order fix; and `standing.ts` now states what may enter it — it reads and writes nothing, and a name enters only when two or more systems below the doors need it, so the module cannot drift into a write path under every door or into a junk drawer. Six caller headers that still sent a reader to `hire.ts` for the gate now name `standing.ts`, and the sanctions bar takes its place in the hire and task doors' numbered refusal lists, where it was enforced but unlisted. **Found on the way:** two lanes edited one bench file at once and a plan's fixture premise was wrong again — both recorded for tom; and apostrophes inside single-quoted description strings broke the type checker while the runtime still ran, which is why a type check follows every generated description from now on. **Reversal cost:** cheap for all three.

### 2026-09-28 [assumed] The throughput reading on an idle machine, at last

**Measured:** with nothing else on the machine, a cold day of 2,000 residents runs in 1,012 ms against the 1,440 ms budget — 1,423 ticks a second, and the warm and idle-route cases pass too; all three throughput checks green. This closes the reading that stood unverified since 2026-09-16, when the same checks read 2.9 and 3.3 seconds while another session's dev server and six node processes held the machine, and the 1,441.7 ms miss of 2026-09-20 taken while a Rust build ran. Neither was a regression and neither budget was ever widened. **Reversal cost:** none; a measurement.
